# ADR-025 — Round-2 DEFER close-out program: owner decisions + per-workstream ledger

**Status:** Accepted (2026-09-07, "Линза доработок" round-2 DEFER close-out program, task W0-0, branch `fix/lens-r2-bh05-approval-diff`).

## Context

The Lens Round-2 audit (ADR-024) triaged 77 remaining findings into 19 FIX (built, `4d0da09..e6c5027` plus the wave that followed) / 56 DEFER / 2 WON'T-FIX, with the 56 DEFER explicitly deferred "intact to a post-release refactor wave" under **ADR-R2-17**, and the FSU (Func-impl/Syntax/UI-UX) god-unit decomposition work split across two separate tree-split branches under **ADR-FSU-13**.

On 2026-09-07 the owner reversed that sequencing (verbatim): **"все Defer надо закрыть в этой ветке"** / **"МЫ НЕ РЕЛИЗИМ"** — no partial release; every one of the 56 DEFER rows closes on the SAME mega-branch as the already-fixed 19 FIX + the 🔴 BH-05 wave, before any tag is cut. This ADR is the durable record of that reversal and of the batched owner-decision table (`.superpowers/sdd/r2-defer-owner-decisions.md`) that unblocks the program described in `.superpowers/plans/2026-09-07-lens-r2-defer-closeout-program.md`.

**Superseded:** **ADR-R2-17** ("defer all design debt intact to a post-release refactor wave") and **ADR-FSU-13** ("two tree-split FSU branches") — everything now lands on **ONE branch**, `fix/lens-r2-bh05-approval-diff`, ending in one whole-mega-branch review and one release tag. Neither superseded decision is wrong in isolation (ADR-R2-17's triage judgment and ADR-FSU-13's per-god-unit design both stand); only the release-sequencing/branch-topology choice each made is overridden.

## Per-workstream appendix (amended by each WS's doc commit)

Each section below is a placeholder amended in place by the workstream doc commit that closes it; this task (W0-0) creates only the headers.

## ADR-025-A — CA-24 outcome

**Outcome (task R1-8b, WS-R1):** `confinedOpen.ts`'s and `rootResolution.ts`'s duplicate `isWithin` predicates are gone. `confinedOpen.ts` (`readContained`'s post-O_PATH-pin containment re-check, ~:153) now imports and calls the canonical `pathConfine.isWithin` (exported by R1-8a) instead of its own local `!rel.startsWith('..')` copy; `rootResolution.ts`'s `isPathWithin` is now `import { isWithin } from '../backend/acp/pathConfine'; export { isWithin as isPathWithin };` — a re-export under its historical name, not a duplicate body — and its former "not a security boundary, kept local" doc is retired in favour of "this IS the canonical function." `resolveRootCoordinator`'s sole caller is unchanged (same signature, same call site). `pathConfine.ts` is **0-diff in this commit** — R1-8a already exported `isWithin`; this task only re-points the two extra copies at it.

The local copy was WEAKER than the canonical one: `!rel.startsWith('..')` denies any in-workspace name merely BEGINNING with `..` (a directory literally named `..cache`, `..x`, etc.), not just a genuine `..`/`../`-prefixed escape. On the Fedora `ConfinedReader` read path this over-denied a legitimate in-workspace read (`<root>/..cache/x`) with a misleading `escape`/symlink error. The canonical `pathConfine.isWithin` (`rel === '' || (rel !== '..' && !rel.startsWith('..' + sep) && !path.isAbsolute(rel))`) correctly treats `..cache/x` and `..x` as contained while still denying `..`, `../x`, and any absolute/cross-root escape.

**Proof (`confinedOpen.test.ts`):** a parity table drives `readContained` (fake port, `readlink` reporting the row path verbatim, identity `realpath`) over `['', '..', '../x', '..x', '..cache/x', 'a/..b', '/abs', 'a/../..']` and asserts its containment decision equals `pathConfine.isWithin`'s on every row, plus one dedicated assertion that `<root>/..cache/x` is CONTAINED. Before the fix (RED): the `..x` and `..cache/x` rows failed — over-denied with `denial.kind: 'escape'` where the canonical predicate says contained. After the fix (GREEN): all rows agree; `..cache/x` is contained; the pre-existing goldens (`confinedOpen.test.ts`, `rootResolution.test.ts`, `pathConfine.test.ts`) are unchanged and pass. Mutation-proven: reinstating the old `!rel.startsWith('..')` body reproduces exactly the same two RED rows and no others; reverting restores GREEN.

**The one golden-visible copy change (`AcpBackend.ts`):** `confinedDenialMessage`'s `'escape'` case used to say the path "resolves through a symlink to a location outside the workspace" — accurate for a genuine post-pin symlink swap, but misleading for the over-deny case this task fixes (nothing symlink-related was happening; the LOCAL predicate was simply wrong) and inaccurate for any other future post-canonicalization mismatch reaching that branch. The copy now reads "resolves outside the workspace after canonicalization" — naming the real, general cause instead of assuming a symlink — with the same actionable "add that location as a workspace folder" hint preserved. This branch had no existing pinning test (searched `AcpBackend.test.ts` for `confinedDenialMessage`/`kind: 'escape'` — none found before this task), so a new test was added (`AcpBackend.handleReadTextFile — F1: bounded confined read`, `'the confined-read escape denial names the real (canonicalization) cause, not "a symlink"'`) that RED'd on the old copy and GREENs on the new one. The OTHER, unrelated copy of the same original sentence — `handleReadTextFile`'s own inline `symlinkEscape` branch (fires earlier, on a real `resolveWithinWorkspaceReal` vs. `resolveWithinWorkspace` divergence, i.e. an actual symlink escaping the workspace) — is untouched and stays accurate; its existing S-M5 golden test (`'rejects a read through an in-workspace symlink that escapes...'`) is unchanged and still green.

**[SEC] direction (verified):** switching `readContained`'s containment re-check to the canonical `isWithin` makes it strictly MORE permissive by exactly the inside-root `..`-prefixed names, and nothing else. Safe because (a) the canonical form is the SAME check `resolveWithinWorkspaceReal` already applied one step earlier to admit the path in the first place — nothing new escapes the workspace root; and (b) `readContained` re-checks the kernel dentry path via `/proc/self/fd` AFTER the O_PATH pin, so a TOCTOU symlink swap is still caught independently. `pathConfine.test.ts`'s existing symlink-escape goldens (`symlink-leaf-escapes-root`, `symlink-dir-escape`) are unchanged and still deny.

## ADR-025-B — CA-26 / ADR-R2-16 completion + the two deferred Minors M-3/M-12

**Outcome (task R1-6, WS-R1):** `skills.toggle`/`toolsets.toggle`, `reload.mcp`, and `model.save_key` — the three `ControlDispatcher.ts` branches that re-fetch a panel after a mutation already persisted server-side — now route through one shared, failure-isolated `private async rePushPanel(panel: DataPanel, method: string): Promise<void>` helper (`ControlDispatcher.ts`). Before this task, only C1/BH-01's toggle branches caught the re-fetch's rejection; `reload.mcp`'s and `model.save_key`'s re-fetches were bare unguarded `await`s, so a `PanelUnavailableError` there turned an already-successful, already-persisted RPC into a rejected one — the exact hazard ADR-R2-16 named. `rePushPanel` catches any `fetchPanelData` rejection, logs one line (`[ControlDispatcher] <method>: panel re-push failed (mutation persisted) — <errorMessage>`), and always resolves — the caller's raw dispatch/toggle result is returned unchanged regardless of the re-push outcome. The helper takes only `panel` and `method`; `params` is not in its scope, so `model.save_key`'s API key can never reach this log line by construction.

Proven by a 3-row table (`ControlDispatcher.test.ts`, `describe('ControlDispatcher — WS-R1 R1-6 (L2-CA-26)…')`) driving all three methods through a rejecting fake panel source: each RPC still resolves its raw result and logs exactly one line naming the method and containing `re-push`. Before the fix, the `reload.mcp` and `model.save_key` rows failed (RED); the `toolsets.toggle` row already passed, pinning that the new helper preserves C1's existing behaviour. Mutation-proven: reverting `reload.mcp` to a bare unguarded `await this.panels.fetchPanelData('mcp')` fails exactly that row and no other. No existing test (searched `ControlDispatcher.test.ts`, `ControlDispatcher.golden.routing.test.ts`, `ControlDispatcher.golden.admin.test.ts`, `AcpBackend.test.ts`) pinned the OLD reject-on-refetch-failure behaviour for `reload.mcp`/`model.save_key`'s OWN re-fetch — the two rejection-shaped tests that exist there (`AcpBackend.test.ts`'s "a failed model.save_key (4006 managed-install) propagates the rejection" and "F2-08: a reload.mcp failure AFTER the config mutate…") both pin a `port.dispatch` REJECTION (the mutation itself failing), a different, unaffected code path — so nothing needed re-pinning.

**Deferred (critic M-3, OUT of scope for this task):** pushing a `panel.data` refresh-error frame so FI-16's banner can surface a failed re-push to the user changes the panel-push wire contract (a new frame shape / new field the webview would need to render) — that is a feature addition, not a bug fix, and stays log-only for now. M-12 tracked no additional action beyond this completion.

**Outcome (task R2-2, WS-R2, L2-CA-19):** `SetupController`'s private `class Emitter<T>`'s `fire(value: T): void` used to deliver events with `for (const listener of [...this.listeners]) listener(value);` — the `[...this.listeners]` snapshot only guarded against mutation-during-iteration; a listener that THREW aborted the `for` loop outright and starved every later-registered listener of that event, for both of the emitter's instances (`progressEmitter`, `statusChangedEmitter`). `fire` now delivers to EVERY listener under its own `try/catch`, collects any throws into `errors: unknown[]`, and — only if `errors.length > 0` — re-throws them together as `new AggregateError(errors, 'SetupController listener(s) threw')`. This is isolation without a silent swallow: every listener still runs, and a throw still surfaces to the caller (as an `AggregateError`, not the raw per-listener error and not nothing).

**Write-time lib check (stated per the brief):** `AggregateError` resolves cleanly under the project's `tsconfig.json` (`"lib": ["ES2022"]`, `"target": "ES2022"`) — confirmed against `node_modules/typescript`'s own lib graph: `lib.es2022.d.ts` carries `/// <reference lib="es2021" />`, and `lib.es2021.promise.d.ts` declares `interface AggregateError extends Error { errors: any[] }` plus the `AggregateErrorConstructor`/`declare var AggregateError` pair; `lib.es2022.error.d.ts` refines the constructor overloads with an additional `options?: ErrorOptions` parameter. No `lib` entry was added to `tsconfig.json` — none was needed. `npm run gate` (tsc, both the host and webview `tsconfig`) reports 0 type errors with the new `AggregateError` usage in place.

**Known-and-accepted boundary (critic M-12 — recorded, NOT fixed):** `progressEmitter.fire(toSend)` inside `pushProgress`'s coalescing `setTimeout` callback (the throttle-tail branch, `SetupController.ts` — the delayed re-fire path guarded by `state.timer`) now inherits the SAME `Emitter.fire` change as every other call site, with no call-site edit needed (the whole diff lives inside `Emitter.fire` itself). If a progress listener throws while that callback runs, the resulting `AggregateError` is an uncaught timer exception — exactly as today's single-listener throw already was before this task; this change does not make that path worse, and it makes it strictly better first (every listener is still served before the throw surfaces). Turning that specific call site from throw-in-timer to log-and-continue would require a logger seam `SetupController`/`SetupControllerDeps` does not currently have (a shape change to the 19-member `SetupControllerDeps` plus every test factory that constructs one) — out of scope for this task. The call site is untouched; this paragraph is its record.

**Proof (`SetupController.test.ts`, describe `'WS-R2 R2-2 (L2-CA-19): a throwing onStatusChanged listener does not starve a later listener'`):** two listeners are registered via the public `onStatusChanged`; listener 1 throws `new Error('boom')`, listener 2 records that it ran. `controller.handle('setup.recheck', {})` is the trigger — `handleRecheck`'s single completion `bumpStatus()` (ADR-025-D) has no `try/catch` around it, so a throw from `Emitter.fire` propagates straight out of `controller.handle()` as a promise rejection, caught in the test. Before the fix (RED): listener 2 never ran (`later` stayed `[]`) and the caught value was the raw `boom` `Error`, not an `AggregateError`. After the fix (GREEN): listener 2 ran (`later` is `[true]`) AND the caught value `toBeInstanceOf(AggregateError)` with `errors.length === 1` and `errors[0]` being the `boom` `Error`. Every pre-existing emitter-observing test — both `onProgress` suites and the full T7 `onStatusChanged` suite in `SetupController.test.ts`, plus the `onProgress`/`onStatusChanged` registrations in `SetupController.statusGolden.test.ts`, `SetupController.provisionModel.test.ts`, `SetupController.pullGate.test.ts`, and `SetupController.pullLatchUnify.test.ts` — was grepped for a registered listener body that itself throws; none does (every `throw` found near those call sites lives inside a fake dependency function, not inside a listener passed to `onProgress`/`onStatusChanged`), so none pins the old "a throw aborts delivery" behaviour and all are unchanged (0 edits) and green.

**Mutation-proven:** removing the `try/catch` (reverting `fire` to the bare `for (const listener of [...this.listeners]) listener(value);` one-liner, doc comment left in place) reproduces the exact pre-fix RED — listener 2 not invoked, `later` stays `[]` — and no other test in the file fails; restoring the `try/catch` + `AggregateError` body returns the suite to green. `npm run gate`: 7496 passed | 15 skipped (was 7495 before this task), tsc 0 across both `tsconfig.json`s.

## ADR-025-C — ADR-R2-06 re-cut: one first-byte deadline = undici default, 120 s inter-chunk idle (TBD)

## ADR-025-D — CA-16 outcome (R2-1, WS-R2)

**Outcome (task R2-1, WS-R2):** `handleRecheck`'s agent-probe section (the `scope === 'all' || scope === 'agent'` branch) now runs its `locatePipx` call under a `'recheck'`-keyed latch, mirroring `handleInstall`'s exact `arm`/`try`/`finally { release }` pattern. Before this task, that branch fired `await this.deps.locatePipx()` with no signal and no latch entry — `dispose()`'s `this.latches.abortAll()` (`:732`) had nothing to reach, so the probe outlived teardown and a late resolve could still write `this.lastAgentIssue`. `latchRegistry.ts` is **0-diff** — `arm(key): AbortController | undefined` already returned the controller (critic I-18); this task only calls it under a new key.

A recheck that arrives while an agent probe is already in flight **coalesces** onto it instead of refusing or re-arming: `if (!this.latches.has('recheck'))` guards the arm/probe/release block, so a concurrent call skips straight through to the method's existing single completion `bumpStatus()` + `{ ok: true }`. This is deliberate, not an oversight — a second `arm('recheck')` would overwrite the `Map` entry under the same key, orphaning the first `AbortController` so `dispose()` could never reach it again (the exact hazard this task closes, reintroduced). Recheck is a read-only status refresh, so refusing the second caller with `{ ok: false }` would surface a spurious error for a panel that just asked to repaint; coalescing is honest because the in-flight probe already covers the agent state the second call asked for. The latch is scoped to the agent-probe section only (not the whole method) — a concurrent `scope: 'all'` recheck still runs its `os`/`llamacpp` rekick work above the guarded block untouched. On dispose (`arm` returns `undefined`), `handleRecheck` returns `{ ok: false, reason: SETUP_DISPOSED_REFUSAL }` immediately, without probing.

**Proof (`SetupController.test.ts`, describe `"R2-1 (L2-CA-16): setup.recheck {scope:'agent'} probes under a 'recheck'-keyed latch"`):** (1) **cancel-on-dispose** — a scripted `locatePipx` captures the signal it receives; before the fix it is `undefined` (RED: `expected undefined to be an instance of AbortSignal`); after the fix, `dispose()` flips the captured signal's `.aborted` to `true`, and the probe's late resolve (after dispose) fires zero `onStatusChanged` events. (2) **single-flight** — two overlapping `{scope:'agent'}` rechecks against a `locatePipx` that never resolves on its own: before the fix, the second call re-arms and calls `locatePipx` again, so the second call's `await` depends on a promise the test never resolves and the test times out (5000ms) reproducing the orphaned-controller deadlock directly; after the fix, `locatePipx` is called exactly once and the second call resolves `{ ok: true }` immediately without waiting on the in-flight probe. All pre-existing recheck goldens (`'setup.recheck re-probes pipx'`, the T6 scoped-recheck suite, `TC-6` dispose suite, `F2-16` disposed-refusal suite) are unchanged (0 edits) and green — none of them exercises overlapping rechecks, so none pinned a whole-method refusal that this design would have had to reconcile against.

**Mutation-proven:** (i) passing `undefined` instead of `abort.signal` into `locatePipx` reproduces the cancel-on-dispose RED (`capturedSignal` stays `undefined`); reverting restores GREEN. (ii) dropping the `if (!this.latches.has('recheck'))` guard (replaced with `if (true)`) reproduces the single-flight timeout — the second call re-arms and re-probes, orphaning the first controller; reverting restores GREEN. Both mutations were applied, run, and reverted; `git diff` against a pre-mutation backup showed zero drift after restore.

**Write-time lock check (critic — stated per the brief):** grepped `SetupController.test.ts`, `SetupController.pullGate.test.ts`, and `SetupController.pullLatchUnify.test.ts` for `'recheck'` (quoted, all quote styles) and for any latch-key/progress-id enumeration (`pullGate`, `pullLatchUnify`, install:${...}, pull:${...}). No hit for the literal string `'recheck'` anywhere in the pre-task tree, and neither `pullGate.test.ts` nor `pullLatchUnify.test.ts` enumerates the full latch-key set (both are scoped to `install:`/`pull:` prefixes only) — no drift-lock could fail on the new `'recheck'` key, so none needed reconciling.

**R2-1-fix (review Minor, coalesce-window closure):** R2-1's review left ONE Minor: `this.hermesDiscoveryMemo.invalidate()` sat *inside* the `if (!this.latches.has('recheck'))` guard, so a **coalescing** recheck (the second, overlapping call) skipped it — only the in-flight call's own `invalidate()` ever ran. Grounding confirmed `SettledProbeMemo.invalidate()` (`settledProbeMemo.ts`) is a cheap, idempotent epoch bump that clears `settled`/`inFlight` and does not touch `locatePipx`, the `'recheck'` latch, or (for this memo, constructed with `cancellable: false`) any in-flight probe's `AbortController` — safe to call unconditionally, including after `dispose()`'s `supersede()`. The fix hoists the single `this.hermesDiscoveryMemo.invalidate()` call to run ONCE per `handleRecheck` invocation, **above** the `if (!this.latches.has('recheck'))` guard (still inside the `scope === 'all' || scope === 'agent'` block), and removes the now-duplicate call from inside the guard/`try`. The `locatePipx` arm/probe/release — the CA-16 single-flight core — is untouched: still exactly one `locatePipx` call for two overlapping rechecks.

This closes a real (if narrow) race: a `status()` poll landing between an in-flight recheck's `invalidate()` and its `locatePipx` settle can re-kick `hermesDiscoveryMemo` and re-settle it with a now-stale value; before this fix, a coalescing second recheck left that stale value untouched (its own `invalidate()` lived inside the never-entered guard), so the panel could still read "hermes not found" for one extra Re-check click after the user actually installed it. After the fix, the coalescing call's hoisted `invalidate()` clears the stale re-kicked value too, so the very next `status()` re-probes fresh.

**Proof (`SetupController.test.ts`, same `describe` block, new sibling test `"R2-1-fix review Minor: the COALESCING recheck also invalidates the Hermes discovery memo..."`):** seeds a settled ("not found") discovery memo via a natural `status()` kick, starts recheck #1 (arms the latch, invalidates, blocks on `locatePipx`), lets an intervening `status()` re-kick and re-settle the memo to a stale value (`discoverCalls` 1→2), then issues the coalescing recheck #2 and asserts `locatePipx` is still called exactly once (single-flight preserved) before resolving it and draining recheck #1. A final `status()` call asserts `discoverCalls` reaches 3 — proving the coalescing recheck re-invalidated the stale memo. Confirmed RED against pre-fix code (`expected 2 to be 3`); GREEN after the hoist. All pre-existing recheck/dispose/discovery-memo suites are unchanged (0 edits) and green (`npx vitest run src/host/setup/SetupController.test.ts`: 310/310 passed). `npm run gate`: 7495 passed | 15 skipped, tsc 0. `latchRegistry.ts` remains 0-diff.

## ADR-025-E — CA-18 outcome (R2-3, WS-R2)

**Outcome (task R2-3, WS-R2, L2-CA-18, OD-C — the accepted PARTIAL, non-frozen half):** when a GGUF download succeeds and renames into place but the sidecar write fails, the model file was PRESENT on disk yet `scanPresence` reported it ABSENT (presence is sidecar-only: `modelStore.ts`'s `scanOnePresence` reads `<dest>.talaria.json`; `null` → `present:false`). The FULL fix would wrap the frozen `ggufIngest.ts:441` `await io.writeSidecar(...)` call in a try/catch with a new `GgufStoreIo.removeLanded` cleanup seam — an edit to the frozen ingest file and its interface. The owner LOCKED **OD-C**, the zero-frozen-touch default: fix at the injected `writeSidecar` binding instead.

NEW `src/host/setup/sidecarWriter.ts` exports `GgufSidecarWriteError extends Error` (message honest about the COST, not the path — I-16: "The model file was downloaded and verified, but not recorded — Setup will show it as absent; downloading again replaces the file and retries the record.") and `makeSidecarWriter(writeFile)`, which returns a `(sidecarPath, content) => Promise<void>` wrapper: it calls `writeFile` once; on failure, an ELOOP refusal (`.code === 'ELOOP'`, the symlink-refusal signal `writeFileNoFollow` throws on its whole purpose — `safeWrite.ts:141`, FROZEN) is rethrown immediately, UNRETRIED and UNRELABELLED — the H3b invariant; any other (transient) failure gets exactly ONE retry; if the retry also throws an ELOOP refusal it is likewise rethrown as-is; if the retry throws anything else, the writer rejects with `new GgufSidecarWriteError({ cause: <the retry's error> })`. The binding at `setupHost.vscode.ts:389-390` — previously a bare `(sidecarPath, content) => writeFileNoFollow(sidecarPath, Buffer.from(content, 'utf8'))` — is now `writeSidecar: makeSidecarWriter(writeFileNoFollow)`; the `Buffer.from` conversion moved INTO `makeSidecarWriter` (it now owns the string→bytes step once, for both the first attempt and the retry). `ggufIngest.ts` (the frozen call site at `:441` and the `SetupIngestIO.writeSidecar` interface member at `:151`) and `safeWrite.ts` are **0-diff**.

The honest message is TRUE, not aspirational: presence is sidecar-only (`modelStore.ts` `scanOnePresence`), so a kept-but-unrecorded file really does show as absent; and a re-run is safe — `ggufIngest.ts:410-425` admits `destKind === 'file'` (not symlink/dir/other) at the pre-rename re-check, so `renameTemp` renames the freshly-downloaded temp OVER the existing file and the pipeline re-writes the sidecar — so "downloading again replaces the file and retries the record" genuinely happens, it is not a promise the code can't keep.

**Proof (`src/host/setup/sidecarWriter.test.ts`, NEW, describe `'makeSidecarWriter — WS-R2 R2-3 (L2-CA-18, OD-C non-frozen half)'`):** a stub `writeFile` driven by a scripted outcome queue proves three cases. (i) first call rejects an `EIO`-coded error, second call resolves → the writer RESOLVES; `writeFile` called exactly 2×. (ii) both calls reject `EIO`-coded errors → the writer REJECTS with `GgufSidecarWriteError`; `.cause` is the SECOND (last) error, not the first; called exactly 2×; the message contains "downloaded and verified, but not recorded" and does NOT contain the sidecar path. (iii) the first call rejects an error with `.code === 'ELOOP'` → the writer REJECTS IMMEDIATELY with that SAME original error object (`Object.is` identity, not merely the same code), NOT a `GgufSidecarWriteError`; `writeFile` called exactly 1× — never retried, never re-labelled. Confirmed RED before `sidecarWriter.ts` existed (`Cannot find module './sidecarWriter'`); GREEN after (`3 passed`).

**Golden (0 edits):** `ggufIngest.test.ts`'s sidecar-observing tests (`writeSidecar` called/not-called, spy-order, argument shape) drive the frozen `downloadGgufToStore` engine directly against a hand-rolled `vi.fn()` fake for `GgufStoreIo.writeSidecar` — they never construct or call `makeSidecarWriter` — so nothing in this task's diff touches their inputs; all pass unchanged.

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — `git diff` against the pre-mutation file showed zero drift after restore):** (a) dropping the early `if (isEloopRefusal(first)) throw first;` guard (so an ELOOP refusal falls into the same retry path as any other failure) reproduces case (iii)'s exact failure mode: the writer resolves `undefined` instead of rejecting with the original ELOOP error (`expected undefined to be Error: ELOOP...`) — the refusal was silently retried and swallowed, exactly the H3b violation this task exists to prevent. (b) replacing the retry block with a bare `throw first;` (i.e. no second `writeFile` call at all) reproduces case (i)'s failure — the writer now rejects on a single transient `EIO` instead of resolving after 2 calls — and as a side effect also fails case (ii) (the error surfaces as the raw first `EIO`, never wrapped as `GgufSidecarWriteError`), which is consistent: with no retry logic left, none of the retry-dependent behaviour holds.

**[SEC] note (self-contained in this task, no separate lens dispatched — the change is 39 new lines in one pure leaf module plus a 2-line binding rewire, no new I/O primitive, no new trust boundary):** `isEloopRefusal` only ever reads `.code` off an already-thrown error object — it manufactures nothing and widens no permission. The retry re-issues the exact same `(sidecarPath, bytes)` pair to the exact same injected `writeFile` (`writeFileNoFollow`, unchanged) — no new path is ever constructed, and `sidecarPath` is always `` `${destPath}.talaria.json` ``, already computed by the frozen `ggufIngest.ts:441` before this seam is ever reached. The only user-visible surface is the error message, and it deliberately omits the path (I-16) precisely so the honest-failure channel cannot become a path-disclosure channel.

## ADR-025-F — CA-15 outcome (R2-4, WS-R2, FROZEN-TOUCH, OD-B — LAST task of WS-R2)

**Outcome (task R2-4, WS-R2, L2-CA-15, OD-B — charter-FROZEN-ZONE touch, owner-authorized):** `modelStore.ts`'s `scanPresence` (:298-315) did up to 26 SERIAL fs round-trips per `status()` call — a `for...of` loop `await`ing each catalog row's `scanOnePresence` one at a time, itself 2 serial awaits (`readFile` the sidecar, then `statSize` the dest). The owner accepted the READ-path concurrency rewrite: `catalog.map(async (model) => ...)` over `Promise.all`, so every eligible row's sidecar read launches in the SAME synchronous pass (all N `readFile` calls fire before `scanPresence`'s own `await Promise.all(...)` first suspends), then the result `Map` is assembled from the settled array in catalog order, skipping `undefined` entries (non-gguf rows). `storeRoot(io.env)` is still computed exactly once, outside the map. `scanOnePresence` (:318-336, unexported) and the write gate `lstatCheckedGgufDest` (:177-208) are **byte-identical** — confirmed by a direct line-range diff against `HEAD` (zero output both ranges). The diff is confined to `scanPresence`'s body; every other frozen file (`hfDigest.ts`, `safeWrite.ts`, and every file outside `modelStore.ts`/`modelStore.test.ts`) is 0-diff per `git status`.

Every existing truth-table golden in `modelStore.test.ts` (present/absent/foreign-file/`.part`-file/size-mismatch/malformed-sidecar/nothing-on-disk/never-hashes/non-gguf-excluded/fail-closed-no-root/multi-row) is unchanged — 0 edits — and green, because none of them observes ORDER of internal fs calls, only the final `Map` contents; `Promise.all` preserves array order identically to the old loop's insertion order, so the assembly is behaviourally indistinguishable to every one of those tests. No memo/cache was added — the map is recomputed on every call, so a freshly-downloaded model still shows present on the very next `status()`.

**Proof (`modelStore.test.ts`, new describe `'scanPresence — WS-R2 R2-4 (L2-CA-15): concurrent sidecar reads (FROZEN-TOUCH, OD-B)'`):** (1) **overlap** — a deferred-IO stub whose `readFile` returns a manually-resolved `Promise` and records call order; a 4-row catalog (`FIXTURE_MODEL`, a non-gguf `OLLAMA_ONLY_MODEL` interleaved, `MODEL_B`, `MODEL_C`) is scanned WITHOUT awaiting the result yet — a synchronous assertion immediately after the call proves all 3 eligible `readFile` calls are already recorded (`readCalls` has all 3 paths) BEFORE any is resolved. Confirmed RED against the pre-fix serial code: only `readCalls.length === 1` (the loop suspends on row 1's `await` before the `for...of` ever reaches row 2). (2) **catalog order** — the 3 deferred reads are resolved deliberately OUT of catalog order (index 2, then 0, then 1); the returned `Map`'s key iteration order is still `['fixture-model', 'model-b', 'model-c']` — the catalog's order, not the resolution order. (3) **reject-on-first-error** — a `readFile` that throws for the SECOND eligible row (`MODEL_B`) makes the whole `scanPresence(...)` call reject with that exact `Error` object (`.rejects.toBe(boom)`, identity-checked) — this test already passed against the pre-fix serial code (both implementations propagate a fs rejection), and stays green after the fix; it exists as the mutation-proof golden for step (b) below. GREEN after the core change: all 44 tests in the file pass.

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — `git diff` against a pre-mutation snapshot showed zero drift after restore both times):** (a) reverting `scanPresence`'s body to the original serial `for...of` reproduces the exact pre-fix RED on the overlap assertion (`readCalls` has only 1 entry, not 3) — 43/44 pass, 1 fail. (b) swapping `Promise.all` for `Promise.allSettled` (mapping a `'rejected'` outcome to `undefined`, i.e. silently dropped from the map) reproduces a NEW failure on the reject-on-first-error golden: `scanPresence` now RESOLVES to a partial `Map` (`{'fixture-model' => false, 'model-c' => false}`, `model-b` silently missing) instead of rejecting — exactly the swallowed-error regression critic M-7 named. 43/44 pass, 1 fail, different test than mutation (a). Both mutations were reverted; the restored diff is byte-identical to the pre-mutation diff.

**Byte-identity checks (recorded, not merely asserted):** `diff <(git show HEAD:src/host/setup/modelStore.ts | sed -n '177,208p') <(sed -n '177,208p' modelStore.ts)` → empty (write gate `lstatCheckedGgufDest` byte-identical). `diff` of the `scanOnePresence` function body (post-edit :318-336) against `HEAD`'s :316-334 → empty. `git status --short` after the full task shows only `modelStore.ts` and `modelStore.test.ts` modified — every other frozen file (`hfDigest.ts`, `safeWrite.ts`, `sidecarWriter.ts`, `ggufIngest.ts`, etc.) 0-diff.

**★ [SEC] review direction (independent lens, dispatched separately per the brief):** The change is a pure CONCURRENCY rewrite of the READ path (`scanPresence`): it launches the SAME `scanOnePresence` reads that ran serially, now overlapped, and assembles the SAME Map. It does NOT touch the WRITE gate (`lstatCheckedGgufDest` :177-208 byte-identical) and does NOT change what counts as "present" (still sidecar-parses + `size === sidecar.bytes`). The security-relevant invariants are: (a) reject-on-first-error is PRESERVED (a fs error still fails `status()` — `Promise.all` not `allSettled`); (b) no TOCTOU is introduced — `scanPresence` is read-only advisory (presence for the UI), never a gate that authorizes a write; (c) the frozen write path is 0-diff. The [SEC] reviewer verifies THESE (read-path concurrency only, write gate untouched, error semantics preserved), and confirms the frozen diff is confined to `scanPresence`.

**WS-R2 closeout:** this is the fourth and LAST task of WS-R2 (R2-1 CA-16 → ADR-025-D, R2-2 CA-19 → recorded inline above ADR-025-C, R2-3 CA-18 → ADR-025-E, R2-4 CA-15 → this section). All four commits are isolated, each touching only its own named seam; the two FROZEN-ZONE touches this workstream made (R2-3's `sidecarWriter.ts` binding rewire under OD-C, and this task's `scanPresence` concurrency rewrite under OD-B) both left their respective write/verify gates byte-identical.

## ADR-025-G — CA-07 outcome (R3-1, WS-R3 — the only Wave-0 webview task)

**Outcome (task R3-1, WS-R3, L2-CA-07 — broader than filed, BOTH reasoning arms):** `transcript.ts`'s `reasoning.delta` and `reasoning.end` folds — the two arms that append/settle a streaming reasoning ("Thinking…") block — used to rebuild the WHOLE transcript with `tab.transcript.map(...)` on every single token, an O(n) rebuild per streamed delta. CA-07 was filed against one arm; the seam refresh confirmed the SAME full-map shape in both. Both arms now mirror `message.delta`'s existing tail-first splice (`:334-364` after this task — the precedent's own line range moved down by the two arms' new bodies above it, but its content is byte-identical to before this task) EXACTLY: check the tail first (`last.kind === 'reasoning' && last.blockId === msg.blockId`, O(1) common case, since any interleaving item — a new tool/approval/plan/message block — closes and moves on, so the open reasoning block is provably the last element in the normal flow); on a miss, fall back to `[...tab.transcript].reverse().find(...)` (rare, a reasoning block that is not last); on a match, a TARGETED SPLICE (`tab.transcript.slice()` + one index write) instead of a per-element `.map` callback — unchanged items keep their references, exactly like the old `.map(i => i === match ? {...} : i)`. On no match at all (a missing reasoning block is not a normal flow — `reasoning.start` always creates it first) both arms return `{ ...tab, transcript: tab.transcript }` — the literal identity fallback, chosen over re-deriving a fresh `.map()` pass for the miss case since a genuine miss never happens on the normal wire and identity is the cheapest, most honest "nothing changed" result.

**Write-time finding — the brief's literal perf numbers don't survive the live cap (recorded here, not just in the task report, since it shapes the test design permanently in `transcript.test.ts`):** the brief asked for "2000 reasoning.delta onto a 2000-item tab" to prove a `< 50 ms` bound. A 2000-item tab is already 4x over `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_ITEMS` (500) — `reduce` runs `capTranscript` (CA-09) right after every fold, and `capTranscript` UNCONDITIONALLY does its own full-array scan-and-rebuild whenever `length > 500`, even when (a same-turn seed, so nothing is eligible to trim) it ends up a no-op. That scan costs the SAME for the old `.map` and the new splice, so above the cap it dominates and the measured old/new wall-clock ratio collapsed to ~1.6–2x — at the brief's literal 2000/2000 numbers, old measured 49.8 ms, barely over the 50 ms line, not a safe RED, and under `npm run gate`'s real parallel worker contention (measured ~3x slower than an isolated run) a wall-clock assertion with that little headroom flaked in both directions during this task's own verification. The test now seeds AT the cap (500 items: 499 settled + 1 open reasoning tail, all sharing the reasoning block's own turnId so `capTranscript`'s turn-aware guard — CA-09 — never trims and the fold's own cost is isolated cleanly) and keeps the brief's literal delta count (2000) as a corroborating wall-clock check (`< 50 ms`, measured ~5-9 ms post-fix, generous margin under contention) — but the PRIMARY, mutation-sensitive, timing-independent proof is two new spy-based tests mirroring `message.delta`'s OWN existing precedent test (`CA-09`, which already solves exactly this class of problem with a spy on `Array.prototype.reverse` rather than a clock): on the tail-match path, neither `reasoning.delta` nor `reasoning.end` may call `Array.prototype.map` (the old full rebuild) or `Array.prototype.reverse` (the fallback scan) — deterministic, and exactly what the brief's own named mutation ("force the tail check always false") breaks.

**Proof (`transcript.test.ts`, describe `'L2-CA-07: reasoning.delta/reasoning.end fold tail-first like message.delta (broader than filed — both arms)'`):** (1) a **result-identity property** test keeps the PRE-CHANGE `.map` bodies as a test-local ORACLE and, over 300 random `reasoning.start`/`reasoning.delta`/`reasoning.end` interleavings across 2 concurrently-open turns on a 300-item seeded tab (a deterministic mulberry32 PRNG, not `Math.random`, so the run is reproducible), asserts the real reducer's transcript `toEqual`s the oracle's after every single step — this also exercises the non-tail reverse-scan fallback organically, since with 2 turns interleaved the earlier turn's open block is frequently NOT last. (2) the two spy-based structural tests above. (3) two reference-identity goldens (`reasoning.delta`/`reasoning.end` each leave an unrelated item's object reference untouched — `toBe`, not `toEqual`). (4) two dedicated FALLBACK goldens (a hand-built tab whose reasoning block sits BEFORE a later message item — the reverse-scan finds it and updates it in place; the later item's reference is untouched). (5) the corroborating wall-clock perf test. Every pre-existing reasoning-fold golden in the file (including the interleaved session-A/session-B partition test and the settleOpenItems/turn.end suites) is unchanged — 0 edits — and green.

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — `git diff` against the pre-mutation file showed zero drift after each restore):** forcing `matchLast` to always be `false` (so even a genuinely-last reasoning block falls through to the reverse-scan branch) was applied independently to each arm. Each time, that arm's OWN spy-based structural test failed immediately (`reverseSpy` WAS called) while the other arm's test stayed green — proving the two arms are tested independently, not by a shared fixture that would mask a one-arm regression. The corroborating wall-clock test (2000 deltas, small-M by design) did NOT reliably fail under this specific mutation — expected and documented above: it is corroborating evidence of absolute speed, not the mutation gate. A full revert of one arm to the original `.map()` body (git-stashed, not left in the tree) was also verified to fail BOTH the wall-clock test (181 ms vs 50 ms, isolated run) and would fail the structural test the same way (no `.map()` call expected, one occurred).

**Chain note for the god-unit decomposition wave (F5, not part of this task):** the closeoout plan's `transcript.ts` chain is `R3-1 → F5-1 (goldens/trip-wire) → F5-2..F5-6`; F5-2 moves `foldTab` (carrying these two fixed arms verbatim) into a new `transcriptFold.ts` leaf. Nothing in this task's diff needs revisiting for that move — the fix lives entirely inside the two `case` bodies F5-2 relocates as-is.

## ADR-025-H — FI-12 outcome, part 1/2 (task F4-1, WS-F4)

**Outcome (task F4-1, WS-F4, FI-12 — first of three, OD-G "DO at the default"):** `formats/shared.ts` (not frozen — no freeze marker; only `sweepV2.ts`/`genericInstruct.ts`/the lock header carry "frozen" prose) now EXPORTS its private line splitter and gains four new helpers it did not carry before, all moved/generalized out of `sweepV2.ts` and `genericInstruct.ts`'s byte-identical private copies: (1) `splitLinesKeepingTerminators` — same name, same body/doc, `export` added; the `lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts` drift lock matches the substring `function splitLinesKeepingTerminators(`, which survives the added keyword, so the lock stays green through this commit. (2) `relativeCursorOffset(region, cursor)` — moved verbatim from `sweepV2.ts:82-99` (byte-identical twin also in `genericInstruct.ts:282-299`), with the ONE deliberate change being that it now calls `shared.ts`'s own exported splitter instead of either format's private `splitKeepingNewlines` twin; `import type { EditableRegion, NextEditCursor } from '../types'` added. (3) `export const DIFF_CHAR_BUDGET = 4000` — the byte-identical budget constant from `sweepV2.ts:43`/`genericInstruct.ts:78`. (4) `renderBudgetedBlocks<T>(items, renderOne, budget, separator)` — generalizes the byte-identical `renderRecentChanges` budget loop (`sweepV2.ts:214-227` via `renderDiffBlock`, `genericInstruct.ts:132-145` via `renderDiffPair`), preserving the `break`-at-first-over-budget (never a skip-and-continue) and the `separator.length` join-cost. (5) `trimAtFirstStopToken(text, stops)` — generalizes the byte-identical stop-trim loop (`sweepV2.ts:352-357`/`genericInstruct.ts:409-415`, both walking `STOP_TOKENS`), sequential and order-sensitive over the RUNNING result, never the original text.

This commit only ADDS — it removes NO copy. `sweepV2.ts` and `genericInstruct.ts` keep their own private twins untouched (0-diff), and `nextedit/lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts` keeps comparing all five splitter copies (this file's now-exported one included) exactly as before. The consolidation — replacing both format modules' copies with imports of these five, and deleting the now-redundant `lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts` — is task **F4-2** (next task of WS-F4), per OD-G's "two-decisions 1a" default.

**Proof (`shared.test.ts`, WS-F4 F4-1 section):** the drift lock's own 12-probe corpus (`empty string` through `trailing whitespace before terminator`) is lifted verbatim and re-run here as real round-trip + per-line-terminator-contract tests of the ONE exported `splitLinesKeepingTerminators` (critic I-14 — this coverage must exist BEFORE F4-2 deletes the lock, so F4-2 causes no coverage drop). `relativeCursorOffset` is proven on all four clamp edges (before-region → 0, past-last-line → start of last line, character-past-line-end → line end before its terminator, mid-region → exact offset). `renderBudgetedBlocks` is proven on the brief's own RED case — `[tiny, huge(>budget), tiny]` with a budget fitting only the first `tiny` — and on separator-cost/genericity. `trimAtFirstStopToken` is proven sequential (`'aXbYc'` against `['Y','X']` and `['X','Y']`, both converging to `'a'` only because each stop runs over the already-trimmed result) AND genuinely order-sensitive: **`trimAtFirstStopToken('ABAB', ['AB','BAB'])` → `''`, but `trimAtFirstStopToken('ABAB', ['BAB','AB'])` → `'A'`** — swapping the order changes the result, because `'BAB'` (starting at index 1) is only findable while the running text still has its full 4-character length, so cutting `'AB'` (index 0) first empties the text before `'BAB'` is ever searched, while cutting `'BAB'` first leaves `'A'`, too short for the 2-character `'AB'` to fit. (Note for the reviewer: the brief's own literal `'aXbYc'`/`['Y','X']` vs `['X','Y']` example converges to `'a'` under EITHER order — it proves the loop is sequential-over-the-running-result, not that order changes the FINAL result; the brief itself flags this ("pick tokens where order changes the result … to make the order test bite"), and the `'ABAB'`/`['AB','BAB']` pair above is the one that actually bites on order.)

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing only the two intended MUTATION-TEST-ONLY lines during each probe, zero drift after restore):** (a) BUDGET — replacing `renderBudgetedBlocks`'s `break` with `continue` (skip-and-continue instead of stop) makes the trailing `tiny` get included after the oversized block (`'tiny\ntiny'` instead of `'tiny'`) — exactly 1 of 38 tests fails, the "NOT included" test, no other. (b) STOP, two independent variants both proven: (i) returning immediately after the first `idx !== -1` match makes `trimAtFirstStopToken('aXbYc', ['Y','X'])` stop at `'aXb'` instead of continuing to `'a'` — exactly 1 test fails (the sequential test). (ii) searching the ORIGINAL `text` instead of the running `out` on every iteration makes `trimAtFirstStopToken('ABAB', ['BAB','AB'])` return `''` instead of `'A'` (the original-text search finds `'AB'` at its original index 0 even though the running result had already been cut to `'A'`) — exactly 1 test fails (the order-sensitivity test), the different one from (i), confirming the two mutations are caught by two DIFFERENT assertions, not one lucky test.

**Byte-fidelity check (recorded, not merely asserted):** `relativeCursorOffset`'s body diffed line-by-line against `sweepV2.ts:82-99` is empty EXCEPT the one line calling the splitter (`splitKeepingNewlines` → `splitLinesKeepingTerminators`, the documented, deliberate change) — every clamp (`Math.min`/`Math.max`), every `?? 0`/`?? ''` fallback, and the doc comment's `04 §1.2/§1.4` citation are byte-identical. `DIFF_CHAR_BUDGET`, the `renderBudgetedBlocks` shape (field order, `break`, join), and `trimAtFirstStopToken`'s loop shape all match the brief's pinned code blocks exactly.

**Golden (0 edits):** `shared.test.ts`'s pre-existing `sliceLines`/`isPureInsertionAboveCursor` suites, both format modules' own byte-pinned prompt tests (`sweepV2.test.ts`, `genericInstruct.test.ts`), the drift lock (`lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts`, all 12 probes plus the synthetic `diagnoseDrift` suite), `reuseLocks.test.ts` (file-count pin unaffected — no file added/removed), `nextEditPurity.test.ts` (pure/headless boundary unaffected — `shared.ts` still imports only a type from `../types`, no `vscode`/`node:fs`), `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` locks (no `as` cast, no `{ ...x, }` spread anywhere in the five new lines of code), and `docTopology.lock.test.ts`/`coexistence.lock.test.ts` are all unchanged and green — 243/243 across all eight sibling lock/golden files run together, plus `npm run gate` full-suite green with the count RISING (baseline → +38, no regressions).

**Next in the FI-12 chain:** F4-2 (WS-F4, same ADR-025-H section, amended in place) replaces `sweepV2.ts`'s and `genericInstruct.ts`'s five private copies with imports of these five `shared.ts` exports and deletes `lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts` in the same commit as the first removed copy, per OD-G.

## ADR-025-H — FI-12 outcome, part 2/2 (task F4-2, WS-F4, FI-12 + FI-22 splitter half — CLOSES the chain)

**Outcome (task F4-2, WS-F4, OD-G "DO at the default"):** the four remaining byte-twin copy sites are replaced with imports of F4-1's `formats/shared.ts` exports, and `nextedit/lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts` — the five-copy drift lock whose entire purpose was catching drift among copies that no longer exist — is `git rm`'d in the SAME commit (a partial removal is impossible: the lock throws "not found" the instant any one copy is gone).

1. **`sweepV2.ts`:** local `splitKeepingNewlines`, `relativeCursorOffset`, and `const DIFF_CHAR_BUDGET = 4000` (plus their doc comments, including the "Task 5's module is frozen" local-twin justification, which now annotates deleted code) are deleted; `computePrefill`'s one internal call-site (`splitKeepingNewlines(prefix)`) is rewired to the imported `splitLinesKeepingTerminators(prefix)`. `renderRecentChanges`'s 14-line budget loop becomes `return renderBudgetedBlocks(diffs, renderDiffBlock, DIFF_CHAR_BUDGET, '\n');` — `renderDiffBlock` (this file's own per-block renderer, unchanged) passed by reference, `'\n'` reproducing the copy's hard-coded `+1` exactly via `separator.length`. `parse`'s 6-line stop-trim loop becomes `completion = trimAtFirstStopToken(completion, STOP_TOKENS);` — `STOP_TOKENS` stays this file's own `[ENDOFTEXT, FILE_SEP] as const`, untouched. 2. **`genericInstruct.ts`:** the same shape — local `splitKeepingNewlines`, `relativeCursorOffset`, `DIFF_CHAR_BUDGET` deleted (this file's copies never called `splitKeepingNewlines` from anywhere except inside its own now-deleted `relativeCursorOffset`, so no other internal call-site needed rewiring). `renderRecentChanges` becomes `return renderBudgetedBlocks(diffs, renderDiffPair, DIFF_CHAR_BUDGET, '\n');` (`renderDiffPair` — this file's own renderer, unchanged). `parse`'s stop-trim loop becomes `completion = trimAtFirstStopToken(completion, STOP_TOKENS);` — this file's own `STOP_TOKENS = [CODE_BLOCK_CLOSE, IM_END, '<|endoftext|>'] as const`, untouched. Same `EditableRegion`/`NextEditCursor` import cleanup. One doc-comment cross-reference (rule 7 of `parse`'s doc comment, "computed by this module's own `relativeCursorOffset`, above") is corrected to name the import.
3. **`shell.vscode.ts`:** local `splitKeepingNewlines` and `extractLines` (a byte-twin of `sliceLines`) deleted; `import { sliceLines, splitLinesKeepingTerminators } from './formats/shared'` added. `extractRegionRange`'s two call sites rewired by symbol (`splitKeepingNewlines(text).length` → `splitLinesKeepingTerminators(text).length`; `extractLines(text, startLine, endLine)` → `sliceLines(text, startLine, endLine)`) — chosen over the brief's offered `import { sliceLines as extractLines }` alias, since a direct rename reads clearer at the two call sites and the file has no other consumer of the old name. The doc comment immediately above (explaining why `getText`'s stripped terminator and the splitter's kept terminator must be composed the way they are) had two prose mentions of the now-deleted `extractLines` name updated to `sliceLines` — a symbol rename only, not the freeze-prose sweep (F4-3's job).
4. **`context/editTrackerAdapter.ts`:** local `splitKeepingNewlines` and `extractLines` deleted; `import { sliceLines, splitLinesKeepingTerminators } from '../nextedit/formats/shared'` added (import direction `context/ → nextedit/formats/` — ACCEPTED per the brief: acyclic, and `nextEditPurity.test.ts` only bans `vscode`/`node:fs` imports, not this one). Three call sites rewired by symbol: `replaceLines`'s `splitKeepingNewlines(text)` → `splitLinesKeepingTerminators(text)`; the change-handler's `extractLines(shadow, startLine, endLine)` → `sliceLines(shadow, startLine, endLine)` and its immediately following `splitKeepingNewlines(span)` → `splitLinesKeepingTerminators(span)`. **`replaceLines` itself STAYS, unmoved and unrenamed** — confirmed NOT a copy (it composes the splitter with slice/concat, a distinct operation none of the four other files carry).
5. **`nextedit/lineSplitDrift.lock.test.ts` — `git rm`'d in this same commit.** Its own doc comment (`W5.2 — the five-copy drift lock`) explained its purpose as tying five independently-frozen copies together so a fix landing in four of five would fail; once F4-2 leaves ONE function with four import sites, that failure mode cannot exist, and the lock's own extraction-and-`ts.transpileModule`-and-invoke machinery has no remaining subject to extract from four of the five original locations (only `shared.ts`'s copy remains a real declaration).

**Golden (0 edits, the faithfulness net):** `sweepV2.test.ts`, `genericInstruct.test.ts` (both files' byte-pinned PROMPT-construction goldens), `shell.vscode.test.ts`, and `editTracker.test.ts` (which carries `editTrackerAdapter.ts`'s own `makeAdapterUnderTest` mocked-`vscode` suite, including the character-precise shadow-splice tests that exercise `replaceLines` via the now-imported splitter) are ALL unchanged (`git status` shows zero test-file diffs for this commit) and green — confirmed by running the five golden files together (240 + 67 = so 5 files together in one run: 4 format/shell files = 240 passed; `editTracker.test.ts` + `shared.test.ts` together = 67 passed, no failures in either run). This is the proof the move is behavior-preserving: any one-character divergence in the ported splitter/budget/stop logic would have shown up as a byte-pinned prompt mismatch, and none did.

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — restored to the exact prior text after each probe, confirmed via a subsequent full gate run showing the same 7512/339 count as before the probes):**
(a) **Renderer swap** (sweepV2): `renderBudgetedBlocks(diffs, renderDiffBlock, …)` mutated to pass a dummy `(d) => \`MUTATION-TEST-${d.filepath}\`` renderer in place of `renderDiffBlock` — `sweepV2.test.ts`'s skip-not-crop suite failed immediately (2 of 33 tests: the "small pair kept, huge pair absent" assertions expected `SMALL_MARKER_XYZ` in the prompt and got `MUTATION-TEST-…` instead). Proves the per-file renderer argument (`renderDiffBlock` vs. `renderDiffPair`) is load-bearing and covered.
(b) **Wrong stop-token set** (genericInstruct): `trimAtFirstStopToken(completion, STOP_TOKENS)` mutated to `trimAtFirstStopToken(completion, ['<|endoftext|>'] as const)` (this file's real completions never carry a bare `<|endoftext|>`, only `</code_block>`/`<|im_end|>`) — `genericInstruct.test.ts`'s echo/stop suites failed immediately (12 of 45 tests: `</code_block>` and `<|im_end|>` remnants stopped being trimmed at all, so completions carried trailing tag/sentinel junk into `newText`, and one echo-dismissal case that should have been `invalid` instead fell through). Proves the per-file `STOP_TOKENS` argument is load-bearing and covered.
(c) **A pure reordering of a real per-file `STOP_TOKENS` array (`[ENDOFTEXT, FILE_SEP]` → `[FILE_SEP, ENDOFTEXT]`, tried on genericInstruct's own array reversed) was ALSO attempted and did NOT break any golden** — worth recording honestly rather than silently dropping. `trimAtFirstStopToken`'s own `shared.test.ts` synthetic suite (F4-1) already proves the algorithm IS genuinely order-sensitive in general (`'ABAB'` against `['AB','BAB']` vs. `['BAB','AB']` converges to two DIFFERENT results, because a multi-character stop token's match can straddle a cut boundary an earlier stop already introduced) — but order only bites when two stop tokens' matches overlap character-wise in the SAME completion, which neither format's real token set does in the existing fixtures (each format's tokens are long, non-overlapping literals, and a well-formed completion stops at exactly one of them). So (a)+(b) are the mutations that actually demonstrate this task's real risk — wiring the wrong renderer or the wrong TOKEN SET — while a same-set reordering is a legitimate but non-biting probe here, consistent with (not contradicting) F4-1's own note that its brief's literal order-sensitivity example also failed to bite and had to be replaced with `'ABAB'`.

6. **One Stop path**: `fimActivity.requestStarted` (`:1016-1033`) interrupting an in-flight prediction — `abortInFlight()` (`:1374-1387`, the real `AbortSignal`'s `abort` event fires FIRST) → `dispatch({fimVisibility:true})` → `fsm.ts`'s unconditional `cleared()` (`:73-76`, ANY state → `idle`+`clearAll`) → the two `setContext(...,false)` calls, in that order.

**HONEST GAP, re-grounded by symbol (report this per the brief's own review-focus item):** on current HEAD, `resolveRoute`, `partitionEgressableDiffs`, `ensureTrailingNewline`, `stripLineTerminator`, `extractRegionRange`, `toWorkspaceRelativePosixPath`, and the `NextEditShell` class itself are ALL module-private (re-confirmed by `grep '^export'` — only `diffMayEgress` among the "pure surfaces" the brief lists, plus `makeExecutor`/`requestNextEditToggle`/`deriveGenericTransport`/`genericUnsupportedBackendMessage`/the two note constants/`registerTalariaNextEdit`, are exported). The brief's "import every pinned surface FROM `./shell.vscode`" and "instantiate the `NextEditShell` class" instructions PRESUME an export surface that F3-2..F3-8 will create by MOVING these into new modules the shell re-exports from — a future state, not today's. Instead, every private surface is pinned INDIRECTLY through the one sole exported entry point that already exists — `registerTalariaNextEdit` — reading each surface's effect off the exact runtime objects it produces: the `NextEditRequest` handed to a mocked `NextEditHttpBackend.predict()` (pins the text helpers' outputs — `fileContext`, `preEditRegion`, `region.filepath` — byte-for-byte), that same mock's construction options (pins `resolveRoute`'s route fields), and the shell's own `deps.reportFailure`/`vscode.window.showWarningMessage` calls (pins the byte-exact failure copy and the route-resolution one-shot messages). ## ADR-025-J — `text` module extracted, F3-2/9 (task F3-2, WS-F3)

**Outcome (task F3-2, WS-F3 extraction #1, FI-06):** the first of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the three PURE text helpers `ensureTrailingNewline`, `stripLineTerminator`, `extractRegionRange` (`shell.vscode.ts:568-609` on the F3-1 HEAD, `cf689fe`) move VERBATIM (bodies + doc comments) into a NEW leaf `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditText.ts`. `extractRegionRange` keeps calling the sibling `stripLineTerminator` in-module and now imports `splitLinesKeepingTerminators`/`sliceLines` directly from `./formats/shared` (the ADR-025-H canonical splitter) rather than through the shell. `shell.vscode.ts` replaces the three bodies with `import { ensureTrailingNewline, extractRegionRange, stripLineTerminator } from './nextEditText';` plus `export { ensureTrailingNewline, stripLineTerminator, extractRegionRange };` — a re-export, per ADR-FSU-01's mechanics, so the shell's own surface and both internal call sites (`extractRegionRange` at the old `:1544`, `ensureTrailingNewline` at the old `:1580`) keep resolving through `./shell.vscode` with zero further edits. **Golden (0 edits, the faithfulness net):** `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts` (53 tests) + `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts` (2 tests) + `shell.vscode.test.ts` — all THREE files show zero diff (`git status`/`git diff` confirm no test-file changes this commit) and all pass, together with the full `formats/`+`editTracker` suites, in the same full-gate run below. ## ADR-025-K — `route` module extracted, F3-3/9 (task F3-3, WS-F3)

**Outcome (task F3-3, WS-F3 extraction #2, FI-06):** the second of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the route-resolution core plus the route/toggle copy move VERBATIM (bodies + doc comments) from `shell.vscode.ts` (re-grounded by symbol on `224905c`, the F3-2 HEAD) into a NEW leaf `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditRoute.ts` — `DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS`, `GENERIC_SETUP_NOTE`, `NEXT_EDIT_MODEL_UNSET_NOTE`, `genericUnsupportedBackendMessage`, `deriveGenericTransport`, `interface NextEditRoute`, the module-private `isLoopbackEndpoint`, `type RouteResolution`, `function resolveRoute`, `function endpointLabel`. `resolveRoute` and `endpointLabel` were module-private in the shell; both are now `export`ed (required for the format-pin obligation below) — `isLoopbackEndpoint` stays module-private, its only caller. `shell.vscode.ts` replaces the moved bodies with `import { resolveRoute, endpointLabel, deriveGenericTransport, GENERIC_SETUP_NOTE, NEXT_EDIT_MODEL_UNSET_NOTE, genericUnsupportedBackendMessage, type NextEditRoute } from './nextEditRoute';` plus `export { deriveGenericTransport, GENERIC_SETUP_NOTE, NEXT_EDIT_MODEL_UNSET_NOTE, genericUnsupportedBackendMessage };` — a re-export of the four symbols that were part of the shell's public surface before the move (per ADR-FSU-01's mechanics), so the shell's own export surface and every internal call site (`requestNextEditToggle`, `resolveReportedRoute` at the old `:1447`, `surfaceTriggerFailure` at the old `:1253`) keep resolving through `./shell.vscode` with zero further edits. `resolveRoute`/`isLoopbackEndpoint`/`NextEditRoute`/`RouteResolution`/`DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS` were NOT part of the shell's pre-move public surface, so none of those five needed re-exporting. `nextEditRoute.ts` imports `readNextEditConfig` from `./config`, `sweepV2Format`/`genericInstructFormat` from their real `./formats/*` homes, and `isLoopbackHost` from `../backends/secureTransport` — the REAL sources, not through the shell, so there is no runtime cycle; it imports `NextEditShellDeps` `type`-only from `./shell.vscode` (erased at compile time, confirmed by a clean `tsc --noEmit`), which is the one runtime-safe way to spell `resolveRoute`'s parameter type without duplicating the interface.

**Ground-truth deviation from the brief (documented, not silent):** the brief's step 3 named `type { NextEditRoute, RouteResolution }` as both needing import into the shell. Re-grounding by symbol on the current HEAD (`noUnusedLocals: true` in `tsconfig.json`) showed `RouteResolution` is never spelled by name anywhere else in `shell.vscode.ts` — every internal call site holds the value from `resolveRoute(...)` in an untyped `const` and narrows it structurally by `.kind`, so importing the type unused would have been a tsc error. `RouteResolution` is imported into `nextEditRoute.ts` only (where it is the real declared type of `resolveRoute`'s return) and is not imported into the shell at all; `NextEditRoute` IS imported into the shell (as the type `surfaceTriggerFailure`/`resolveReportedRoute`/`buildRequest`/`runPrediction` still spell in their own signatures).

**`nextEditPurity.test.ts` self-trip caught and fixed within this task, before the commit:** the module's own header doc originally spelled the literal banned token verbatim — `` no `import … from 'vscode'` in any form `` — which is exactly the `VSCODE_IMPORT_BAN` regex's `from\s+['"]vscode['"]` shape, since that scan (unlike `reuseLocks.test.ts`'s `num_ctx` lock) does NOT strip comments before matching. Caught by running the full `nextedit/` suite before considering the move done (RED: "no module under nextedit/ imports vscode EXCEPT the four sanctioned files" flagged `nextEditRoute.ts`); fixed by rewording to "never imports the `vscode` package itself, in any form" — same meaning, no longer a literal `from '...'` shape — re-verified green.

**Format-pin obligation ([F3-3] Important carry-forward — DISCHARGED):** F3-1's golden pinned `resolveRoute`'s output only INDIRECTLY (through `registerTalariaNextEdit`, never asserting `.route.format` itself); its only format guard was `shell.vscode.test.ts:938`/`:954` on the F3-1 HEAD (re-grounded at `:951`/`:964` on this task's own HEAD, having drifted only by the import-block's line count — same two assertions, byte-identical, zero test edit), exercised through the public entry. A NEW file, `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditRoute.test.ts` (15 tests), now calls the exported `resolveRoute` directly and asserts the WHOLE `RouteResolution` shape — every field of `.route` plus the discriminated `.kind` — for: `next` with model set/empty endpoint (loopback default, `sweepV2Format`), `next` with a REMOTE endpoint (`remote: true`), `next` with `cfg.model === ''` (`next-model-unset`), `generic`/ollama with and without an API key (`genericInstructFormat`, the apiKey present/absent branch), `generic`/vllm (derives `openai-compat`), `generic` with a REMOTE endpoint (`remote: true`), `generic`/codestral and `generic`/openai-compat (both `generic-unsupported-backend`), `generic` with an empty endpoint and, separately, an empty model (`generic-unconfigured`), and `mode:'off'` (`mode-off`) — plus a direct `endpointLabel` pin and a non-vacuous "reach" test asserting the two format singletons are distinct objects, both actually exercised as `.route.format`. `./config` is mocked (mirroring `config.test.ts`'s own reason: `readNextEditConfig` reads `vscode.workspace.getConfiguration` under the hood) so this file needs no `vi.mock('vscode', ...)` of its own — `nextEditRoute.ts`'s own `NextEditShellDeps` import is type-only and never runtime-pulls the shell.

## ADR-025-L — `egress` module extracted, F3-4/9 (task F3-4, WS-F3, `[SEC]`)

**Ground-truth deviation from the brief (documented, not silent):** the brief's own text said "ADR-025-D absorbs the retired dead-`.dropped` paragraph." Re-grounding on the live HEAD showed `ADR-025-D` is already a filled section (CA-16/R2-1/WS-R2's concurrency outcome, unrelated) — the letter was stale from an earlier planning pass. The correct, still-open slot per this file's own append-only per-workstream-appendix convention is `ADR-025-L` (the next `<topic> (TBD)` stub after F3-3's `ADR-025-K`), used here instead.

**Outcome (task F3-4, WS-F3 extraction #3, FI-06, FI-27):** the third of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the egress predicate + the pure diff/content-change helpers move VERBATIM (bodies + doc comments) from `shell.vscode.ts` (re-grounded by symbol on `88f6729`, the F3-3 HEAD) into a NEW leaf `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditEgress.ts` — `diffMayEgress` (the SECURITY PREDICATE, moved BYTE-IDENTICAL, no logic change), `partitionEgressableDiffs` (**RENAMED `filterEgressableDiffs`, FI-27**), `computeChangesAboveCursor`, `toContentChangeLites`. `shell.vscode.ts` replaces the four bodies with `import { computeChangesAboveCursor, filterEgressableDiffs, toContentChangeLites } from './nextEditEgress';` plus `export { diffMayEgress } from './nextEditEgress';` — a re-export per ADR-FSU-01's mechanics, so `diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts`'s own `import { diffMayEgress } from './shell.vscode'` keeps resolving through `./shell.vscode` with zero further edits (F3-9 later repoints that lock directly at `./nextEditEgress` and drops its `vi.mock('vscode')` — not this task). The one internal call site (old `:1363`) drops the `.kept` indirection: `const ringDiffs = partitionEgressableDiffs(...); const diffs = ringDiffs.kept;` becomes `const diffs = filterEgressableDiffs(this.ensureEditTracker().tracker.getRecentDiffs(), route.format.sentinels);` — the neighbouring F-3 comment ("changesAboveCursor reads the same kept list") needed no wording change, since it already described the list, not the wrapper shape. The now-unused `import { scanSnippetForSecrets } from '../context/secretScanner'` and the `RecentDiff` name in the `./types` import are dropped from `shell.vscode.ts` (`noUnusedLocals: true` — nothing else in the file names either).

**FI-27 — `.dropped` retired, not carried forward.** `partitionEgressableDiffs` returned `{ kept, dropped }`; the sole call site destructured `.kept` alone, so `.dropped` had exactly zero readers. `filterEgressableDiffs` now returns `readonly RecentDiff[]` — the kept list only — and the long "`dropped` — WHAT IT IS AND IS NOT" paragraph that explained why the unread count was nonetheless deliberately kept (final review Finding 6, itself correcting an earlier wrong-tense claim that the count WAS read) is deleted from the source; this ADR section is its retirement record. The kept-computation is byte-identical to before: `kept` accumulates `diff` exactly when `diffMayEgress(diff, sentinels)` is true, now via a bare loop instead of also incrementing a `dropped` counter.

**The structural re-type (critic I-1) — what makes THIS module vscode-free.** `toContentChangeLites`'s parameter was `readonly vscode.TextDocumentContentChangeEvent[]`; it is now the LOCAL structural `ContentChangeLike` declared in `nextEditEgress.ts`:
```ts
export interface ContentChangeLike {
  range: { start: { line: number; character: number }; end: { line: number } };
  text: string;
}
```
**Brief correction, applied:** the brief's own plan text for this shape omitted `character`; re-grounding the moved body showed the sort tiebreaker reads `change.range.start.character` (`b.range.start.character - a.range.start.character`), so the shape used here includes it — the plan's literal proposal would have failed `tsc` with "Property 'character' does not exist". `vscode.TextDocumentContentChangeEvent` is structurally assignable to `ContentChangeLike` (its `range.start` is a `Position`, which carries both `line` and `character`; `range.end` carries `line`; `text` is a `string`), and readonly-array covariance carries `readonly vscode.TextDocumentContentChangeEvent[]` into `readonly ContentChangeLike[]` — so the shell's own call site (`toContentChangeLites(e.contentChanges)`, unmoved) typechecks with zero change. The return type stays the pre-existing `ContentChangeLite` (`import type { ContentChangeLite } from './anchors'` — already a vscode-free structural type there); `ContentChangeLite` itself is now unused in `shell.vscode.ts` and dropped from that file's `./anchors` import.

**Direct pins added (FI-27 Minor carry-forward — discharged):** F3-1's golden pinned the KEPT list only INDIRECTLY, via the algebraic identity `diffs.filter(d => diffMayEgress(d, sentinels))` applied to the exported `diffMayEgress` (`nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts:284-295`) — it never called the wrapper by name, which is precisely what makes F3-4's rename 0-edit against it, but also what would let a regression INSIDE the wrapper itself (not the predicate) slip past unnoticed. A NEW file, `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditEgress.test.ts` (10 tests), now calls `filterEgressableDiffs` directly on a 4-entry corpus (a clean diff, a sentinel-bearing diff, an above-cursor pure insertion, an empty diff) and asserts the exact kept subset `[clean, aboveCursorInsertion, empty]`, plus a direct `computeChangesAboveCursor` pin (above/at/below the cursor line, and the no-tracked-diff-for-this-uri case) and a direct `toContentChangeLites` pin (multi-part unsorted input sorts descending by start line then start character, `newLineCount` = newline-count + 1, and the input array is not mutated). This module is pure (no `vscode`, no `./config`), so unlike `nextEditRoute.test.ts` it needs no `vi.mock` at all.

**Golden (0 edits, the faithfulness net):** `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts` (55 tests, incl. the `:284-295` KEPT-list identity) + `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts` + `diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts` (268 L, still `vi.mock('vscode')`, still `import { diffMayEgress } from './shell.vscode'`) — all THREE files show zero diff (`git status`/`git diff --stat` confirm no test-file changes to any of them this commit) and all pass in the same full-gate run below.

**Purity:** `nextEditPurity.test.ts` green with `ADAPTER_ALLOW` UNCHANGED (`config.ts`, `guard.ts`, `shell.vscode.ts`, `nextEditNotice.vscode.ts`) — `nextEditEgress.ts` is vscode-FREE (no `import … from 'vscode'`, not even `import type`) and is not added to that allowlist. `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` all green — the move introduces no `as ScannedSnippet`/`as ScannedNextEditRequest` cast and no `{ ...x, }` spread (the `kept.push(diff)` loop pushes the reference, never spreads it).

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — restored to the exact prior text via a byte-for-byte diff against a pre-mutation backup, confirmed identical both times):** (a) `diffMayEgress` mutated to an unconditional `return true;` → `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts`'s KEPT-list identity assertion AND `diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts`'s filter/mint agreement suite AND the new `nextEditEgress.test.ts` all failed (3 test files, 10 individual test failures) — the sentinel-bearing diff that should have been dropped now appeared in every kept-list assertion; reverted, re-ran green. (b) `filterEgressableDiffs` mutated to `return diffs;` (unfiltered passthrough) → the NEW direct pin in `nextEditEgress.test.ts` failed on 2 of its own assertions (the 4-entry corpus returned all 4 instead of the 3 kept), while the OLDER indirect golden identity (which calls `diffMayEgress` directly, never through the wrapper) correctly stayed green — proving the new pin catches a class of regression (inside the wrapper, not the predicate) the pre-existing golden structurally cannot; reverted, re-ran green.

## ADR-025-M — `executor` module extracted, F3-5/9 (task F3-5, WS-F3)

**Outcome (task F3-5, WS-F3 extraction #4, FI-06):** the fourth of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the FSM's effect-executor moves VERBATIM (bodies + doc comments) from `shell.vscode.ts` (re-grounded by symbol on `2fb9855`, the F3-4 HEAD — line numbers matched the brief's estimates exactly, no drift) into a NEW leaf `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditExecutor.ts` — `type NextEditContextKey`, `interface NextEditExecutorHost`, `interface NextEditExecutor`, `function makeExecutor`. All four were already `export`ed in the shell before this move (unlike F3-3/F3-4's `resolveRoute`/`endpointLabel`/`filterEgressableDiffs`, which went from module-private to exported as PART of their moves) — so no new direct-pin test file is added here; `makeExecutor` was already exhaustively pinned directly (not just through F3-1's golden) by `shell.vscode.test.ts`'s own `describe('next-edit effect executor', …)` block, which imports `makeExecutor`/`type NextEditExecutorHost` from `./shell.vscode` and stays 0-edit + green through the re-export below. This mirrors `nextEditText.ts`'s F3-2 precedent (also already-exported symbols, also no dedicated test file), not `nextEditRoute.ts`'s/`nextEditEgress.ts`'s (newly-exported symbols, each got one).

`shell.vscode.ts` replaces the four moved bodies with `import { makeExecutor, type NextEditContextKey, type NextEditExecutorHost, type NextEditExecutor } from './nextEditExecutor';` plus `export { makeExecutor }; export type { NextEditContextKey, NextEditExecutorHost, NextEditExecutor };` — a re-export per ADR-FSU-01's mechanics, so the shell's own export surface and `shell.vscode.test.ts`'s import of `makeExecutor`/`NextEditExecutorHost` from `./shell.vscode` (used at 14 call sites across that file) keep resolving through `./shell.vscode` with zero further edits. `NOTE_MESSAGES` (the `noteOnce` msgId → user-facing copy table) correctly STAYS in `shell.vscode.ts` — grep confirmed its only reader is the shell CLASS's own vscode-bound `note:` host-implementation property (`NOTE_MESSAGES[msgId] ?? …` then `vscode.window.showWarningMessage`), never the executor, which only ever forwards the raw `msgId` string to `host.note`. `makeExecutor` does not import `./fsm` either — it consumes already-reduced `NextEditEffect[]` batches, never calls `reduceNextEdit` itself.

**Now-unused shell imports dropped (verified before removal, `noUnusedLocals: true`):** `LineRange` and `NextEditEffect` — grepped across the whole file first; both names appeared ONLY inside the moved block (`LineRange` in `NextEditExecutorHost.reveal`'s parameter, `NextEditEffect` in the `NextEditExecutor.run` signature and `makeExecutor`'s internal `applyOne`/`run`), with zero other reference anywhere else in `shell.vscode.ts` (the one remaining call site, `this.executor.run(next.effects)` at the class's `dispatch()`, has its element type inferred from `reduceNextEdit`'s return, no explicit annotation needed). `AnchoredProposal`, `ApplyExpectation`, `EditableRegion` all stay imported — each has a genuine surviving use in the unmoved class body (`currentProposal(): AnchoredProposal | null`, `pendingApplyExpectation: ApplyExpectation | null` + its getter passed to `makeExecutor`, and the `applyEdit` host method's own `region: EditableRegion` local).
