## ADR-025-N — `relay` module extracted, F3-6/9 (task F3-6, WS-F3)

**Outcome (task F3-6, WS-F3 extraction #5, FI-06):** the fifth of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the FIM-activity relay — the fixed forwarding address that decouples `provider.ts` from the shell's swappable listener — moves VERBATIM (bodies + doc comments) from `shell.vscode.ts` (re-grounded by symbol on `8d152b8`, the F3-5 HEAD — line numbers matched the brief's estimates exactly, no drift) into a NEW leaf `src/autocomplete/nextedit/fimActivityRelay.ts` — `const NO_OP_FIM_ACTIVITY`, the module-level `let currentFimActivity` slot, and `export const fimActivityRelay`. The module-level slot is KEPT BY DESIGN (provider→shell decoupling; FI-26's future home, task F10-2) — this move only relocates it, unchanged.

This is the ONE WS-F3 task carrying real stateful logic (the attach/detach swap-guard identity check), so it also adds TWO NEW thin exported setters that did not exist before as separate symbols — `attachFimActivity(listener)` and `detachFimActivity(listener)` — whose bodies are LITERALLY the shell's own former assignment statements (`currentFimActivity = this.fimActivity;` at the attach-last site, and the guarded `if (currentFimActivity === this.fimActivity) { currentFimActivity = NO_OP_FIM_ACTIVITY; }` at dispose). The swap-guard `=== listener` identity check inside `detachFimActivity` is preserved EXACTLY — a stale registration (one already replaced by a newer `attachFimActivity` call) must not reset the slot back to the no-op, which would silently disarm R2 for the shell registration that is actually live.

`shell.vscode.ts` replaces the moved block with `import { attachFimActivity, detachFimActivity, fimActivityRelay } from './fimActivityRelay';` plus `export { fimActivityRelay };` — a re-export per ADR-FSU-01's mechanics, so the shell's own export surface, `provider.ts`'s/`index.ts`'s `import { fimActivityRelay, registerTalariaNextEdit, requestNextEditToggle } from './nextedit/shell.vscode'`, and both `shell.vscode.test.ts`'s and `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts`'s own `import { fimActivityRelay, ... } from './shell.vscode'` all keep resolving through `./shell.vscode` with zero further edits. The class's own `private readonly fimActivity: FimActivityListener` field and its construction are untouched — the shell still OWNS its listener; only the module-level slot + relay + the attach/detach mechanics moved. The two call sites are repointed: `currentFimActivity = this.fimActivity;` (attach-last, after `FIM_ACCEPT_COMMAND` registration) → `attachFimActivity(this.fimActivity);`; the dispose-path guarded reset → `detachFimActivity(this.fimActivity);`. `fimActivityRelay.accepted()` (the `FIM_ACCEPT_COMMAND` handler, unmoved) is unaffected — it still calls the same (now-imported) `fimActivityRelay` object.

**`fimActivityRelay.ts` imports:** `import type { FimActivityListener } from '../provider'` — type-only; `FimActivityListener` is a plain 4-callback shape (`requestStarted`/`resultShown`/`accepted`/`acceptCommandId`) with no `vscode.*` type in it, and `from '../provider'` does not trip `VSCODE_IMPORT_BAN` (which matches only the exact `'vscode'` specifier).

**Direct pin (the move's teeth), NEW `fimActivityRelay.test.ts`:** 5 tests against fake listeners (spies per method) prove: a fresh/detached module reaches the no-op (no throw, nothing forwarded); attach A forwards to A (including `acceptCommandId`'s return value); attach A then attach B swaps the relay onto B, not A; **attach A, attach B, detach A still forwards to B** — the stale registration does not reset the slot, the guard's whole point; attach B then detach B returns the relay to the no-op. F3-1's golden masters only observe this guard INDIRECTLY (through the shell's trigger/dispose paths); this is the first DIRECT pin.

**Golden (0 edits, the faithfulness net):** `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts` + `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts` + `shell.vscode.test.ts` — all THREE files show zero diff (`git status` confirms no test-file changes to any of them this commit) and all pass in the same full-gate run below.

**Purity:** `nextEditPurity.test.ts` green with `ADAPTER_ALLOW` UNCHANGED at exactly 4 (`config.ts`, `guard.ts`, `shell.vscode.ts`, `nextEditNotice.vscode.ts`) — `fimActivityRelay.ts` is vscode-FREE (no `import … from 'vscode'`, not even `import type`) and is correctly NOT added to that allowlist. `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` all green — the move introduces no new cast and no `{ ...x, }` spread.

**Mutation-proven (applied, run, reverted — restored to the exact prior text, confirmed byte-identical against a pre-mutation backup both times):** dropping the `=== listener` guard inside `detachFimActivity` (so it unconditionally resets the slot to the no-op) flips exactly ONE test RED — the new pin's "attach A, attach B, detach A: the relay STILL forwards to B" case (`expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 0 times`) — and no other; reverting restores GREEN, all 5 tests pass, and the file diffs byte-identical to the pre-mutation copy (both this task's own run and a second confirmation run after a test-file cleanup pass, below).

**Test-file cleanup during the task (no behaviour change):** the first draft of `fimActivityRelay.test.ts`'s "attach A" case used `(a.acceptCommandId as ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>).mockReturnValue('cmd-a')` to set A's return value AFTER construction — an `as` cast, banned by this task's own global constraints. Fixed by giving `fakeListener` an optional `commandId` constructor parameter instead (`fakeListener('cmd-a')`), so the mock's return value is fixed at construction and no cast is ever needed; the mutation proof above was re-run after this fix to confirm it still catches the same single case.

## ADR-025-O — shared failure classifier extracted, `failureSurface` F3-7/9 (task F3-7, WS-F3, HIGHEST-RISK non-frozen task)

**Outcome (task F3-7, WS-F3 extraction #6, FI-13 — a SHARED-CLASSIFIER refactor across TWO surfaces, not a pure move):** the sixth of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the two near-duplicate `instanceof` ladders — `nextedit/shell.vscode.ts`'s `surfaceTriggerFailure` and `provider.ts`'s `surfaceCompletionFailure` — now share ONE classification leaf. NEW `src/autocomplete/failureClass.ts` (deliberately at the autocomplete ROOT, sibling of both `nextedit/` and `backends/`, not inside either — neither surface may import the other's directory) exports `classifyBackendFailure(err: unknown): { statusClass: string; kind: BackendFailureKind }`, a pure, vscode-free AND fs-free function over the shared 9-member union FSU §5 Q11 settled: `'insecure-transport' | 'missing-key' | 'auth' | 'dialect' | 'model' | 'stream' | 'http' | 'mint' | 'unreachable'`. NEW `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditFailureSurface.ts` exports `describeTriggerFailure(err, route, mode): { key; message; channel: 'toast' | 'log' }` — the pure copy builder, every string LIFTED VERBATIM from the pre-task `surfaceTriggerFailure`. `shell.vscode.ts`'s `surfaceTriggerFailure` shrinks from a ~90-line ladder to a 9-line thin caller that keeps ONLY `surfaceOnce`/`surfacedFailures` (the toast + dedup side-effects a pure function cannot own). `provider.ts`'s `surfaceCompletionFailure` adopts the SAME classifier in the SAME commit, converting its `instanceof` ladder to a `switch` on `kind` while keeping its OWN copy-string table verbatim (FSU §5 Q11: `kind` is shared, `statusClass`/the dedup key stay per-surface — FIM's own `key(...)` still builds `status-${err.status}` for its catch-all, ignoring the classifier's `statusClass` field entirely).

**The one real design decision this task made (ground-truthed, not assumed):** the brief's own draft mapping named `BackendStreamError`, `StreamIdleTimeoutError` (R1-7), and `StreamByteCapError` together as "stream classes → `kind:'stream'`". Ground-truthing BOTH surfaces' CURRENT pinned behaviour before writing the classifier found this would have been a copy regression: `provider.test.ts` carries a dedicated characterization (`"a StreamIdleTimeoutError ... returns null silently — same treatment as any other timeout-class failure, no new toast"`) proving FIM deliberately stays silent on an idle-stream reap — only `BackendStreamError` (a mid-stream SSE error FRAME on an otherwise-200 response) gets FIM's toast today. Folding all three into one `kind:'stream'` bucket, with `provider.ts` switching on `kind` alone, would have made `StreamIdleTimeoutError`/`StreamByteCapError` toast where today nothing does — exactly the drift this task's own acceptance criteria forbid ("if a copy pin needs editing to pass, the copy DRIFTED — fix the code, never the pin"). `classifyBackendFailure` therefore maps `kind:'stream'` from `BackendStreamError` ONLY; `StreamIdleTimeoutError` and `StreamByteCapError` fall through (no explicit branch needed) to the generic `kind:'unreachable'` bucket — which is what BOTH surfaces already, structurally, did for them before this task (FIM: no matching arm, silent; next-edit: no stream arm at all, generic fallback toast). This is documented in `failureClass.ts`'s own module doc, not merely in this ADR, so a future reader does not "helpfully" re-widen the bucket.

**Ground-truth deviation from the brief (documented, per this WS's own convention):** the brief's stated import list for `nextEditFailureSurface.ts` did not name `BackendHttpError`. Re-grounding the moved copy showed three of its four HTTP-derived arms (`auth`/`dialect`/`http`; `model`'s copy hardcodes the literal `"(404)"`, needing no field) read `err.status`/`err.statusText` directly — fields `classifyBackendFailure` deliberately does not return (the classifier hands back `kind` only, never the original error's fields, per its own type signature). `nextEditFailureSurface.ts` therefore imports `BackendHttpError` from its DEFINING leaf (`../backends/http`) and re-narrows `err` via its own `instanceof` check inside each HTTP-derived `case`, exactly as `classifyBackendFailure` itself does internally — this is not a second classifier, only a field recovery the classifier's own return shape does not carry. `provider.ts` keeps its own pre-existing imports of `BackendHttpError`/`InsecureTransportError` for the identical reason, but no longer needs `BackendStreamError`/`MissingApiKeyError` at all: neither of ITS `stream`/`missing-key` arms reads any field off the original error object (both build their copy purely from `backend`/`host`), so those two imports were dropped as genuinely dead once the classifier took over the `instanceof` decision — caught by `tsc`'s `noUnusedLocals` on the first `check-types` run, fixed before the gate run below.

**Mint path preserved exactly (review focus (c)):** `classifyBackendFailure` maps `NextEditMintRejectionError` → `kind:'mint'`, `statusClass:'mint'`; `describeTriggerFailure`'s `'mint'` case re-narrows `err` via its own `instanceof NextEditMintRejectionError` (imported from `./scan`, its defining leaf) to recover `.ruleId`, building the SAME `key(\`mint|${err.ruleId}\`)` and the SAME ruleId-only, content-free log line as before. `channel: 'log'` — never `'toast'` — so the shell's thin caller routes it through `surfacedFailures`/`reportFailure` only, never `showWarningMessage`, exactly reproducing the pre-task toast-free, log-only, ruleId-keyed dedup. The pre-existing SEPARATE `err instanceof NextEditMintRejectionError` check at the trigger call site (`shell.vscode.ts`, the `notifyEgress(uri, 'content-block')` call) is untouched — it is a different concern (the CA-06-NE-face egress-verdict notification) with its own, already-imported `NextEditMintRejectionError`, unrelated to the copy-building this task moved.

**Import-cycle proof (critic M-10, mechanically confirmed, not merely argued):** `grep -rn "from ['\"].*failureClass['\"]" src` finds exactly three hits — `provider.ts`, `nextedit/nextEditFailureSurface.ts`, and `failureClass.test.ts` itself; no module `failureClass.ts` imports (`backends/http.ts`, `backends/secureTransport.ts`, `backends/CodestralFimBackend.ts`, `nextedit/scan.ts`) imports `failureClass.ts`, `provider.ts`, or `shell.vscode.ts` back (confirmed by reading each of the four leaves' own import lists). `classifyBackendFailure` calls `instanceof` on every imported class ONLY inside its own function body, at CALL time, never at module-eval time, so even a hypothetical future cycle could not corrupt an evaluation order here. `tsc` (0 errors, both `tsconfig.json`s) and the full gate below are the runtime proof.

**Proof (2 NEW direct-pin files, 29 tests total, mirroring `nextEditRoute.test.ts`'s/`nextEditEgress.test.ts`'s precedent for a newly-exported symbol):** `src/autocomplete/failureClass.test.ts` (14 tests) drives `classifyBackendFailure` directly over all 9 `kind`s from every real error class (`InsecureTransportError`, `MissingApiKeyError`, `NextEditMintRejectionError`, `BackendHttpError` at 404/401/403/400/500/501, `BackendStreamError`, `StreamIdleTimeoutError`, `StreamByteCapError`, a bare `Error`, and two non-`Error` thrown values) — ground-truthed against BOTH surfaces' pre-task ladders, one class at a time. `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditFailureSurface.test.ts` (15 tests) drives `describeTriggerFailure` directly, pinning the exact `{ key, message, channel }` shape for every arm incl. both `'next'`/`'generic'` mode's endpoint/model setting names, the mint arm's ruleId-keyed `channel:'log'`, and — the two facts the indirect golden cannot see through the shell's private dedup Set — that `MissingApiKeyError` and `BackendStreamError` (kinds next-edit never surfaced distinctly before this task) now reach `describeTriggerFailure` at all and produce a result `toEqual` the generic fallback's, not merely "whatever happened to come out". Needed one `vi.mock('./config', ...)` (mirroring `nextEditRoute.test.ts`'s own, for the identical reason: `nextEditFailureSurface.ts` imports `endpointLabel` from `./nextEditRoute`, which imports `readNextEditConfig` from `./config`, which imports `vscode` — this test file has no other need of `vscode` at all).

**Golden (0 edits, the faithfulness net — THE load-bearing proof for this task):** `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts` (55 tests, Part D's byte-exact failure-copy suite incl. the R1-7 `StreamIdleTimeoutError → unreachable` case) + `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts` + `shell.vscode.test.ts` + `provider.test.ts` (its own FUNC-PROVIDER characterization suite, T13/T14's exact catch-classification-string pins, incl. the dedicated `StreamIdleTimeoutError` silent-treatment test) — all FOUR files show zero diff (`git diff --stat` confirms no test-file changes to any of them this commit) and all pass in the same full-gate run below.

**Mutation-proven (review focus (f) — applied, run, reverted, `diff` against a pre-mutation backup confirmed byte-identical restore):** `classifyBackendFailure`'s `BackendHttpError` 404 branch mutated from `{ statusClass: 'model', kind: 'model' }` to `{ statusClass: 'auth', kind: 'auth' }` → BOTH faithfulness nets flipped RED in the SAME run: `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts`'s `'BackendHttpError 404 — names the model + the model setting'` (received the auth-rejection copy instead of the model-not-served copy) AND `provider.test.ts`'s `'404: exact model toast naming getModelName()'` PLUS its dedup-key-shape test (which expected two independent toasts for two different statusClasses and got only one, since 404 now collided with the 401/403 case's `'auth'` key) — 3 test files failed, 7 individual test failures, none of them a test this task itself added (the direct-pin files also flipped, confirming they are exercising the real shared function, not a stand-in). This is the proof review focus (f) asks for: the shared classification path genuinely couples both surfaces' output — a regression in the ONE shared function is caught from BOTH directions, not just one. Restored via a saved pre-mutation copy; `diff` against it showed zero drift.

**reuseLocks / purity / cast-and-spread locks (review focus (d)/(g)):** `reuseLocks.test.ts`'s `files.length` re-pinned **20 → 21**, with `nextEditFailureSurface.ts` added to the named list (non-vacuously verified fetch-free) — `failureClass.ts` lives OUTSIDE `nextedit/` (at the autocomplete root) and so is correctly NOT counted by this `nextedit/`-scoped lock, exactly as the brief specified. `nextEditPurity.test.ts` green with `ADAPTER_ALLOW` UNCHANGED at exactly 4 (`config.ts`, `guard.ts`, `shell.vscode.ts`, `nextEditNotice.vscode.ts`) — `nextEditFailureSurface.ts` is vscode-free (no `import … from 'vscode'`, not even `import type`) and correctly NOT in it. `failureClass.ts` itself has no mechanical root-level purity scan (`src/autocomplete/`'s own `*Purity.test.ts` does not exist — only `context/` and `nextedit/` have one, each scoped to its own subdirectory) — verified BY HAND instead: `grep -nE "from ['\"]vscode['\"]|from ['\"]node:fs['\"]" failureClass.ts nextEditFailureSurface.ts` returns nothing. `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` (repo-wide over `src/autocomplete/`, so it DOES reach the new root-level `failureClass.ts`) all green — no `as ScannedSnippet`/`as ScannedNextEditRequest` cast and no `{ ...x, }` spread appears in either new production file. No `fetch(` text in either new production file (`grep 'fetch(' failureClass.ts nextEditFailureSurface.ts` → empty).

After this commit: **7626 passed | 15 skipped (7641), 346 test files, tsc 0** (both `tsconfig.json`s, host + webview) — RISES by exactly the 29 new direct-pin tests (14 in `failureClass.test.ts`, 15 in `nextEditFailureSurface.test.ts`) across 2 new test files, zero removed, zero regressions.

## ADR-025-P — `wiring` module extracted, F3-8/9 (task F3-8, WS-F3 — LAST real extraction, FI-07)

**Outcome (task F3-8, WS-F3 extraction #7, FI-07):** the seventh and LAST leaf of the `text/route/egress/executor/relay/failureSurface/wiring` module map (ADR-FSU-08, recorded at the end of ADR-025-I) lands: the ctor's three wiring blocks move VERBATIM (bodies + doc comments) from `shell.vscode.ts` (re-grounded by symbol on `ff7a211`, the F3-7 HEAD — line numbers matched the brief's estimates exactly, no drift) into a NEW leaf `src/autocomplete/nextedit/nextEditShellWiring.ts` — `buildFimActivity` (the former `this.fimActivity = {...}` object literal), `registerListeners` (the `onDidChangeTextDocument`/`onDidChangeActiveTextEditor`/`onDidChangeWindowState` subscriptions — ground-truthing the ctor found a THIRD listener, the window-state one, sitting inside the same `// ── listeners ──` section the brief's two named subscriptions bracket; it closes over nothing the other two don't already expose, so it moved with them), and `registerCommands` (the four `vscode.commands.registerCommand` calls, including `FIM_ACCEPT_COMMAND`'s registration). `FIM_ACCEPT_COMMAND` itself moved with its two use sites rather than being re-exported, since nothing else in `shell.vscode.ts` ever named it.

**UNLIKE every other WS-F3 leaf, this one IS a `vscode` adapter** — it genuinely wires vscode events/commands to the shell's FSM (`vscode.workspace.onDidChangeTextDocument`, `vscode.window.onDidChangeActiveTextEditor`/`onDidChangeWindowState`, `vscode.commands.registerCommand`), so it is ADDED to `nextEditPurity.test.ts`'s `ADAPTER_ALLOW` (4 → 5 files: `config.ts`, `guard.ts`, `shell.vscode.ts`, `nextEditNotice.vscode.ts`, `nextEditShellWiring.ts`) — the "exactly 4" assertions re-pinned to "exactly 5", and the non-vacuity check ("all N sanctioned files DO import vscode") now also verifies `nextEditShellWiring.ts` for real, since it iterates `ADAPTER_ALLOW` rather than a fixed list.

**`ShellHostSeams` — the narrow port, derived by reading exactly what the three blocks close over, not the whole class:** `fim: { visible: boolean; inFlightCount: number }` (the SAME mutable object the shell holds — reference-shared, no accessor needed, since mutating `seams.fim.x` mutates the shell's own field directly), `trackedVersion: number | null` (a get/set ACCESSOR PROPERTY bridging to the shell's own private primitive field — chosen specifically so every moved block's `this.trackedVersion` becomes `seams.trackedVersion`, a literal rename, rather than reshaping a field read/write into two named methods), and four methods — `dispatch`, `armTrigger`, `abortInFlight`, `currentProposal` — each called by one or more of the three blocks exactly as they called `this.X` before. Six members total; nothing wider.

**Passed as an EXPLICIT seam object, never `this` itself:** `NextEditShell`'s `fim`/`dispatch`/`armTrigger`/`abortInFlight`/`currentProposal` are all `private`, and `npx tsc --strict` confirms passing `this` structurally into a public interface that names a private member does NOT type-check (`Property 'x' is private in type 'Foo' but not in type 'Bar'` — verified with a standalone repro before committing to this design, not assumed). The ctor instead builds one `hostSeams: ShellHostSeams` object literal and passes it to all three builders; a local `const shell = this;` backs the `trackedVersion` accessor pair, because a `get`/`set` accessor shorthand's own `this` binds to the object literal it lives on, not the enclosing constructor's `this` — the surrounding arrow-function members (`dispatch`/`armTrigger`/`abortInFlight`/`currentProposal`) don't need this, since arrow functions inherit the constructor's lexical `this` directly.

**Ctor after the split, ORDER preserved exactly (review focus (d)):** decoration types → `this.executorHost = {...}` (unmoved, in place) → `this.executor = makeExecutor(...)` (unmoved) → `hostSeams` built → `this.fimActivity = buildFimActivity(hostSeams)` → `registerListeners(hostSeams)` → `registerCommands(hostSeams)` → `attachFimActivity(this.fimActivity)` (unmoved, still attach-LAST) → `this.disposable = vscode.Disposable.from(...listenerDisposables, ...commandDisposables, guardToggleSubscription, this.regionDecoration, this.locatorDecoration, { dispose: ... })` — the SAME sequence the composite always held, now assembled via two spread arrays instead of seven named locals. `...listenerDisposables`/`...commandDisposables` are ARRAY spreads inside a function-call argument list, not the object-literal `{ ...x, }` shape `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `SPREAD_RE` bans — confirmed the regex (`/\{\s*\.\.\.[A-Za-z_$][\w$.]*\s*,/`) requires an immediately-preceding `{`, which neither spread has.

**STAYED in `shell.vscode.ts` exactly as the brief required (critic I-2, LOAD-BEARING — review focus (a)):** the two `createTextEditorDecorationType` calls, the entire `this.executorHost` object including the paint/`showDecorations` block with the `contentText` template and the verb line, `this.executor = makeExecutor(...)`, `attachFimActivity(this.fimActivity)`, and the `vscode.Disposable.from(...)` composite itself. `docTopology.lock.test.ts` — which reads the `contentText: \`...\`` template (LOCK 1) and the verb-line string literal straight off `SHELL_SRC` (the shell file's own source) — is confirmed 0-diff (`git status --porcelain` on the file shows nothing) and green in the same full-gate run below.

**Ground-truth deviation from the brief, discovered and fixed during the TDD loop (documented, per this WS's own convention):** `shell.vscode.test.ts`'s pre-existing `'no other non-test file under src/ writes talaria.nextEdit.* context keys'` lock went RED on the first narrow-suite run. Its mechanism is a COINCIDENTAL two-condition proxy — `SET_CONTEXT_WRITE_RE.test(file) && /['"\`]talaria\.nextEdit\./.test(file)` on the SAME file's stripped content — that never actually checked whether the matched `talaria.nextEdit.*` string was the key passed to the matched `setContext` write; it only required both substrings to occur somewhere in one file. Before this task, `shell.vscode.ts` satisfied both halves purely because the ctor's `registerCommand('talaria.nextEdit.jump', ...)`-style command-id literals happened to live in the SAME file as the executorHost's `setContext` write — an accident of co-location, not a property of context-key writing. This task moves those command-id literals (verbatim, with their `registerCommand` calls) into `nextEditShellWiring.ts`, so no single file matches both halves of the proxy any more; the mechanically correct result is now `[]`, not `['autocomplete/nextedit/shell.vscode.ts']`. Fixed with a 1-line assertion change plus an explanatory comment (the ONLY edit to `shell.vscode.test.ts` this task makes, out of 3899 lines and 680 tests — every other test in the file, including the 4 nearby in the same `describe` block, is unaffected and unedited). The REAL property the lock protects — `shell.vscode.ts`'s `executorHost.setContext` is the ONLY call to `executeCommand('setContext', ...)` for a next-edit context key anywhere under `src/` — still holds and is structurally unaffected: `nextEditExecutor.ts` (defines the `NextEditContextKey` string-literal union) calls no `executeCommand` at all, and `nextEditShellWiring.ts` calls `registerCommand`, never `setContext`.

**Golden (0 edits, the faithfulness net):** `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts` + `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts` (BOTH spy-ORDER banks — the happy-path trigger sequence and the FIM-interrupt "Stop" sequence) — zero diff (`git status --porcelain` on both files shows nothing) and green in the full-gate run below. `shell.vscode.test.ts` carries the ONE deviation documented above; all other 679 of its 680 tests are unedited and pass unchanged.

**Purity:** `nextEditPurity.test.ts` green with `ADAPTER_ALLOW` re-pinned 4 → 5 (`nextEditShellWiring.ts` added, non-vacuously verified to import `vscode` for real). `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` all green — the move introduces no new brand cast and no object-literal spread-with-override. No `fetch(` text anywhere in `nextEditShellWiring.ts` (`grep 'fetch(' nextEditShellWiring.ts` → empty).

**Mutation-proven (review focus (g) — applied, run, reverted, `diff` against a pre-mutation backup confirmed byte-identical restore):** inside `buildFimActivity`'s `requestStarted`, swapped `seams.abortInFlight()` to run AFTER `seams.dispatch({ kind: 'fimVisibility', visible: true })` instead of before → F3-1's golden shell test's Part F ("Stop") spy-ORDER bank flipped RED exactly as expected: the observed sequence became `[setContext:jumpVisible=false, setContext:jumped=false, backend.signal.aborted]` instead of the pinned `[backend.signal.aborted, setContext:jumpVisible=false, setContext:jumped=false]` — 1 test file failed, 1 test failure, confirming the golden is genuinely sensitive to the exact internal order this move carried across intact. Restored via a saved pre-mutation copy; `diff` against it showed zero drift (exit code 0), and the full narrow suite (680 tests, 25 files) re-ran green immediately after.

## ADR-025-R — `coexistence.lock.test.ts` `ABORT_RECEIVER` narrowed to `['this.inFlight']` (task F9-1, WS-F9 KICKOFF, FI-21 site 1, `[SEC]`)

**Outcome (task F9-1, WS-F9 — lock-test discipline, task 1 of 4, OD-I):** `NextEditShell.abortInFlight()` (`shell.vscode.ts`) loses its alias contortion, and the `coexistence.lock.test.ts` `ABORT_RECEIVER` lock (WS-F3's HALF 2 source-scan) is re-pinned to the natural, MORE SPECIFIC receiver it should have named from the start.

1. **`abortInFlight()` de-aliased.** Before this task, the method read `this.inFlight` into a LOCAL first — `const inFlight = this.inFlight; if (inFlight !== null) { inFlight.abort(); this.inFlight = null; }` — for one reason only: the `ABORT_RECEIVER` scan's allowed set was `['inFlight']` (the bare trailing identifier), and `this.inFlight.abort()` would have scanned as the DIFFERENT string `this.inFlight`, tripping the lock. The method now reads the natural `this.inFlight?.abort(); this.inFlight = null;` — behaviour-identical (optional-chain aborts iff non-null; the unconditional `= null` is a no-op when already null; confirmed empirically — see the mutation note below, removing the `?` throws on a null in-flight controller at real call sites, so the optional chain is load-bearing, not decorative). The alias-contortion comment (the "Read into a local first…" block) is deleted — it documented a workaround that no longer exists.
2. **The lock's allowed set moves to `['this.inFlight']`.** Critic I-19: `['this.inFlight']` is strictly STRONGER than `['inFlight']` — a local alias `const inFlight = someFimController; inFlight.abort()` would have PASSED the old set (bare-`inFlight` receiver, indistinguishable from the shell's own legitimate field) but FAILS the new one. The `ABORT_RECEIVER` regex itself (the full-chain-plus-optional-`?` capture, its own two-blind-spot doc block) is BYTE-IDENTICAL — only the allowed-set literal moved.
3. **Two RED probes prove the strengthening has teeth.** A NEW test plants `const inFlight = fim.inFlight; inFlight.abort()` in-memory (mirroring the file's existing planted-violation idiom): the regex captures the bare local `inFlight` at the call site — under the OLD `['inFlight']` set this would have been indistinguishable from the shell's own former bare receiver and WRONGLY ALLOWED; under the new `['this.inFlight']` set it is flagged (`toContain('inFlight')`, `not.toEqual(['this.inFlight'])`). The pre-existing `deps.getFimController?.()?.abort()` and `fimController.abort()` probes needed no code change — their `not.toEqual([...])` assertions already held true against any allowed-set literal (a 2-receiver array is never equal to either 1-element array) — both still flag, confirmed by the full suite run. The REGRESSION control (previously: collapsing the old `if`-guarded body to `inFlight?.abort()` still resolves to the same bare receiver) is re-cut for the new baseline: reverting the shell's now-permanent `this.inFlight?.abort()` to a direct member call `this.inFlight.abort()` (no `?`) must still resolve to the SAME receiver, `this.inFlight` — receiver IDENTITY is invariant to the `?.` operator's presence. This is also the exact mutation the `[SEC]` lens re-runs (in-memory string substitution only — the mutated text is never executed as code, since actually removing the `?` from the live `abortInFlight()` throws on a null in-flight controller, proven by a manual apply-run-revert during this task).
4. **Mutation evidence (manual, applied to the live file then reverted byte-exact):** (a) removing the `?` from the real `abortInFlight()` body (`this.inFlight.abort()`) and running `npx vitest run src/autocomplete/nextedit/coexistence.lock.test.ts` throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'abort')` at multiple real call sites (`runPrediction`/`trigger`) — the optional chain is BEHAVIOURALLY necessary, not merely lock-satisfying cosmetics; reverted, full `nextedit/` suite (682 tests) green afterward. (b) the new bare-alias probe, run against the fixed code, flags as designed (see point 3). No `.abort()` call in `shell.vscode.ts` other than `this.inFlight?.abort()` exists (verified: `this.abortInFlight()` at its two call sites is a METHOD call, not `.abort(`).

**Locks confirmed unchanged (same full-gate run below, 0 failures):** `reuseLocks.test.ts` (`files.length` stays **22** — no new module); `nextEditPurity.test.ts` (`ADAPTER_ALLOW` stays **5**); `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE`; `docTopology.lock.test.ts`; `diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts`; `nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts`/`nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts` (both spy-ORDER banks, 0-diff — this task touches no golden). HALF 1's `fimActivityRelay` method-set assertion (`coexistence.lock.test.ts`) is untouched — byte-identical.

**Byte-scan:** the two touched files (`shell.vscode.ts`, `coexistence.lock.test.ts`) — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

After this commit: **7628 passed | 15 skipped (7643), 346 test files, tsc 0** (both `tsconfig.json`s, host + webview) — +1 test (the new bare-alias RED probe), 0 test files added, confirmed by one fresh full `npm run gate` run. ## ADR-025-S — `codeActionSerialize.ts` regains its natural names, the tool-name ban already scoped (task F9-2, WS-F9, FI-21 site 2)

**Outcome (task F9-2, WS-F9 — lock-test discipline, task 2 of 4):** `codeActionSerialize.ts` carried two production contortions with "apology" comments, both dodging the OLD blunt form of the `lspInvariant.test.ts` tool-name ban. That ban was tightened to REGISTRATION-SCOPED at W3 T8a (`TOOL_NAME_DECL_PATTERN` requires `registerTool(`/`name:`/`id:`/`toolName:` immediately before the quoted literal; the write-verb test then runs only on the CAPTURED name, anchored — never on an arbitrary substring elsewhere in the file). Re-verified live on this task's HEAD (`lspInvariant.test.ts:217-278`, plus the positive control at `id: 'lsp_apply_edit'`/:840-844 and the M-2 negative control planting `case 'delete':`/`'edit'` union members outside any registration context/:865-878, both still passing) before touching anything — both contortions are stale.

1. **`WireEdit` → the natural indexed-access type.** The standalone `interface WireEdit { startLine; startChar; endLine; endChar; newText }` (kept in sync with `SerializedCodeAction['edits']`'s element shape "by comment only") is replaced with `type WireEdit = NonNullable<SerializedCodeAction['edits']>[number];` — structurally IDENTICAL to the old interface by construction (verified independently in isolation with `tsc --strict`), and can never drift from `SerializedCodeAction.edits` again. `NonNullable` is required, not decorative: `edits` is an optional property, and TypeScript's indexed-access distributes over the union `readonly {...}[] | undefined` — `Foo['edits'][number]` alone does not compile (`TS2537: … has no matching index signature for type 'number'`, confirmed by direct isolated repro), so `NonNullable<...>` strips the `undefined` member first. The apology comment ("written as a standalone interface … to avoid a quoted field-name string literal … would also collide with the tool-name-literal ban") is deleted — the quoted `'edits'` here is a TYPE-LEVEL indexed-access key, never in a `registerTool(`/`name:`/`id:`/`toolName:` context, so it was never actually at risk even under the old blunt scan's neighboring logic, and is doubly not at risk under the now-scoped one.
2. **`DiffOpKind` apology removed; labels kept (already read naturally).** The apology comment ("Kind labels are deliberately NOT the more obvious verb-shaped trio … sidesteps that collision [with the OLD blunt ban]") is deleted and replaced with a plain, factual doc comment describing what `'kept' | 'added' | 'dropped'` mean and that `DIFF_LINE_PREFIX` is the only place the label reaches the rendered output. Per the brief's explicit option ("if the current labels already read naturally, keeping them + only removing the apology is acceptable"): `kept`/`added`/`dropped` is a clear, parallel, past-tense trio for a diff line's per-line classification and needed no renaming — so `DiffOpKind`'s union, `DiffOp.type`, `DIFF_LINE_PREFIX`'s keys, and every `op.type === '…'` comparison in `buildHunks` are UNTOUCHED (0-diff on all of those sites); only the two comment blocks changed.
3. **No lock change.** `lspInvariant.test.ts` is byte-identical (0-diff) — confirmed by `git diff` showing only `codeActionSerialize.ts` touched. Its registration-scoped positive control (write-shaped name in a `name:`/`id:`/`toolName:`/`registerTool(` context still flags) and its M-2 negative control (a bare write-verb-root data literal outside any registration context stays green) both still pass, exercised by this task's own `npx vitest run src/mcp/lsp/` run.

**Rendered output byte-identical (proof).** No `DiffOpKind` label, no `DIFF_LINE_PREFIX` key/value, and no `op.type` comparison changed — `tools.test.ts`'s framed-output goldens are 0-EDIT (untouched by this task) and green in the same run. `WireEdit`'s new indexed-access definition is a pure type-level change with zero runtime code difference; `classifyCodeAction`'s `wireEdits` construction (the `sortedEdits.map(...)` object-literal shape) is unchanged.

**Frozen check.** `tools.ts` (the 3 `Object.freeze` constants `ZERO_RANGE`/`UNCLASSIFIED_TAIL_VERDICT`/`DIAGNOSTIC_SEVERITY_LABEL` + the LSP allowlist tables) is a DIFFERENT file from `codeActionSerialize.ts` — not touched, 0-diff, confirmed by `git status`/`git diff` showing exactly one file changed.

## ADR-025-T — `contextService.ts` sanctioned as an unbranded-input spread; `SPREAD_RE` unchanged (task F9-3, WS-F9, FI-21 site 3, `[SEC]`)

**Outcome (task F9-3, WS-F9 — lock-test discipline, task 3 of 4, OD-I):** `CrossFileContextService.runGatherCycle()` (`contextService.ts:461-476`) carried a field-by-field literal + a conditional post-assign for `score`, both existing ONLY to dodge `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `SPREAD_RE` source-scan lock (its own comment: "a spread here would be a false positive for THAT lock's actual concern"). The finding: let the natural spread win and close the site the lock's OWN way — its allowlist — because `candidate` here is an UNBRANDED, pre-scan `SnippetCandidate` (`context/types.ts`), never a branded `ScannedSnippet`, so a spread of it can never smuggle unscanned content under the brand SPREAD_RE actually guards against.

1. **`SPREAD_RE` is byte-identical — NOT narrowed (critic C-2).** `ScannedSnippet` has no `path` field, so a `\bpath\s*:` (or any field-name) narrowing would have let `{ ...scanned, filepath: evil }` / `{ ...scanned, uri: evil }` / `{ ...scanned, content }` (shorthand) straight through; today's blunt regex (`/\{\s*\.\.\.[A-Za-z_$][\w$.]*\s*,/`, `ringBuffer.test.ts:559`) still flags all of them by construction (it doesn't look at field names at all), and its own doc block says the bluntness is deliberate, with false positives handled by `SANCTIONED_SPREAD_FILES` + a line-by-line justification. `git diff` on this task's HEAD shows the regex line untouched.
2. **`contextService.ts` sanctioned, not exempted from scrutiny.** Added to `SANCTIONED_SPREAD_FILES` (now 5 entries) with an inline justification: *"spreads `IngestCandidate` — PRE-scan, UNBRANDED (`context/types.ts`) — a false positive for `SPREAD_RE`'s ScannedSnippet-provenance concern by construction; the only overridden field is `anchor` (a request-scoped value, not content)."* `SANCTIONED_FILES` (the sibling `CAST_RE` allowlist, a different guard) is untouched.
3. **The construction itself: natural spread, exactOptional-safe, grounded not assumed.** `SnippetCandidate.score` is declared `score?: number` (absent-or-number form, `types.ts:26`), NOT `score: number | undefined` (present-possibly-undefined form) — ground-truthed empirically before writing the fix: an isolated `tsc --strict --exactOptionalPropertyTypes --noEmit` repro of the exact shape (`{ ...candidate, anchor: requestAnchor }: IngestCandidate` where `candidate: SnippetCandidate` has `score?: number`) compiles with **zero errors** — TypeScript's spread-type inference keeps an optional source property optional on the result, it does not widen it to include an explicit `undefined` member. The brief's conditional destructure fallback (`const { score, ...rest } = candidate; …`) was therefore NOT needed; the real project `tsc --noEmit` (host tsconfig, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true`) confirms 0 errors on the actual file. The field-by-field literal, its "Field-by-field (not an object spread) on purpose" comment (`:454-460`), and the `if (candidate.score !== undefined) { ingestCandidate.score = candidate.score; }` post-assign + its explanation comment (`:470-476`) are all deleted, replaced by `const ingestCandidate: IngestCandidate = { ...candidate, anchor: requestAnchor };`.
4. **Behaviour-parity, exact.** `candidate: SnippetCandidate` has exactly the 7 keys `uri`/`filepath`/`content`/`kind`/`startLine`/`endLine`/`score?` — none extra, none missing versus `IngestCandidate`'s own field set (`IngestCandidate extends SnippetCandidate` adds only `anchor`). The spread copies every present key verbatim and the `anchor: requestAnchor` override supplies the one field `candidate` never carries; `score` is included in the result if and only if it was present on the runtime `candidate` object, matching the old post-assign's `!== undefined` gate exactly (an absent `score` key spreads as absent, not as an explicit `undefined`). The `ringBuffer.ingest(ingestCandidate, liveAnchor.uri, liveAnchor)` call site (`:477`) is unchanged.
5. **RED probes extended, mechanism re-verified.** The existing sanity-check test (`ringBuffer.test.ts` "the guard regex actually catches a hypothetical unsafe spread") already covered `{ ...scanned, content: attackerControlledBytes }` (flags) and the bare clone `{ ...scanned }` (passes); this task adds the three probes critic C-2 named as the narrowing risk: `{ ...scanned, filepath: evilPath }` (flags), `{ ...scanned, uri: evilUri }` (flags), and the shorthand form `{ ...scanned, content }` (flags — `SPREAD_RE` only requires a spread followed by a comma-terminated identifier, it never inspects what follows the comma, so shorthand and `key: value` forms are indistinguishable to it by design). All five probes pass against the byte-identical `SPREAD_RE`, proving the sanction did not blunt the lock.
6. **`contextService.test.ts` is 0-EDIT.** Confirmed by `git diff` (only `contextService.ts` and `ringBuffer.test.ts` touched) and by the file's own suite (part of the 13-file/311-test `src/autocomplete/context/` run below) passing unchanged — its tests exercise the real `RingBuffer` end-to-end via the public `handle*` API and assert on the resulting `SnippetSnapshot`, never on the internal `ingestCandidate` literal's shape, so the construction rewrite is invisible to them by design.

**Locks confirmed unchanged (same full-gate run below, 0 failures):** `reuseLocks.test.ts` (`files.length` stays **22** — `contextService.ts` is a `context/` file, not a `nextedit/` reuse module, so it was never a candidate for that list); `nextEditPurity.test.ts` (`ADAPTER_ALLOW` stays **5**); `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` and their own `SANCTIONED_FILES`/`NEXT_SANCTIONED_FILES` sets (untouched — only `SPREAD_RE`'s sibling `SANCTIONED_SPREAD_FILES` gained one entry); `coexistence.lock.test.ts`; `docTopology.lock.test.ts`; `diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts`.

**Byte-scan:** the two touched files (`contextService.ts`, `ringBuffer.test.ts`) — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**Count discipline:** F9-2 HEAD baseline (ADR-025-S) = **7628 passed | 15 skipped (7643), 346 test files, tsc 0**. After this commit: **unchanged — 7628 passed | 15 skipped (7643), 346 test files, tsc 0** (both `tsconfig.json`s, host + webview) — the three new probes were added as assertions inside the existing sanity-check `it` block (not new `it`s), so the assertion count rose with no test-count delta; confirmed by one fresh full `npm run gate` run. Known pre-existing `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` order-flake did not recur this run (all 346 files green). During TDD, `npx vitest run src/autocomplete/context/` ran green throughout (13 files, 311 tests, 0 type errors) before the one full-gate run at the end.

FI-21 site 3 CLOSED — `contextService.ts`'s field-by-field contortion and both apology comments are gone, replaced by the natural exactOptional-safe spread; `SPREAD_RE` itself is byte-identical and its allowlist mechanism (not a regex change) is what absorbed the false positive, per the lock's own documented design. Remaining WS-F9 task (F9-4) continues the same policy against the last FI-21/FI-22 site named in OD-I.

## ADR-025-U — `endpoints.ts` pure leaf ends the FI-22 `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` triplication; lock policy + LOCK INDEX (task F9-4, WS-F9 CLOSED, FI-22, OD-I)

**Outcome (task F9-4, WS-F9 — lock-test discipline, task 4 of 4, OD-I):** the last FI-21/FI-22 site named in OD-I closes. `config.ts`'s private `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` was restated as literals in TWO other files (`nextedit/nextEditRoute.ts`'s `DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS`, `backendFactory.ts`'s two port/endpoint constants) *because* `config.ts` is the `vscode` adapter (`import * as vscode from 'vscode'` :1) and importing from it would have dragged the two otherwise-pure files onto `vscode` (critic I-5) — a real reason, but the FI-22 anti-pattern OD-I retires: a duplicated literal, not an exported constant. A new pure leaf, `src/autocomplete/endpoints.ts`, now owns the five rows; all three sites import it.

1. **New pure leaf, `src/autocomplete/endpoints.ts`.** `export const DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS: Readonly<Record<FimBackendName, string>> = Object.freeze({ … })`, the five rows copied byte-for-byte from `config.ts`'s former `:43-49` (`ollama`/`llamacpp`/`vllm`/`codestral`/`openai-compat`). The explicit `Readonly<Record<FimBackendName, string>>` annotation is used (not `as const`), per the brief's own reasoning: functionally equivalent, but it reads unambiguously as a type annotation rather than a cast to a reviewer scanning for `as`. Zero imports beyond `type { FimBackendName }` (type-only, erased) — no `vscode`, no `node:fs`.
2. **`config.ts`** deletes its local `const DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS: Record<FimBackendName, string> = {…}` and imports `{ DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS } from './endpoints'`. `DEFAULT_MODEL` (`:51`, now the line directly above `isFimBackendName`) is **untouched, still declared in `config.ts`** — `docTopology.lock.test.ts:240`, `modelCatalog.test.ts:246-247` and `registry.test.ts:219-222` (a.k.a. "registry (f)") all pin the literal `const DEFAULT_MODEL = '…'` there by reading `config.ts`'s raw source text, and none of the three needed to move. `readConfig()`'s one usage site (`DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS[backend]`) is unchanged, because the imported binding has the identical name and shape as the deleted local one.
3. **`nextedit/nextEditRoute.ts`** deletes its restated `export const DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS: Readonly<Record<NextEditTransportId, string>> = Object.freeze({ ollama: …, 'openai-compat': … })` (verified byte-identical to `config.ts`'s corresponding two rows before deletion) and its doc comment ("mirror `config.ts`'s own `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` rows … restated rather than reached into"), and imports `{ DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS } from '../endpoints'`. The one call site, `resolveRoute`'s `DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS[cfg.backend]`, becomes `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS[cfg.backend]` — `cfg.backend: NextEditTransportId` (`'ollama' | 'openai-compat'`) indexes cleanly into `Record<FimBackendName, string>` because `NextEditTransportId`'s members are a subset of `FimBackendName`'s; `tsc` confirms no widening/narrowing issue. `nextEditRoute.ts` stays vscode-free (its one other `vscode`-adjacent import, `NextEditShellDeps` from `./shell.vscode`, is already `import type`-only, erased at compile time — unchanged by this task).
4. **`backendFactory.ts`** GROUND-TRUTHED first: it restated a PORT (`VLLM_DEFAULT_PORT = '8000'`) plus a full URL (`OPENAI_COMPAT_DEFAULT_ENDPOINT = 'http://127.0.0.1:8000'`), not two full URLs. Repointed to derive both from the imported leaf: `const VLLM_DEFAULT_PORT = new URL(DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS.vllm).port;` (WHATWG `URL` parsing of `'http://127.0.0.1:8000'` yields `port === '8000'` — confirmed by direct `node -e` repro — byte-identical to the deleted literal) and `const OPENAI_COMPAT_DEFAULT_ENDPOINT = DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS['openai-compat'];` (a direct reference — the leaf already holds the full URL, no parsing needed). Both "so this file's dependency surface stays exactly what it was" apology comments are deleted and replaced with comments naming the new single source of truth. The F6 vLLM-port heuristic (`endpointPort(cfg.endpoint) === VLLM_DEFAULT_PORT`) and the CA-8 self-default suppression (`sameEndpoint(cfg.endpoint, OPENAI_COMPAT_DEFAULT_ENDPOINT)`) are behaviourally IDENTICAL — same string values flow into the same comparisons — confirmed by the full `backendFactory.test.ts` suite (F1/F4/F6/C-1/C-4 blocks, including both `openai-compat` self-default and cross-port warning tests) passing unchanged.
5. **`registry.test.ts` "(c)" reverse-drift-lock re-pointed — the one file outside the brief's original list that had to move.** GROUND-TRUTHING surfaced a coupling the brief's touch-list did not anticipate: `registry.test.ts`'s "(c) FIM endpoint defaults" suite carried a REVERSE drift-lock, `expect(readFileSync(CONFIG_TS_PATH, 'utf-8')).toContain("ollama: 'http://127.0.0.1:11434'")` (×5), asserting `config.ts`'s raw on-disk text still held the five pairs. Deleting `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` from `config.ts` would have turned this pre-existing lock permanently, correctly red — not a regression this task introduced, but a real coupling that going green REQUIRES resolving, because "gate green, count not dropped" is binding. The fix is the drift-lock's own stated remedy for exactly this shape (OD-I policy point 1: "duplicates guarded by drift-locks are consolidated … and the lock deleted" — here, RE-POINTED, since the registry-vs-literal comparison itself is still load-bearing, only its second leg's file moved): `ENDPOINTS_TS_PATH` now names `src/autocomplete/endpoints.ts`, the reverse-lock reads that file instead, and the describe/it titles + module doc are updated to say so. The lock is not weakened — it is the SAME two-way byte-comparison, now pointed at the true source. `registry.test.ts` "(f)" (the sibling `DEFAULT_MODEL` reverse-lock, which reads `CONFIG_TS_PATH`) and `modelCatalog.test.ts`'s own `config.ts`-source-text check needed NO change — neither reads any `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` text, confirmed by grep before editing either file.
6. **`nextEditRoute.test.ts` repointed — the other file the deletion required touching.** It imported `DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS` directly from `./nextEditRoute` for its own two whole-shape route-resolution assertions (`apiBase: DEFAULT_NEXT_EDIT_ENDPOINTS.ollama` / `['openai-compat']`); with the export gone this would not compile. Repointed to `import { DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS } from '../endpoints'` and the two usage sites — same values, same assertions, 0 behaviour change to the test's own expectations.

**Purity, confirmed non-vacuously.** `endpoints.ts` sits directly under `src/autocomplete/`, so `policyAcpPurity.test.ts`'s T-18 "autocomplete/ ROOT purity guard" walk (non-recursive, `AUTOCOMPLETE_ADAPTER_ALLOW` = 4: `index.ts`/`provider.ts`/`config.ts`/`egressNotice.vscode.ts`) discovers it automatically on every run and asserts it imports neither `vscode` nor `node:fs` — it passes WITHOUT being added to the allowlist, because it genuinely never imports either (re-verified: the file's only import is `import type { FimBackendName } from './types'`). This is the whole point (critic I-5): `nextEditRoute.ts` and `backendFactory.ts` now import a leaf that is provably off `vscode`, instead of importing `config.ts` (which is ON `vscode`) or restating literals to dodge that coupling.

**Locks confirmed unchanged (full-gate run below, 0 failures beyond the one intentionally re-pointed lock):** `reuseLocks.test.ts` (`files.length` stays **22** — `endpoints.ts` is under `src/autocomplete/`, not `nextedit/`, so it was never a candidate for that list); `nextEditPurity.test.ts` (`ADAPTER_ALLOW` stays **5** — unaffected, `endpoints.ts` is outside `nextedit/` entirely); `ringBuffer.test.ts`'s `CAST_RE`/`SPREAD_RE`/`NEXT_CAST_RE` and their sanctioned-file sets; `coexistence.lock.test.ts`; `docTopology.lock.test.ts` (LOCK 2's `DEFAULT_MODEL` triple-agreement untouched — `config.ts` still declares it); `diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts`; `configScope.test.ts`; `modelCatalog.test.ts`.

**Byte-scan:** the six touched files (`endpoints.ts` new, `config.ts`, `nextedit/nextEditRoute.ts`, `nextedit/nextEditRoute.test.ts`, `backendFactory.ts`, `host/setup/registry.test.ts`) — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

This task adds one new non-test source file (`endpoints.ts`) and edits no test assertions' cardinality (the `registry.test.ts` "(c)" and `nextEditRoute.test.ts` edits change WHAT is read/imported, not how many `it`s or `expect`s run) — see the gate run below for the confirmed after-count.

FI-21's three production contortions (the `abortInFlight()` alias, `codeActionSerialize.ts`'s two dodges, `contextService.ts`'s field-by-field spread) are gone, each lock they were dodging is untouched or strictly STRICTER, and every RED probe still flags. OD-I's remaining deliverable, the lock-discipline POLICY plus a repo-wide LOCK INDEX, follows below.

### OD-I policy deliverable: lock-test discipline (ADR-FSU-02) + LOCK INDEX

**Policy** (reproduced verbatim from the FSU remediation architecture's own "Target design (ADR-FSU-02)" §1, `.superpowers/plans/2026-09-06-lens-r2-fsu-remediation-arch.md`; no standalone `docs_claude/adr/ADR-FSU-02-lock-discipline.md` file was ever created — this ADR, not that path, is the durable record OD-I's own row commits to):

> A source-scan lock is legitimate only when (a) the source of truth cannot be imported (foreign package / true frozen zone) or (b) it guards a security invariant that has no type-level expression; a lock must be scoped to the real sink and **must never dictate production shape**; every lock is registered in the ADR's index with its invariant, allowed shapes and RED probe. Duplicates guarded by drift-locks are consolidated (export + import) and the lock deleted.

FI-22's `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` case is the textbook instance of the failure mode this policy retires: no security invariant and no foreign/frozen source justified the duplication — an ordinary `export`/`import` (this task) was always available, and the two copies existed only because nobody had done it yet. FI-21's three sites (ADR-025-R/S/T) are the OTHER failure mode: a lock that, by being blunter than the invariant it actually protects (a bare-identifier receiver scan, an unscoped tool-name-literal ban, a field-name-blind spread ban), pushed PRODUCTION into an unnatural shape to dodge a false positive — the "must never dictate production shape" clause, restored in each case by either re-scoping the lock (FI-21 site 2, already fixed upstream at W3 T8a) or sanctioning the natural shape (FI-21 sites 1 and 3) rather than loosening the lock's real guarantee.

**LOCK INDEX.** Every source-scan lock in the repo as of this task's HEAD — a lock defined as: reads its own subject's RAW SOURCE TEXT (or, for `configScope.test.ts`, `package.json`'s parsed KEY set) from disk and applies a regex/string-membership/byte-hash check against it, as opposed to importing and calling real functions. Locks that instead invoke the real production code on a shared corpus and compare VERDICTS (`diffEgressDrift.lock.test.ts`, `wireContract.lock.test.ts`, `coexistence.lock.test.ts`'s own R2/R3/R5 `describe` blocks) are a different, valid pattern — behavioural/golden drift-locks — and are named here as deliberately EXCLUDED, not overlooked, so the index's boundary is not ad hoc.

| L20 | `webview/src/mock/fixtures.driftLock.test.ts` | The standalone browser dev build `fixtures.ts` mirrors `src/shared/mockScenario.ts`'s scripted turn/panel snapshots — BYTE-COMPARE of extracted values (`extractValue`/`normalize`), not a whole-file compare, not a runtime import | Both files are `vscode`-free, so live import would be *possible*; byte-compare chosen so the lock survives either side gaining a dependency the other can't satisfy | N/A (value-identity) | ACTIVE |

Not a source scan… the REAL `diffMayEgress` and the REAL `mintScannedNextEditRequest` are both invoked… and their verdicts are required to agree"); `src/autocomplete/backends/wireContract.lock.test.ts` (drives the REAL parsers — `OllamaFimBackend`/`LlamaCppInfillBackend`/`readOpenAiSseText` — against fixture wire payloads with mocked `fetch`, never scans source text); `coexistence.lock.test.ts`'s own R2/R3/R5 `describe` blocks (behavioural goldens over the real shell/guard, covered under L12's row instead of duplicated here).
