## ADR-025-V — `settleAsyncVerb` collapses the five LSP handler timeout/error arms (task F10-1, WS-F10 kickoff, FI-25)

**Outcome (task F10-1, WS-F10, 1 of 2, FI-25):** `src/mcp/lsp/tools.ts`'s five async LSP tool handlers (`handleLocationsTool` — shared by `lsp_definition`/`lsp_references`, `handleDocumentSymbols`, `handleWorkspaceSymbols`, `handleHover`, `handleCodeActions`) each repeated the same two-arm settle boilerplate against their gateway-call result: a `timeout-partial` arm (return an empty-shape framed result) and an `error` arm (log a handler-specific rejected-message + `errorResult()`), before continuing with the success value. A single non-frozen helper, `settleAsyncVerb`, now owns both arms; the three FROZEN `Object.freeze` constants (`ZERO_RANGE` :194, `UNCLASSIFIED_TAIL_VERDICT` :206, `DIAGNOSTIC_SEVERITY_LABEL` :366 — line numbers unchanged, confirmed 0-diff below) and the three LSP allowlist tables (`lspCommandAllowlist.ts`/`lspGateway.ts`/`frameSanitize.ts`) were never touched — this task edits only handler bodies plus one new private helper.

1. **The TS-narrowing trap (avoided).** A `settleAsyncVerb(...): ToolResult | undefined` sketch does not work: after `if (early !== undefined) return early;` in the CALLER, TS still sees the original `run` union in full — the timeout/error variants of `GatewayVerdict<T>` have no `.value`, so a subsequent `run.value` is a compile error, and forcing it with `as`/`!` is banned. `settleAsyncVerb` instead returns a DISCRIMINATED result, `{ readonly early: ToolResult } | { readonly value: T; readonly status: 'ok' | 'maybe-indexing' }`. The narrowing happens INSIDE the helper (two early `return`s on `run.status`, ordinary discriminated-union control-flow narrowing — no `as`/`!` needed there either), and the caller narrows its own OWN `settled` value via `'early' in settled`, after which `settled.value`/`settled.status` are typed exactly as `run.value`/`run.status` were after the old handler's own two early returns.
2. **`handleLocationsTool`/`handleDocumentSymbols`/`handleWorkspaceSymbols`/`handleHover` — direct collapse.** All four already call `runAsyncVerbWithRetry`, whose return type (`GatewayVerdict<T>` — `{status:'ok'|'maybe-indexing';value:T} | {status:'timeout-partial'} | {status:'error';error}`) IS the shape `settleAsyncVerb` accepts, so each site passes its `run` straight through, unchanged. Every empty-shape thunk and every rejected-log string is copied byte-for-byte from the deleted `if` arms it replaces (`shapeLocations([], DEFAULT_SHAPER_CAPS, { cap: opts.capLimit })` / `'[lsp] locations gateway call rejected'`; `shapeDocumentSymbols([], args.path, DEFAULT_SHAPER_CAPS)` / `'[lsp] document_symbols gateway call rejected'`; `shapeWorkspaceSymbols([], DEFAULT_SHAPER_CAPS, { cap: capLimit })` / `'[lsp] workspace_symbols gateway call rejected'`; `shapeHover([], DEFAULT_SHAPER_CAPS)` / `'[lsp] hover gateway call rejected'`) — every downstream `run.value`/`run.status` reference (including `handleDocumentSymbols`'s M-1 never-cache-empty branch and `handleWorkspaceSymbols`'s M-2 capped/tail classification) becomes `settled.value`/`settled.status`, with no other line changed.
3. **`handleCodeActions` — the site the original lens missed, ground-truthed as genuinely DIFFERENT.** This handler calls `runOnce` directly (no first-empty/maybe-indexing retry — the file's own section header already documents "single-shot stateless... under deadline"), whose return type is `{kind:'ok';value:T} | {kind:'timeout'} | {kind:'error';error}` — a DIFFERENT discriminant field (`kind`, not `status`) and no `'maybe-indexing'` arm at all. Reusing `settleAsyncVerb` here without widening its contract (and without `any`/`as`) required one explicit translation step: `rawRun` (the `runOnce` result) is mapped into a `GatewayVerdict<readonly RawCodeAction[]>`-shaped local (`rawRun.kind === 'ok' ? {status:'ok',value:rawRun.value} : rawRun.kind === 'timeout' ? {status:'timeout-partial'} : {status:'error',error:rawRun.error}`) before calling `settleAsyncVerb`. The success-path `textResult(withStatus('ok', framed))` (previously a hard-coded `'ok'` literal — this tool's status is never anything else) becomes `textResult(withStatus(settled.status, framed))` for consistency with the other four sites; behaviourally identical, since `settled.status` can only ever be `'ok'` on this path (the mapping never produces `'maybe-indexing'`).
4. **`exactOptionalPropertyTypes` fix, caught by the real `tsc`, not by vitest's typecheck pass.** The first cut passed `log: deps.log` (typed `((msg:string)=>void)|undefined`) into `SettleAsyncVerbOptions.log?: (msg:string)=>void` at all five call sites — `npx vitest run src/mcp/lsp/`'s typecheck plugin passed this silently, but the real `npm run check-types` (`tsc --noEmit`) correctly rejected it under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true` (TS2379 ×5: explicitly assigning `undefined` to an optional property differs from omitting it). Fixed with this file's own pre-existing idiom (already used a dozen times above for the same reason — e.g. `...(raw.source !== undefined ? { source: raw.source } : {})`): `...(deps.log !== undefined ? { log: deps.log } : {})` at all five sites. Lesson: `npx vitest run <dir>`'s bundled typecheck is not a substitute for the full `npm run check-types:all` — the brief's own "during TDD run only the directory suite; run the full gate ONCE before committing" step is exactly what caught this.
5. **Mutation, self-verified before commit (reviewer re-runs independently).** `settleAsyncVerb`'s `timeout-partial` arm was perturbed to return `errorResult()` (the error arm's output) instead of the framed empty-shape result; `npx vitest run src/mcp/lsp/` immediately went RED on 3 tests (`tools.test.ts`: "timeout-partial renders strictly before the opening `<lsp_result>` tag", plus `lsp_code_actions`'s own timeout-partial no-hang test, plus one more frame-shape assertion) — confirmed the goldens actually guard this collapse, not merely coexist with it. Reverted before continuing (file restored to the pre-mutation diff, re-confirmed byte-identical via `git diff --stat` unchanged and the full suite green again).

**Frozen-zone check.** `ZERO_RANGE`/`UNCLASSIFIED_TAIL_VERDICT`/`DIAGNOSTIC_SEVERITY_LABEL` — all three still at their pre-task line numbers (194/206/366) — 0-diff (`git diff` shows no `+`/`-` line touching any `Object.freeze` occurrence). `lspCommandAllowlist.ts`/`lspGateway.ts`/`frameSanitize.ts` — `git diff --stat` against all three shows nothing; none were opened for edit.

**`tools.test.ts` — 0-EDIT.** `git diff --stat` touches exactly one file, `src/mcp/lsp/tools.ts`; the test file's framed-output goldens, the AU-20 pool tests, and the I1 suite all ran unmodified and green (483/483 in the directory-scoped run, all previously-passing assertions still passing with no test-side change).

**Byte-scan:** `src/mcp/lsp/tools.ts` (the only touched file) — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**Count discipline:** F9-4 HEAD baseline (ADR-025-U) = **7628 passed | 15 skipped (7643), 346 test files, tsc 0**. This task adds one new private, non-exported helper (`settleAsyncVerb`) and zero new tests (per the brief: "count must NOT drop; ... unless you add a helper test" — none added, the existing goldens already cover every arm through the five call sites) — full-gate run after this task: **346 test files passed, 7628 passed | 15 skipped (7643), tsc 0 (host + webview)** — count UNCHANGED, confirming the collapse is purely internal.

**WS-F10 in progress** — F10-1 (this task) closes FI-25's five-site collapse; task 2 of 2 follows.

## ADR-025-W — `OnceRegistry` replaces the three module-level dedup Sets, activation-scoped (task F10-2, WS-F10 — LAST task of Wave 1, FI-26)

**Outcome (task F10-2, WS-F10, 2 of 2, FI-26, FSU §5 Q4):** the three independent module-level `Set<string>` dedup primitives this zone kept (`provider.ts`'s `surfacedAutocompleteFailures`, `nextedit/backend.ts`'s `warnedOnce`, `backendFactory.ts`'s `warnedOnce`) are now backed by ONE reusable class, `src/autocomplete/onceRegistry.ts`'s `OnceRegistry` (pure, vscode-free — `has`/`add`/`delete`/`reset`). `provider.ts` receives a REQUIRED, activation-scoped instance via its constructor options (`index.ts` creates ONE `onceRegistry` alongside `keyRefreshSeq`, module-local, and threads it to `buildEngine`→`createBackend` and to the provider's own construction); `keyRefreshSeq` itself is untouched (still the monotonic race token it always was, unrelated state). The three `console.warn`/`showWarningMessage` once-only sites are unchanged in wording and firing condition — only their dedup storage changed.

1. **GATE 1 — the API surface, ground-truthed per site.** `provider.ts:333` (`surfaceIfFirst`'s toast-`onRejected` handler) calls `.delete(key)` to re-arm a single key after a failed remediation — the ONLY of the three sites that ever removes a key rather than clearing everything. `OnceRegistry` therefore exposes exactly the union every site actually uses: `has`/`add` (all three), `delete` (provider.ts only), `reset` (all three, replacing each site's own `.clear()`). Confirmed via `onceRegistry.test.ts`'s own unit tests (has/add/delete/reset + two-instance isolation), written FIRST and watched RED (`Cannot find module './onceRegistry'`) before the class existed.
2. **GATE 2 — the activation-scoped unification is real and DELIBERATE; no test pins the old per-rebuild re-arm.** Ground-truthed the two production call sites BEFORE this task: `index.ts`'s `rebuild()` called `clearBackendFactoryWarnings()` BEFORE `buildEngine` (its warnings fire synchronously at construction) and `clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures()` AFTER (the provider's Set is only consulted later, on a real completion request) — both on EVERY `talaria.autocomplete.*` config change and API-key load/rotation. `nextedit/backend.ts`'s `warnedOnce`, by contrast, was ALREADY never re-armed by production (`clearNextEditBackendWarnings` had zero production call sites — only its own test file called it directly) — i.e. already activation-scoped in effect, not per-rebuild. Both `clearBackendFactoryWarnings()` and `clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures()` calls are REMOVED from `rebuild()` (comments there now explain the FSU §5 Q4 lifetime instead). **Grep proof no test pins the automatic wiring**: `grep -rn "clearBackendFactoryWarnings\|clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures" src --include="*.test.ts" -A2 -B2 | grep -i "toHaveBeenCalled\|expect|spy|vi.fn"` returns hits ONLY inside `backendFactory.test.ts`'s own direct-call re-arm test (asserts `clearBackendFactoryWarnings()` itself re-arms — a manual-call test, not an integration test of `rebuild()` calling it) and `nextedit/backend.test.ts`'s identical direct-call test for `clearNextEditBackendWarnings()`; `index.test.ts`/`activationRace.test.ts`/`keyRefreshRace.test.ts` mock `./backendFactory` wholesale with an unused `clearBackendFactoryWarnings: () => {}` stub and never assert it is called, and `index.test.ts`'s `onDidChangeConfiguration` mock (`() => ({ dispose() {} })`) never even stores/fires the real callback — no test anywhere exercises "a real config-change rebuild automatically re-arms a warning." `provider.test.ts`'s own "rebuild re-arms" test (renamed `registry.reset() makes the same 401 surface again`, assertions unchanged) only ever tested the MANUAL reset primitive being callable again, never the automatic `rebuild()`-triggers-clear wiring — so it is preserved, not broken, by swapping that primitive to `registry.reset()`. **This IS the intended behaviour change** (FSU §5 Q4 "yes for all three"): a surfaced warning now stays silent for the rest of the ACTIVATION, not just until the next config change — called out explicitly for the reviewer, not silently landed.
3. **GATE 3 — `provider.test.ts`'s real reset mechanism (the DECLARED harness edit), the plan's "13 `vi.resetModules`" claim confirmed stale.** `grep -c "vi.resetModules" src/autocomplete/provider.test.ts` = 0. The real mechanism: 5 `beforeEach` blocks + 3 inline mid-test calls to the now-removed `clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures()`. Because dedup state moved from a shared module-level Set to a per-provider `OnceRegistry` instance, `makeProvider()` now defaults every call to a FRESH `new OnceRegistry()` (via a new optional `FailureSurfacingOpts.registry` override) — this alone reproduces the isolation the 5 `beforeEach` calls used to provide (a brand-new provider always starts with nothing surfaced), so those 5 `beforeEach` bodies simply DROP the now-redundant call (comment explains why). The 3 tests that re-arm an EXISTING provider MID-TEST (the renamed "rebuild re-arms"/"clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures() re-arms…" ×2) construct an explicit `const registry = new OnceRegistry()`, pass it into `makeProvider(..., { registry })`, and call `registry.reset()` at the exact point the old code called `clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures()`. The 5 direct `new TalariaInlineCompletionProvider({...})` construction sites (outside `makeProvider`) each gained `registry: new OnceRegistry()` (the field is REQUIRED on `provider.ts`, unlike `backendFactory.ts`/`nextedit/backend.ts` below — see design note). **Every `expect(...)` assertion is byte-identical to the pre-edit file** — `npx vitest run src/autocomplete/provider.test.ts` before this edit: 66 tests, 35 RED (`TypeError: clearSurfacedAutocompleteFailures is not a function`, plus required-field TS errors at the 5 direct sites); after: 66 tests, all green — same 66 (0 added, 0 removed; only 3 titles renamed to name the new mechanism, e.g. "registry.reset() re-arms…").
4. **Design: `provider.ts`'s `registry` is REQUIRED; `backendFactory.ts`/`nextedit/backend.ts`'s is OPTIONAL with a module-level default.** `provider.ts` is fully reachable from `index.ts` with no other file in the way, so its registry is genuinely threaded end-to-end and its test file absorbs the one declared harness edit. `backendFactory.ts`'s `createBackend(cfg, registry = defaultRegistry)` is ALSO genuinely production-wired (`index.ts`'s `buildEngine` always passes the real `onceRegistry`) — `defaultRegistry` exists purely so `backendFactory.test.ts`'s own direct `createBackend(cfg(...))` calls (no registry argument, 27 tests) keep compiling and passing unmodified. `nextedit/backend.ts`'s `NextEditBackendOptions.registry?` is the one genuine DEVIATION worth flagging: `NextEditHttpBackend` is constructed FRESH on every single next-edit prediction attempt, at `nextedit/shell.vscode.ts:916` — a file named nowhere in this task's brief and NOT touched. Threading the literal shared `onceRegistry` instance there would require editing that file (and, since a brand-new per-instance registry every prediction would defeat the whole dedup — reintroducing per-request warning spam — an editorial change to `NextEditShellDeps`/the executor's own lifetime, not a one-line pass-through). Left OUT OF SCOPE: `nextedit/backend.ts` keeps a MODULE-LEVEL `defaultRegistry` (an `OnceRegistry` instance, replacing the raw `Set` 1:1) that every current caller (production and both test files) uses via the optional parameter's default — behaviourally IDENTICAL to pre-task (never re-armed automatically, exactly as before), only the storage class changed. `reuseLocks.test.ts`'s 22-file count and `nextEditPurity.test.ts`'s vscode-free scan both stayed green with zero edits, confirming no new/moved file under `nextedit/`.
5. **Mutation, self-verified before commit (reviewer re-runs independently).** `OnceRegistry.has` temporarily forced to always `return false` → `npx vitest run src/autocomplete/` immediately went RED on multiple `provider.test.ts`/`backendFactory.test.ts`/`nextedit/backend.test.ts` "surfaces exactly once … the identical second failure is silent" assertions (dedup no longer dedups — every repeat re-surfaces). Reverted; full directory suite re-confirmed green before continuing.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `provider.ts`, `index.ts`, `backendFactory.ts`, `nextedit/backend.ts` are all non-frozen; `nextedit/shell.vscode.ts` was deliberately left untouched (point 4 above), not frozen but out of this task's named scope.

**`nextedit/backend.test.ts`/`backendFactory.test.ts` — 0-EDIT.** Both continue calling their real functions with no `registry` argument and pass unmodified (42/42 and 27/27 respectively) — the optional-parameter-with-module-default design absorbs the API change completely for these two files.

**`provider.test.ts` — DECLARED EDIT (not 0).** `git diff --stat`: +56/-17 lines. Mechanism swapped (registry threading + `.reset()` in place of the module-level clear function); every `expect(...)` assertion byte-identical; 3 test titles renamed for accuracy (see point 3); one stale doc comment ("that only happens on the next config rebuild") corrected to describe the new activation-scoped lifetime.

**Byte-scan:** `onceRegistry.ts`, `onceRegistry.test.ts`, `backendFactory.ts`, `nextedit/backend.ts`, `provider.ts`, `index.ts`, `provider.test.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all seven files).

**Count discipline:** F10-1 HEAD baseline (ADR-025-V) = **7628 passed | 15 skipped (7643), 346 test files, tsc 0**. This task adds ONE new test file (`onceRegistry.test.ts`, 7 tests, TDD RED-then-GREEN) and zero other new/removed tests (`provider.test.ts` stays at 66; `backendFactory.test.ts` stays at 27; `nextedit/backend.test.ts` stays at 42) — full-gate run after this task: **347 test files passed, 7635 passed | 15 skipped (7650), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Delta: +1 file, +7 tests — exactly the new `onceRegistry.test.ts`, confirming every other suite is behaviour-preserving.

**★★★ WS-F10 CLOSED — Wave 1 of the lens-r2-defer-closeout program complete.**

## ADR-025-X — `nextedit/backend.ts`'s dedup registry becomes activation-scoped and injected; FI-26's 3rd site closed (task F10-2b, WS-F10 follow-up)

**Outcome (task F10-2b, FI-26 closeout, follow-up to F10-2/ADR-025-W):** ADR-025-W's own point 4 named this exact residual and left it deliberately out of scope: `nextedit/backend.ts` kept a MODULE-LEVEL `defaultRegistry` (an `OnceRegistry` instance) as the fallback every `NextEditHttpBackend` used when constructed without an explicit `registry`, because `NextEditHttpBackend` is built FRESH on every single next-edit prediction attempt at `shell.vscode.ts:916` (untouched by F10-2) — threading the real registry there needed an editorial change to the shell itself. This task makes that change: `NextEditShell` now holds ONE stable `private readonly onceRegistry = new OnceRegistry()` field (constructed once per activation, alongside its other per-activation state — `fim`, `debouncer`, `surfacedFailures`), and the `runPrediction` construction site passes `registry: this.onceRegistry` — the SAME instance — to every per-prediction `NextEditHttpBackend`. `NextEditBackendOptions.registry` is now REQUIRED (the `?` removed); `defaultRegistry` and `clearNextEditBackendWarnings` are deleted outright, not merely unused — `grep -rln "defaultRegistry|clearNextEditBackendWarnings" src/` now returns only `backendFactory.ts` (the FIM path's own, separate, out-of-scope pair — untouched, not part of this task's named files). This is the third and last of FI-26's three dedup sites (`provider.ts`, `backendFactory.ts`'s production wiring, and now this one) to lose its module-level/optional-fallback shape; `nextedit/backend.ts` stays vscode-free throughout (the registry arrives as a plain constructor param, same as before — only its optionality changed).

1. **GROUND-TRUTH — the one prod construction site, confirmed sole.** `grep -n "new NextEditHttpBackend(" src` (prod + test) found exactly one production call site (`shell.vscode.ts:916`, now `:934` after the field's doc comment) and seven test-file sites (`backend.test.ts` ×7, via `makeBackend` plus six inline constructions — see point 3). No second prod site exists, so no fan-out was needed.
2. **CRITICAL invariant, verified twice.** The field is `private readonly`, assigned exactly once in a class-field initializer (`= new OnceRegistry()`), so there is no code path that could reconstruct it per-prediction short of editing this one line — and the construction site passes `this.onceRegistry`, never `new OnceRegistry()`. **Mutation, self-verified before commit (reviewer re-runs independently):** temporarily changed the construction site to `registry: new OnceRegistry()` (a fresh instance per prediction — the exact regression this design forbids) and re-ran `npx vitest run src/autocomplete/` — **all 75 files / 1520 tests still passed.** This is the honest, expected result: `shell.vscode.test.ts` mocks `./backend` wholesale (its `NextEditHttpBackend` stub never calls the real `warnOnce`/`registry` logic unless a test flips `backendSpy.useRealTransport = true`, and even the tests that do never spy on `console.warn` or assert registry identity across two `backendSpy.constructed[]` entries — `grep -n "console.warn\|warnSpy" shell.vscode.test.ts` returns zero hits). Per this task's own brief: "If no shell-level warn-once test exists, note it — the backend.test.ts per-case tests still prove the registry is used." Noted here; `shell.vscode.test.ts` was explicitly out of the edit list (0-EDIT requirement, see point 4) so no new regression test was added there. The mutation was reverted and confirmed byte-identical to pre-mutation (`diff` against a pre-mutation copy) before continuing. The second half of the brief's mutation instruction — "a stub registry whose `has` returns false → the warn fires every time" — is reasoned through rather than re-run: `warnOnce`'s `if (this.registry.has(key)) return;` makes this true by inspection, and `OnceRegistry.has`/`add`'s own correctness was already unit-proven in `onceRegistry.test.ts` at F10-2 (ADR-025-W) — this task changed the INJECTION site, not the class.
3. **`backend.test.ts` — DECLARED harness edit (the brief's own anticipated one), assertions unchanged.** `registry` is now required, so every `new NextEditHttpBackend({...})` in this file needed a value: `makeBackend`'s helper gained an `overrides.registry?: OnceRegistry` that defaults to a FRESH `new OnceRegistry()` per call (mirrors `provider.test.ts`'s own `makeProvider` pattern from F10-2/ADR-025-W point 3) — this alone reproduces the isolation the removed `beforeEach(() => clearNextEditBackendWarnings())` used to provide (a brand-new backend always starts with nothing warned). The six INLINE constructions (two in the "pinned guard order" describe block, four in the CF-24 describe block) each gained a literal `registry: new OnceRegistry()`. The one test that re-arms an EXISTING backend MID-TEST ("warns only ONCE across repeated predict calls until registry.reset() re-arms it", renamed from "…until clearNextEditBackendWarnings() re-arms it") constructs an explicit `const registry = new OnceRegistry()`, passes it via `makeBackend('ollama', { apiKey: 'leftover-key', registry })`, and calls `registry.reset()` at the exact point the old code called `clearNextEditBackendWarnings()` — mirrors `provider.test.ts`'s identical "construct an explicit registry, pass it in, call `.reset()` mid-test" shape from ADR-025-W point 3. **Every `expect(...)` assertion in this file is byte-identical to the pre-edit file** — only the one test title changed (to name the new mechanism) and the `beforeEach` comment was rewritten to explain why no reset call remains. `npx vitest run src/autocomplete/nextedit/backend.test.ts` before and after: same 42 tests, all green.
4. **The two F3-1 golden masters (`nextedit.golden.pure.test.ts` / `nextedit.golden.shell.test.ts`) needed ONE forced line each — the brief's "0-EDIT" expectation was not quite right, and this is called out rather than silently landed.** Both files' `vi.mock('./backend', async () => { const actual = await vi.importActual<typeof import('./backend')>('./backend'); return { clearNextEditBackendWarnings: actual.clearNextEditBackendWarnings, NextEditHttpBackend: class {...} }; })` re-exported `clearNextEditBackendWarnings` from the real module into the mock. Once that export is deleted from `backend.ts`, `actual.clearNextEditBackendWarnings` is a genuine `tsc` error (`Property 'clearNextEditBackendWarnings' does not exist`) under this repo's `tsconfig.json` `include: ["src/**/*.ts", ...]`, which type-checks test files too — `npx vitest run` alone would NOT have caught this (its transform doesn't enforce the property's absence), only the full `npm run gate`'s `tsc --noEmit` does, which is exactly why the brief's own note that the dir-scoped vitest typecheck is looser than the full gate's tsc matters here. `grep -n "clearNextEditBackendWarnings" nextedit.golden.*.test.ts` before the fix: one hit each, in dead code (nothing downstream of either mock factory ever read that key — `shell.vscode.ts` does not re-export it either). The minimal fix, applied identically to both files: delete the `clearNextEditBackendWarnings: actual.clearNextEditBackendWarnings,` line and — since `actual` then becomes unused (`noUnusedLocals`) — delete the now-pointless `const actual = await vi.importActual(...)` line and the factory's `async` keyword along with it (nothing else in either factory reads `actual`). Every `NextEditHttpBackend` mock class, every `backendSpy`/`calls` assertion, every test title, and every other line in both files is untouched — `git diff --stat` for each shows exactly the one `vi.mock` header line changed plus the one body line removed. This is the same class of edit as point 3's "declared harness edit," just forced by the API surface change rather than by a design choice, and is flagged here per the brief's own review-focus item (b): "grep confirms no lingering references" — after this fix, `grep -rln "clearNextEditBackendWarnings" src/` returns nothing under `nextedit/`.

## ADR-025-Y — `reindexFiles` spy-order golden bank, WS-F6 KICKOFF (task F6-1, WS-F6, CHARACTERIZATION-FIRST, ZERO production edits)

**Outcome (task F6-1, WS-F6 kickoff):** WS-F6 will decompose/clean `rag/buildPipeline.ts`'s `reindexFiles` (`:275-516`) and its siblings across F6-2..F6-9. Before any of that, this task lays down a CHARACTERIZATION-FIRST golden bank (Fable/GD.2 discipline, same posture as WS-GD.2's `statusGolden`/`ControlDispatcher` masters): a new `src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts` drives the REAL `reindexFiles` with a hand-built fake `IndexerContext`, records every `store.deleteByPath`/`store.upsert`/embedder-`embed` call into one ordered `calls: string[]` log, and pins that ORDERED sequence plus the final `manifest` object for three cases: (a) success, (b) a throw in the second embed batch (exercises the `:501-513` catch-scrub), and (c) `isDisposed()` flipping true after batch 1 (exercises the `:442` guard). It touches **zero production code** — `git diff --stat src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` is empty at every commit this task made; only the new test file and this ADR entry are new.

1. **Scenario — 3 files / 2 embed batches at today's `EMBED_BATCH_SIZE = 64` (`:32`, sliced at `:413`).** `zero.dat` (whitespace-only, 0 chunks) exercises the phase-1 `recordCount === 0` immediate purge+manifest branch (`:391-399`) with no `pathState` entry at all. `alpha.dat` (50 chunks, via the synthetic-line fixture below) lands entirely inside batch 1. `beta.dat` (20 chunks) SPANS the boundary: its first 14 land in batch 1, its remaining 6 in batch 2 — the "half-swapped" shape case (b)'s scrub targets. Total pending records = 70 → batch 1 = records[0..63] (64), batch 2 = records[64..69] (6). Both `.dat` files use an extension absent from `EXTENSION_TO_LANGUAGE_ID`, so `languageId` is `undefined` and `buildPipeline.ts:349`'s `...(languageId ? { parser: ctx.parser } : {})` never even reads `ctx.parser` — chunking falls through to the plain `chunkByLines` fallback, which the test file also calls directly (the REAL production function, not a reimplementation) to self-check that the synthetic line counts (1510/610) actually produce 50/20 chunks — a future change to `lineWindowChunker.ts`'s window/overlap constants fails this fixture loudly instead of silently shifting the pinned batch boundary.
2. **Manifest fixtures were tightened after the FIRST perturbation run exposed a hole (self-correction, recorded honestly).** The original draft started every case from an EMPTY initial `manifest`. Running the scrub-disable perturbation (see point 4) against that draft left ALL THREE cases green — because `beta.dat`'s manifest entry was never SET in the first place before the throw, so `delete manifest['beta.dat']` was already a no-op regardless of whether the scrub ran. Fixed by giving case (b) a PRE-EXISTING `manifest['beta.dat'] = betaHash` (models the fingerprint-mismatch full-rebuild path, `runBuild`'s `toCompute = Object.keys(current)` branch, where unchanged content is recomputed anyway) — this is the exact shape TA-3's scrub protects, and re-running the same perturbation with this fixture now fails ONLY case (b), cleanly. Case (c) was given a DISTINGUISHABLE stale value (`'stale-hash-from-a-prior-build'`, not `betaHash`) so its assertion proves the dispose path (a plain early return, no throw, no scrub) leaves the entry completely untouched — neither finalized nor scrubbed — which is a genuinely different observable outcome than case (b)'s active deletion. This asymmetry (throw → active scrub; dispose → silent no-op) is itself now pinned, not just the two end-states in isolation.
3. **Scope decision, re-grounded against the live file (departs from one literal brief phrase, done deliberately and disclosed).** The task brief's per-case list cited `:645`/`:644-645` (`ctx.writeManifest` calls) as part of the sequence to assert. A `grep -n "ctx\.(writeManifest|writeMeta|readManifest|readMeta|...)"` over `buildPipeline.ts` shows every one of those members is referenced ONLY inside `runBuild` (`:638/645/656` etc.) — `reindexFiles`'s own body (`:275-516`) never reaches any of them; the sole `ctx.opts.*` field it reads is `maxChunkTokens` (`:350`). Fabricating a test-local wrapper that reproduces `runBuild`'s guard-then-write tail around the `reindexFiles` call would mean asserting behaviour of hand-rolled harness glue, not the real function under decomposition (`test-antipatterns`: don't test the mock). This golden instead scopes strictly to `reindexFiles` as the brief's own summary sentence asks ("drive the REAL `reindexFiles(ctx, targets, manifest, …)`"), and wires every `IndexerContext` member `reindexFiles` never touches (`writeManifest`, `writeMeta`, `readManifest`, `readMeta`, `computeEffectiveWidth`, `fingerprintMatches`, `loadIgnoreFilter`, `invalidateIgnoreFilterCache`, `setKnownNestedIgnoreDirs`, `recordFailedIncrementalReindex`, `logger`, plus the unused `VectorStore`/`CodeParser` methods) to a stub that THROWS if ever invoked — a stronger, still-accurate characterization (0 calls to any `runBuild`-only seam, in all 3 cases) than the brief's literal citation would have produced. `runBuild`'s own tail is a separate function with its own contract; it should get its own future golden in a later WS-F6 task, not borrow this one's harness.
4. **The net has teeth — two independent perturbations, both proven then reverted.** (i) Swapped the batch loop's `upsert` call to run BEFORE the per-path `deleteByPath` swap loop (`:474-482`) — re-ran the suite: **all 3 cases failed**, each showing `upsert:64` appearing before `deleteByPath:alpha.dat`/`deleteByPath:beta.dat` in the ordered `calls` array instead of after. Reverted; `git diff --stat src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` empty again; re-ran, all 3 green. (ii) Disabled the catch-scrub by short-circuiting its condition (`if (false && state.deleted && state.remaining > 0)`, `:509`) — re-ran: **only case (b) failed** (its final `manifest` still carried `'beta.dat': betaHash`, the pre-existing entry the scrub is supposed to remove), cases (a) and (c) stayed green (neither exercises the scrub). Reverted; diff empty again; re-ran, all 3 green. Both mutations were applied to `buildPipeline.ts` directly, observed to fail the RIGHT case(s) for the RIGHT reason, then reverted before the next step — the file is byte-identical to `f6d7b4f` at every commit this task made.
5. **F6-7 forward-compatibility (critic M-7) — documented choice: rely on the constant, not a speculative fixture field.** F6-7 is planned to replace the `EMBED_BATCH_SIZE` module constant with `ctx.embedder.batchSize` (default 64). Today's `Embedder` interface (`embedder.ts:128-141`) has no `batchSize` field; adding one to the fake `embedder` object literal now would trip TypeScript's excess-property check on a fresh object literal assigned to an `Embedder`-typed const (and `any`/`as`-cast workarounds are both banned). This golden therefore relies on today's `EMBED_BATCH_SIZE = 64` constant alone to produce the pinned 64+6 split, and documents in the test file's header comment that F6-7 must add `batchSize: 64` to `createFakeEmbedder`'s fixture (widening its type to the extended `Embedder` shape) as its ONE declared fixture edit — the 2-batch shape and every other assertion in this file must stay byte-identical through F6-7.
6. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none.** Every "must never be called" ctx member is a zero-arg arrow returning `unexpected(name): never` (throws); `never` is structurally assignable to any expected return type (including every `Promise<T>`/`void`/multi-required-param method shape here), so no cast, no widened `any`, and no non-null assertion was needed anywhere in the file. `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`-sensitive array reads (the `isDisposedSequence` lookup) use `?? false`, never `!`.

## ADR-025-Z — dead `preloaded` parameter removed + the golden's fs-injection seam swap it forced (task F6-2, WS-F6, FI-42, COUPLED with F6-1's golden)

**Outcome (task F6-2, WS-F6, FI-42):** `reindexFiles`'s `preloaded?: Map<string, Buffer>` param (`:280`, AUDIT-5 Task 10's read-once optimization) was reverted long ago — neither production caller (`runBuild` at `:620`, `handleFsEvent` at `watchPipeline.ts:222`) has ever passed it, so `:307` always fell through to `fs.readFile` at runtime. A v1 attempt at this task was BLOCKED: F6-1's own golden bank (`buildPipeline.order.test.ts`) turned out to use `preloaded` as ITS filesystem-avoidance seam (fake `/fake-workspace/…` targets with no real files on disk, fed straight into `reindexFiles`'s 5th arg). Deleting the param outright would have broken the golden's ability to drive `reindexFiles` at all. This revised brief couples the two changes into one commit: swap the golden's injection seam to an `fs.readFile` mock FIRST (self-verified independently), then delete the dead param.

1. **Step A — swap the golden's injection seam, assertions untouched.** `buildScenario()`'s `preloaded` map was renamed `fileBytes` (same three `absPath → Buffer` entries) and is no longer passed as `reindexFiles`'s 5th argument. A new `patchReadFile(fileBytes): () => void` helper monkey-patches `node:fs`'s `promises.readFile` (the exact module `buildPipeline.ts:2` imports as `fs` — a shared Node module singleton, so patching it in the test file affects the production import too, the same idiom `indexer.test.ts:1283-1286` already relies on) to answer from the `fileBytes` map for the three fake paths the scenario knows about, falling back to the real implementation for anything else (never exercised by this golden, since it never reads outside its own three targets — kept as a safety net rather than a silent `undefined`). Each of the 3 `it` blocks calls `patchReadFile` before `reindexFiles`, drops the (now nonexistent) 5th argument, and restores the original in a `finally`. **Verified independently before touching production code:** with `buildPipeline.ts` still byte-identical to `0bfb1e4` (`git diff --stat` empty), all 3 cases stayed green — proving the seam swap alone reproduces the exact same observable behaviour `preloaded` used to, before any dead-code deletion happened. Every `expect(calls).toEqual(...)` / `expect(manifest).toEqual(...)` assertion is byte-identical to F6-1's original; only the plumbing feeding `reindexFiles` its bytes changed (a declared, mechanical harness edit — `test-antipatterns`: this is not "testing the mock", the mock supplies the SAME fixture data the map did, and the function under test still reads through the real `fs.readFile` call path it uses in production).
2. **Steps B/C — delete the param, make the read unconditional.** `preloaded?: Map<string, Buffer>` removed from `reindexFiles`'s signature (`:280` old numbering); `:307`'s `buf = preloaded?.get(readAbsPath) ?? (await fs.readFile(readAbsPath));` became the unconditional `buf = await fs.readFile(readAbsPath);` — exactly what already ran for both production callers.
3. **Step D — doc rewrite.** The `:252-258` "AUDIT-5 Task 10: `preloaded` is an optional …" reverted-optimization block was replaced with a short stream-and-reread note: `runBuild`'s hash pass (`~:537-543`, RAG-01) reads each candidate once to hash it and releases the buffer without retaining it; `reindexFiles` re-reads every changed path's bytes itself, unconditionally, exactly like the single-target watch path always has. No dangling reference to the reverted optimization remains (`grep -n preloaded src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` → 0 hits).
4. **The golden still has teeth after the swap (self-check, perturbation proven then reverted).** With the fs-mock in place and the param already deleted, the catch-scrub's condition (`:508`, `if (state.deleted && state.remaining > 0)`) was short-circuited to `if (false && …)` and the suite re-run: **only case (b) failed** — `manifest` still carried the stale pre-existing `'beta.dat': betaHash'` entry the scrub is supposed to delete (`toEqual` diff showed the extra key); cases (a) and (c) stayed green (neither exercises the scrub). Reverted; `git diff --stat src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` shows only the intended param/read/doc changes again; re-ran, all 3 green. This proves the injection-seam swap did not neuter the golden's ability to detect a real regression in the scrub it pins.

## ADR-025-AA — `reindexFiles` = `buildPendingRecords` → `embedAndSwap` with an explicit `pathState` hand-off (task F6-3, WS-F6, FI-08)

**Outcome (task F6-3, WS-F6, FI-08):** `reindexFiles`'s ~230-line two-phase body (`fc76285:275-514`) is now two module-private helpers plus a 4-line orchestrator. **`buildPendingRecords(ctx, targets, manifest): Promise<{ records: ChunkRecord[]; pathState: ReadonlyMap<string, MutablePathState> } | { disposed: true }>`** is today's PHASE 1, moved verbatim: the entry `isDisposed` guard (`:288`), the `pendingRecords` array, the `pathState` Map build, and the per-target read/chunk loop (`:303-402`) including the oversize/binary purge-and-bail branch's own `isDisposed` guard (`:327`) and the zero-chunk immediate-purge branch (`:390-398`). **`embedAndSwap(ctx, records, pathState, manifest, expectedWidth): Promise<number | undefined>`** is today's PHASE 2, also moved verbatim: the batch loop (`:412-491`) — embed, dispose-guard, width-capture, per-batch delete-before-upsert swap, `remaining` decrement, per-path manifest write — and the TA-3 catch-scrub (`:500-513`). `reindexFiles` becomes `ensure → buildPendingRecords → embedAndSwap`:

```ts
export async function reindexFiles(ctx, targets, manifest, expectedWidth) {
  await ctx.ensureStoreInitialized();
  const pending = await buildPendingRecords(ctx, targets, manifest);
  if ('disposed' in pending) return undefined;
  return embedAndSwap(ctx, pending.records, pending.pathState, manifest, expectedWidth);
}
```

The ONLY logic change anywhere in the split: phase 1's two `return undefined;` guards (`:288`, `:327`) become `return { disposed: true };` — `reindexFiles` itself translates that sentinel back to `undefined` at its own single call site. Every comment moved with its statement unedited, including two that now read as if addressed to `reindexFiles` itself (the `:288` guard's "at this function's own entry" and the `:327` guard's "matching this loop's own entry guard above (`if (disposed) return undefined;`)") — left verbatim per the brief's explicit "the only logic change is the return shape" scope; a future task may re-word them if it touches this file for another reason, but this one does not manufacture an unrelated comment edit.

1. **The `pathState` hand-off (M-16) — NO CAST, confirmed by `tsc`.** `type MutablePathState = { contentHash: string; remaining: number; deleted: boolean }` keeps its fields MUTABLE. `buildPendingRecords` builds `const pathState = new Map<string, MutablePathState>()` and returns it as a bare object-literal property (`{ records: pendingRecords, pathState }`) against a return type annotated `ReadonlyMap<string, MutablePathState>` — TypeScript's structural typing widens `Map<K,V>` to `ReadonlyMap<K,V>` there with zero syntax, confirmed by `npx vitest run src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts`'s own typecheck pass ("Type Errors: no errors") and by the full-gate `tsc --noEmit` (host) reporting 0. `embedAndSwap` receives that same `ReadonlyMap` and only ever calls `.get`/iterates it (`pathState.get(relPath)`, `for (const [relPath, state] of pathState)`) — reading the map, then mutating the returned VALUE object's fields directly (`state.deleted = true`, `state.remaining -= 1`), which typechecks because `MutablePathState`'s fields aren't `readonly`, independent of the map's own read-only-ness. `grep -n "as " src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` shows no cast was added anywhere in this diff (the file's only two pre-existing `as` occurrences are `import { promises as fs }`, a module alias, not a type assertion).
2. **By-reference, not by-copy — the same `pathState` Map instance and the same `manifest` object reach both phases.** `pending.pathState` in `reindexFiles` is literally the `pathState` local `buildPendingRecords` constructed (no `new Map(pathState)`/spread anywhere in the return path); `manifest` is the same parameter object threaded unchanged into both calls (never destructured into a copy or reassigned). This is what lets phase 1's `pathState.set(relPath, { contentHash, remaining: recordCount, deleted: false })` value objects be the EXACT objects phase 2 later mutates and the scrub later reads — the sharing the split was asked to make explicit, not implicit.
3. **F6-1's 3-case golden — 0-EDIT, GREEN.** `git diff --stat src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts` for this task is empty. `npx vitest run src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts`: 3/3 passed, no type errors — all three ordered-`calls` sequences and all three final `manifest` objects (success, throw-in-batch-2, dispose-after-batch-1) are byte-identical to the pre-split behaviour, since the split moved code without reordering or renaming anything the golden observes.
4. **All other rag tests — 0-EDIT, GREEN.** `git diff --stat` for this task touches only `src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` and this ADR file. `npx vitest run src/rag/`: 22 files / 226 passed | 2 skipped (228), no type errors — `indexer.test.ts`, `chunker.test.ts`, `watchPipeline.test.ts`, and every other `src/rag/*.test.ts` file included, none edited.
5. **M-16 teeth-check — self-run before commit, per the brief's own instruction (point 4 asks the reviewer to re-run this; the implementer ran it first and reports the result honestly rather than assuming it flips).** At the exact phase boundary, `buildPendingRecords`'s return statement was mutated to `return { records: pendingRecords, pathState: new Map([...pathState].map(([k, v]) => [k, { ...v }])) };` — a deep copy of every value object, as the brief specifies verbatim. Re-ran `npx vitest run src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts`: **all 3 cases still passed — case (b) did NOT flip.** This is a genuine finding, not a mutation-testing failure to chase further: in THIS design, `buildPendingRecords` is the sole owner of `pathState` before it returns — nothing else in `reindexFiles` or elsewhere holds or compares against the pre-return map, so a deep copy taken at the very moment of return is observationally identical to the original (same keys, same field values, just different object identity) to every downstream reader (`embedAndSwap`, the scrub, the test's own `expect(manifest)` assertions operate on `manifest`, not on `pathState` object identity). M-16's "by-reference, no cast" requirement is still fully satisfied by the actual (non-mutated) implementation — sections 1-2 above prove that independently, with tsc and with the by-reference identity argument — but the golden bank does not (and structurally cannot, from outside the function) distinguish "phase-2 mutates the SAME objects phase-1 built" from "phase-2 mutates an up-front full clone of them," because both leave `manifest` in the identical end state the assertions check. The mutation was reverted immediately (`diff` against a pre-mutation backup copy confirmed byte-identical restoration) before any further work; `git diff --stat src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` for this task shows only the intended split, no trace of the mutation.
6. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none new.** `grep -nE '\bany\b'`, `grep -nE '\bas\s+[A-Za-z]'`, and `grep -nE '[A-Za-z0-9_)\]]!'` over the changed file return only comment-prose hits (the words "any"/"as" inside sentences) and the one pre-existing `import { promises as fs }` module alias — no real cast, no non-null assertion, no `any`, and no `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spread appears anywhere in the diff.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `buildPipeline.ts` is non-frozen.

**Byte-scan:** `buildPipeline.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.
