(One `npm run gate` run hit the pre-existing, brief-flagged `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` order-flake — a Windows temp-dir `ENOTEMPTY` race in that suite's own `afterEach` cleanup, unrelated to this task's files; `npx vitest run src/rag/indexer.test.ts` in isolation passed 53/2 skipped clean, and a second full `npm run gate` run came back fully green at the counts above.)

**★ FI-08 closed. `reindexFiles` reads as `ensure → buildPendingRecords → embedAndSwap`; the `pathState` hand-off between them is an explicit `ReadonlyMap<string, MutablePathState>` passed by reference, with no cast anywhere in the diff. WS-F6's golden (`buildPipeline.order.test.ts`) stays the contract F6-4..F6-9 must keep green with zero further edits. Finding for the controller/reviewer to adjudicate: the golden's black-box observation of `manifest` cannot by itself distinguish "shared value objects" from "a full value-copy taken at the hand-off" — M-16's by-reference guarantee rests on the code's own structure (sections 1-2), not on this test bank having teeth against that specific mutation.**

## ADR-025-AB — `IndexerContext` = intersection of role contexts; each function takes the narrowest Pick (task F6-4, WS-F6, FI-18, PURE TYPE refactor)

**Outcome (task F6-4, WS-F6, FI-18):** `IndexerContext` (`a838a89`'s `buildPipeline.ts:119-142`) was an 18-member bag every one of `walk`/`buildPendingRecords`/`embedAndSwap`/`reindexFiles`/`runBuild` took WHOLE regardless of how many of those 18 members its own body actually reads. Ground-truthed every function's exact field references (`grep -n 'ctx\.'` over `buildPipeline.ts`, cross-checked against `watchPipeline.ts`/`indexer.ts` for the two members ((`logger`, `recordFailedIncrementalReindex`)) that turned out to be used ONLY outside this file) and built the role→member map from that, not from the brief's own illustrative (and, on inspection, not-fully-accurate) example signatures.

1. **Bootstrapping, resolved via a private base bag (brief option (a)).** A module-private `interface IndexerContextBag { …the same 18 members, byte-identical field-for-field… }` replaces the old `export interface IndexerContext`. Four role types are `Pick<IndexerContextBag, …>`:
   - `export type IndexerStoreCtx = Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'store' | 'gate' | 'ensureStoreInitialized'>` — the store-lifecycle role.
   - `export type IndexerManifestCtx = Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'readManifest' | 'writeManifest' | 'readMeta' | 'writeMeta' | 'computeEffectiveWidth' | 'fingerprintMatches'>` — the manifest/fingerprint-sidecar role.
   - `export type IndexerIgnoreCtx = Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'loadIgnoreFilter' | 'invalidateIgnoreFilterCache' | 'setKnownNestedIgnoreDirs'>` — the ignore-filter role.
   - `export type IndexerLogCtx = Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'logger'>` — the logging role (used only by `watchPipeline.ts`, never referenced anywhere in `buildPipeline.ts`).
   `export type IndexerContext = IndexerStoreCtx & IndexerManifestCtx & IndexerIgnoreCtx & IndexerLogCtx & Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'opts' | 'parser' | 'embedder' | 'isDisposed' | 'recordFailedIncrementalReindex'>` is the intersection — every one of the 18 members appears in exactly one role or the residual Pick, so `IndexerContext` is structurally the SAME 18-member shape as before (no member added, dropped, or retyped). No circular reference: the roles are built from `IndexerContextBag`, never from `IndexerContext` itself.
2. **Per-function Pick, grounded by usage, not by the brief's own illustrative signatures.** The brief's suggested examples (e.g. `embedAndSwap(ctx: IndexerStoreCtx & IndexerLogCtx & …)`) turned out NOT to match real usage on inspection — `embedAndSwap` never reads `ctx.logger` — so those examples were treated as illustrative only, per the brief's own "GROUND-TRUTH... do NOT over-narrow... do NOT widen" instruction, and the actual Picks below were built from `grep -n 'ctx\.'` over each function body instead:
   - `walk(ctx: Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'opts'>, …)` — reads only `ctx.opts.workspaceRoot` (`:183-184,204` pre-refactor numbering).
   - `buildPendingRecords(ctx: IndexerStoreCtx & Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'isDisposed' | 'parser' | 'opts'>, …)` — `ctx.isDisposed()`, `ctx.gate.sink(() => ctx.store.deleteByPath(...))` (both branches), `ctx.parser` (chunker call), `ctx.opts.maxChunkTokens`.
   - `embedAndSwap(ctx: IndexerStoreCtx & Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'embedder' | 'isDisposed'>, …)` — `ctx.embedder.embed`, `ctx.isDisposed()`, `ctx.gate.sink(() => ctx.store.{deleteByPath,upsert})`.
   - `reindexFiles(ctx: IndexerStoreCtx & Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'isDisposed' | 'parser' | 'opts' | 'embedder'>, …)` — `ctx.ensureStoreInitialized()` directly, plus the union of what its two callees (`buildPendingRecords`, `embedAndSwap`, both called with this same `ctx`) need — `IndexerStoreCtx` already covers `gate`/`store`/`ensureStoreInitialized` for both.
   - `runBuild(ctx: IndexerStoreCtx & IndexerManifestCtx & IndexerIgnoreCtx & Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'opts' | 'parser' | 'embedder' | 'isDisposed'>): Promise<void>` — the store/manifest/ignore roles ALL directly (every member of each is read somewhere in `runBuild`'s body), `opts`/`isDisposed` directly, `parser`/`embedder` transitively (passed on to `walk`/`reindexFiles`). Deliberately narrower than the brief's own suggested "likely the full intersection" for the orchestrator: `runBuild` never reads `ctx.logger` or `ctx.recordFailedIncrementalReindex` (both are `watchPipeline.ts`-only members), so `IndexerLogCtx` and that residual field are correctly absent even from this biggest function's Pick.
3. **Minimality spot-check (review focus (b)) — self-run before commit, all 3 confirmed tsc-error on drop, then reverted:**
   - Dropped `IndexerManifestCtx` from `runBuild`'s Pick → 7 `TS2339` errors (`readManifest`/`readMeta`/`fingerprintMatches`/`computeEffectiveWidth`/`writeManifest`×2/`writeMeta`), confirming the role is load-bearing there, not decorative.
   - Dropped `IndexerStoreCtx` from `buildPendingRecords`'s Pick → 4 `TS2339` errors (`gate`/`store`, both call sites), confirming that role is load-bearing there.
   - Dropped `embedder` from `reindexFiles`'s residual Pick → `TS2345` at its `embedAndSwap(ctx, …)` call site (`ctx`'s narrowed type no longer assignable to what `embedAndSwap` requires), confirming a function's Pick must cover not just its OWN direct reads but every callee it threads `ctx` into.
   All three reverts confirmed byte-identical to the pre-mutation file via `diff` against a backup copy before the real commit; `git diff --stat` for this task shows only the intended 5-signature change, no trace of the mutation round-trips.
4. **`IndexerContext` structurally unchanged — `indexer.ts`/`watchPipeline.ts` 0-edit.** `git diff --stat` for this task touches only `src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` (+ this ADR); `indexer.ts`'s `const ctx: IndexerContext = { opts, store, embedder, gate, parser, logger, isDisposed: …, …, recordFailedIncrementalReindex: … }` (its own full 18-key literal) and `watchPipeline.ts`'s two functions taking `ctx: IndexerContext` both compile unedited — the exported `IndexerContext` name still resolves to the same 18-member shape, just now assembled via `&` instead of listed inline in one `interface` body.
5. **ZERO runtime/behaviour change.** No function body line changed — every edit is a parameter TYPE annotation swap; the exact same `ctx` object (`indexer.ts`'s one literal) is passed to every call site, unchanged. `npx vitest run src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts`: F6-1's 3-case golden — 0-EDIT, GREEN (3/3 passed, no type errors). `npx vitest run src/rag/`: 22 files / 226 passed | 2 skipped (228), no type errors — every other `src/rag/*.test.ts` file, none edited.
6. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none new.** `git diff src/rag/buildPipeline.ts | grep '^+'` scanned for `\bany\b`, `\bas\s+[A-Za-z]`, and a trailing non-null `!` — the only `as` hit is comment prose ("satisfied ... as it satisfied"), not a type assertion; no `any`, no non-null assertion, no spread anywhere in the diff.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `buildPipeline.ts` is non-frozen.

**Byte-scan:** `buildPipeline.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**Count discipline:** baseline (`a838a89`, F6-3 HEAD) = **348 test files passed, 7638 passed | 15 skipped (7653), tsc 0 (host + webview).** This task is a pure type refactor — 0 tests added, 0 removed, 0 production files added or removed — full-gate run after this task: **348 test files passed, 7638 passed | 15 skipped (7653), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: none.

## ADR-025-AC — `readMeta` classifies and logs like `readManifest`; `isWellFormedMeta` replaces the blind `as IndexMeta` cast (task F6-5, WS-F6, FI-19)

**Outcome (task F6-5, WS-F6, FI-19):** at `a923883` (F6-4 HEAD), `readMeta` (`indexer.ts:319-322`, pre-fix numbering) was a bare `return JSON.parse(await fs.readFile(metaPath, 'utf8')) as IndexMeta;` with a plain `catch { return undefined }` — no errno classification (ENOENT vs any other read failure), no logging of a non-ENOENT read failure, and no validation of the parsed shape (the `as IndexMeta` cast trusted whatever `JSON.parse` produced). Its sibling `readManifest` (`:264-301`) already classifies errno, logs non-ENOENT failures and shape/parse corruption (both name-only, never the path/raw err), and validates shape via `isRecord` before trusting the parsed value. `readMeta` now mirrors all three behaviours.

1. **`isWellFormedMeta(x: unknown): x is IndexMeta`** — a new module-level structural validator (placed directly after the `IndexMeta` interface). Re-grounded `IndexMeta`'s exact 5 fields on HEAD (`schema: 2`, `embedModel: string`, `dims: number`, `width?: number`, `scannerVersion?: number`) and validates all five: `isRecord(x)` (reuses the same shared guard `readManifest` already imports from `../shared/typeGuards` — no new import needed) + `typeof x.schema === 'number'` (TYPE only, not the literal `2` — `fingerprintMatches` already treats any `!== 2` value as a downstream mismatch, so re-checking the literal here would be redundant) + `typeof x.embedModel === 'string'` + `typeof x.dims === 'number'` + `(x.width === undefined || typeof x.width === 'number')` + `(x.scannerVersion === undefined || typeof x.scannerVersion === 'number')` (both optional fields accept absence or a number, matching their `?:` declarations).
2. **`readMeta` rewritten to the `readManifest` shape exactly as the brief specified:** a `try { raw = await fs.readFile(...) }` first pass whose `catch (err)` classifies errno — `!(err instanceof Error && 'code' in err && (err as { code?: string }).code === 'ENOENT')` (the SAME errno-narrowing idiom `readManifest` already uses, verbatim) logs `` `hermes-codebase: meta read failed (${err.name}) — rebuilding` `` for any non-ENOENT failure and stays silent for ENOENT, both returning `undefined`; then a second `try { JSON.parse → isWellFormedMeta → return parsed } catch { log 'corrupt (parse error)'; return undefined }`, with a `'corrupt (bad shape)'` log on a failed `isWellFormedMeta` check. **The blind `as IndexMeta` cast is GONE** — `isWellFormedMeta` proves the type via its `x is IndexMeta` predicate; the function's return type is `IndexMeta | undefined` unchanged.
3. **No caller changed.** `readMeta`'s contract (`Promise<IndexMeta | undefined>`) is unchanged; `undefined` still means "no/invalid meta" to both call sites (`buildPipeline.ts:706`'s `runBuild` and `watchPipeline.ts:203`), both of which pass the result straight into `fingerprintMatches(storedMeta)` — `fingerprintMatches(undefined)` was already `false` before this task and still is, so a corrupt/unreadable/missing meta still forces the same full-recompute path it always did. `writeMeta`'s doc comment (`:396`, "readMeta already treats any read/parse failure as a rebuild") stays true.
4. **RED-first tests (`indexer.test.ts`, appended to the existing F2-13 describe block):** 4 new `it`s exercise `readMeta` indirectly through `createIndexer(...).build()` (it has no direct export, same pattern the existing `readManifest`-corruption test above it uses) — (a) bad-shape meta (`dims` is a string — parses fine, only shape validation catches it) → exactly 1 log matching `/meta/i` + `/corrupt/i`, rebuild still happens (`upsertMock` called); (b) parse-error meta (`'{ this is not json'`) → same; (c) a non-ENOENT read error, simulated by `vi.spyOn(fs, 'readFile').mockImplementation` throwing a custom `FakeMetaReadError` (name `'FakeMetaReadError'`, message `'permission denied reading a secret path'`, `code: 'EACCES'`) only for the exact `manifest.meta.json` path (passing every other path through to the real `fs.readFile`) → exactly 1 log containing `'FakeMetaReadError'` and NOT containing the meta file's absolute path or the error's message, rebuild still happens; (d) ENOENT (no meta file at all — ordinary first build) → zero `/meta/i` logs. **Verified genuinely RED on the pre-fix code:** ran `npx vitest run src/rag/indexer.test.ts -t readMeta` before touching `indexer.ts` — (a)/(b)/(c) failed with `expected +0 to be 1` (the old code never logs, period — a bad-shape sidecar parses "successfully" and is cast blindly; a parse-error and every read errno fall into the same silent `catch { return undefined }`); (d) passed trivially (the old code's total silence already satisfies "zero meta logs" for the one case that doesn't need logging) — expected per the brief ("ENOENT -> no log" holds on both old and new code; the RED signal lives in (a)-(c)). After the fix, all 4 pass.
5. **Existing tests, 0-edit.** No existing `readMeta`/`readManifest`/`D-2` fingerprint test asserted the old no-log/no-validate behaviour (the `D-2` describe block's meta-fixture tests assert on `recomputedPaths()`/the written sidecar shape, never on log silence for a corrupt/errno read), so none needed updating. F6-1's `buildPipeline.order.test.ts` golden is buildPipeline-scoped and unaffected: 0-edit, green.
6. **`any`/`!`/spread/new-`as`-cast — none new.** The only cast in the diff is the pre-existing, explicitly-sanctioned errno narrowing `(err as { code?: string })` — copied verbatim from `readManifest`'s own established idiom, not a new pattern. The removed `as IndexMeta` cast is the one cast this task deletes. The RED test's `fs.readFile` mock needed no cast at all — typing the mock's callback params as inferred (`(file, options) => …`) and calling `realReadFile(file, options)` straight through compiles clean under `tsc --noEmit` (confirmed directly, exit 0), so the file avoids even the `as never`/`as unknown as never[]` passthrough idiom an earlier `fs.readFile`-mocking test elsewhere in this same file already uses for an analogous purpose.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `indexer.ts`/`indexer.test.ts` are both non-frozen.

**Byte-scan:** `indexer.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0. `indexer.test.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**Count discipline:** baseline (`a923883`, F6-4 HEAD) = **348 test files passed, 7638 passed | 15 skipped (7653), tsc 0 (host + webview)** (per ADR-025-AB's own after-count, 0 delta from F6-3). Full-gate run after this task: **348 test files passed, 7642 passed | 15 skipped (7657), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: +4 tests (the 4 new `readMeta` cases above), 0 files added/removed, 0 skipped-count change.

**★ FI-19 closed. `readMeta` now classifies read errno (ENOENT silent, any other error logged with `err.name` only — never the path or raw err) and validates the parsed shape via `isWellFormedMeta` (corrupt shape or parse failure both logged then treated as "no meta"), mirroring `readManifest` exactly; the blind `as IndexMeta` cast is gone, proven instead of asserted; no caller behaviour changed (`undefined` → full-recompute rebuild, unchanged via `fingerprintMatches`).**

**★ FI-18 closed. `IndexerContext` is the intersection of four named role `Pick`s (`IndexerStoreCtx`/`IndexerManifestCtx`/`IndexerIgnoreCtx`/`IndexerLogCtx`) plus a small residual `Pick`, assembled from a private `IndexerContextBag` base with no circular type definition; every one of the five functions in `buildPipeline.ts` now declares only the roles its own body (or, for `reindexFiles`/`runBuild`, its own body plus what it threads `ctx` into) actually needs, each confirmed minimal by a mutate-and-revert spot-check, not by inspection alone. `IndexerContext`'s public shape and `createIndexer`'s bag construction are untouched.**

## ADR-025-AD — chunker/parser log through the injected seam; walk and hash-pass log non-ENOENT errno names (task F6-6, WS-F6, FI-20 + FI-31)

**Outcome (task F6-6, WS-F6, FI-20 + FI-31):** at `c6c244f` (F6-5 HEAD), three sink classes in the RAG indexer violated the injected-logger/err.name-only discipline `readManifest`/`readMeta` (F2-13/F6-5) already establish: (1) `chunker.ts` `chunkFile`'s AST-failure catch (`:47-53`) called `console.error(\`... AST chunking failed for ${opts.relPath} ...\`, err)` — the workspace-relative PATH and the RAW error object, straight to the process's stderr, bypassing the injected-logger seam entirely; (2) `parser/WebTreeSitterParser.ts`'s `loadLanguage` grammar-load catch (`:172`) called `console.error(\`... for ${languageId}\`, err)` — the raw error object (its `.message` can carry filesystem detail) via `console.error`, same bypass; (3) `buildPipeline.ts`'s `walk` (the readdir catch and the two nested `.gitignore`/`.hermesignore` read catches) and `runBuild`'s hash pass read catch all swallowed EVERY failure silently (`catch { return; }` / `catch { ... }` / `catch { continue; }`), with no ENOENT/non-ENOENT distinction at all. All five sites now go through an injected `logger`, carrying `err.name` ONLY (never a path, never the raw `err`, never file content) — mirroring `readManifest`/`readMeta`'s `(${err.name})` idiom verbatim.

1. **`chunker.ts` — `ChunkFileOptions` gains a REQUIRED `logger: (line: string) => void`.** The AST-failure catch (`chunkFile`, `:47-53` pre-fix) becomes `opts.logger(\`hermes-codebase: AST chunking failed (${err instanceof Error ? err.name : 'unknown'}), falling back to line windows\`);` — `opts.relPath` is gone from the message entirely, `console.error`/the raw `err` argument are gone, `opts.logger` replaces both.
2. **`parser/WebTreeSitterParser.ts` — `WebTreeSitterParserOptions` gains a REQUIRED `logger: (line: string) => void`.** `loadLanguage`'s grammar-load catch becomes `this.opts.logger(\`hermes-codebase: failed to load tree-sitter grammar for ${languageId} (${err instanceof Error ? err.name : 'unknown'})\`);` — `languageId` stays in the message (it's a language id, not a path/secret); the raw `err` second argument to `console.error` is gone. `indexer.ts`'s `createIndexer` wires its own `logger` (the same `console.error`-defaulting seam every other member of its `ctx` bag already shares) into the `WebTreeSitterParser` constructor call.
3. **`buildPipeline.ts` — a shared errno classifier, factored once.** Two small module-private helpers, `isEnoent(err: unknown): boolean` (the SAME `err instanceof Error && 'code' in err && (err as { code?: string }).code === 'ENOENT'` idiom `readManifest`/`readMeta` use inline, factored out here since this file has four such catches where `indexer.ts` has two) and `errName(err: unknown): string` (`err instanceof Error ? err.name : 'unknown'`), placed directly after `looksBinary`. All four of this file's catches now read: `walk`'s readdir catch → `hermes-codebase: directory read failed (${errName(err)})`; the nested `.gitignore` read catch → `hermes-codebase: nested .gitignore read failed (${errName(err)})`; the nested `.hermesignore` read catch → the parallel `.hermesignore` message; `runBuild`'s hash-pass read catch → `hermes-codebase: hash read failed (${errName(err)})`. Every one of the four is gated `if (!isEnoent(err)) { ctx.logger(...); }` — ENOENT (a directory/file that vanished or was never there, mid-walk or mid-hash-pass — the documented, ordinary "deleted between walk and read" case) stays completely silent; any other errno (EACCES, EIO, …) logs.
4. **Pick widenings, minimal, tsc-enforced, with one necessary cascade.** `walk`'s `ctx` param (`Pick<IndexerContextBag, 'opts'>`) gains `IndexerLogCtx` — it now reads `ctx.logger`. `buildPendingRecords`'s `ctx` param gains `IndexerLogCtx` — it now reads `ctx.logger` to pass `logger: ctx.logger` into its `chunkFile({...})` call site. Both are exactly the two widenings the brief named. `tsc --noEmit` then forced ONE further, un-named-but-necessary cascade: `reindexFiles` calls `buildPendingRecords(ctx, ...)` with its OWN `ctx` unchanged, so `reindexFiles`'s Pick had to gain `IndexerLogCtx` too for that call to typecheck; and `runBuild`'s Pick had to gain `IndexerLogCtx` both because its own hash-pass now reads `ctx.logger` directly and because it calls `walk(ctx, ...)`. All four widenings are additive-only (no field removed from any Pick) and every real caller (`indexer.ts`'s `createIndexer`, `watchPipeline.ts`'s functions, `buildPipeline.order.test.ts`'s golden harness) already passes the FULL `IndexerContext` bag, which already carries `logger` — so none of them needed a single line changed. `runBuild`'s doc comment (which used to say `logger`/`recordFailedIncrementalReindex` are "unused here … correctly absent from this Pick") is updated to note `logger` is now used directly.
5. **`chunkFile`'s call site** (`buildPendingRecords`, pre-fix `:407`) gains `logger: ctx.logger,` alongside its existing conditional `parser`/`maxChunkTokens` spread fields.
6. **RED-first tests, verified genuinely RED against the pre-fix code (not just written and trusted).** `chunker.test.ts`: one new test drives an AST-parse failure with a distinctly-named error and a secret-looking `relPath`/message, spies on `console.error`, and asserts the injected logger received exactly one line containing the error's `.name` and NEITHER the path nor the raw message, and that `console.error` was never called. `WebTreeSitterParser.test.ts`: one new test drives a `Language.load` rejection the same way (grammar-load arm); the pre-existing `Parser.init()`-rejection test is STRENGTHENED (not weakened) — it already spied on `console.error` to silence noise but asserted nothing about it, so it now also asserts the injected logger received exactly one `err.name`-bearing line and `console.error` was never called. `indexer.test.ts` (new `F6-6` describe block, same file/idiom as F6-5's `readMeta` errno test): one test spies `fs.readdir` to fail one sibling directory with a custom-named EACCES error and another with ENOENT, driving a real `createIndexer(...).build()`, and asserts exactly one `directory read failed` log (the EACCES one, `err.name` only, no path, no raw message) while the ENOENT sibling stays silent and the rest of the tree still indexes; a second test does the parallel thing for the hash pass via `fs.readFile`. **Verified genuinely RED:** `git stash push -- src/rag/buildPipeline.ts` (isolating just that file's fix), re-ran `npx vitest run src/rag/indexer.test.ts -t F6-6` — both new tests failed with `expected +0 to be 1` (the old code logs nothing, full stop) — then `git stash pop` restored the fix and both went green. The chunker/parser tests were confirmed RED the ordinary way (written against the untouched source before its own fix landed, one file at a time).
7. **Existing tests, 0-edit except the one legitimate strengthening.** Every pre-existing `chunkFile(...)`/`new WebTreeSitterParser({...})` call site across `chunker.test.ts`, `WebTreeSitterParser.test.ts`, `WebTreeSitterParser.initMemo.test.ts`, and `WebTreeSitterParser.real.test.ts` gained a `logger: () => {}` (or a real capturing logger, for the one strengthened test) field — mechanical, required by the now-mandatory `logger` field, no assertions touched. No existing test anywhere asserted that the removed `console.error` calls fired (a grep of the pre-fix `console.error`/`AST chunking failed`/`failed to load tree-sitter` strings across every test file found none), so there was nothing to "repoint" beyond the one test named in item 6. F6-1's `buildPipeline.order.test.ts` golden bank is 0-edit and green (its harness's `ctx.logger` stub was never reachable before this task and stays unreachable — `buildPendingRecords`'s new `logger: ctx.logger` pass-through to `chunkFile` is only ever invoked on an AST-parse THROW, which this golden's `.dat`-extension fixtures never trigger).
8. **Scope note (FI-20).** The following `console.*` sinks in `src/` are deliberately OUT of FI-20's scope, not missed: `indexer.ts`'s own `logger` seam still DEFAULTS to `console.error` when a caller doesn't wire one (`opts.logger ?? ((line) => console.error(line))`) — that default is the seam itself, not a violation of it; `backendFactory.ts:42` and `nextedit/backend.ts:100` are intended once-only user-facing warnings (F10-2's `OnceRegistry`, a different concern — user notification, not log hygiene); `CheckpointTracker.ts` uses its own `errCode()` classifier, already err.name/code-shaped; `codebase-server.ts` is the MCP child process's own stderr stream (not the extension host's log surface `readManifest`/`readMeta`/this task's five sites all write to). This note may also be read from ADR-025-E (F9's kickoff ADR) if a future task revisits FI-20's full scope; it is recorded here since F6-6 is the task that actually closes FI-20's last violation inside `src/rag`.
9. **`any`/`!`/spread/new-`as`-cast — none new.** The only cast in the diff is `isEnoent`'s `(err as { code?: string })` — copied verbatim from `readManifest`/`readMeta`'s own established, previously-sanctioned idiom (ADR-025-AC item 6 already calls this "not a new pattern"; F6-6 factors the SAME idiom into a shared helper rather than re-inlining it a fourth time), not a new unsafe cast. No `catch (e: any)` anywhere — every new catch is the bare `catch (err)` this codebase's `useUnknownInCatchVariables` already makes `unknown`, narrowed via `instanceof Error`. No new non-null assertions, no `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spreads.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `chunker.ts`/`WebTreeSitterParser.ts`/`buildPipeline.ts`/`indexer.ts` and their test files are all non-frozen.

**Byte-scan (all 9 touched files):** `buildPipeline.ts`, `chunker.ts`, `chunker.test.ts`, `indexer.ts`, `indexer.test.ts`, `parser/WebTreeSitterParser.ts`, `parser/WebTreeSitterParser.test.ts`, `parser/WebTreeSitterParser.initMemo.test.ts`, `parser/WebTreeSitterParser.real.test.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all nine).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`c6c244f`, F6-5 HEAD, per ADR-025-AC's own after-count) = **348 test files passed, 7642 passed | 15 skipped (7657), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: +4 tests (1 in `chunker.test.ts`, 1 in `WebTreeSitterParser.test.ts`, 2 in `indexer.test.ts`), 0 files added/removed, 0 skipped-count change.

**★ FI-20 + FI-31 closed (for `src/rag`'s chunker/parser/build-pipeline sinks — see item 8's scope note for what's deliberately out of scope elsewhere). Every log message this task touches carries `err.name` ONLY — never a path, never a raw `err`, never file content — through an injected logger, never `console.error`/`console.log`. `chunkFile`'s AST-failure arm and `WebTreeSitterParser`'s grammar-load arm no longer call `console.error` at all (spy-proven, not just asserted). `walk`'s readdir/nested-ignore-file catches and `runBuild`'s hash-pass catch all now distinguish ENOENT (silent, the ordinary vanished-mid-walk/read case) from every other errno (logged). The `IndexerLogCtx` Pick widening propagates minimally and only where `tsc` forced it (`walk`, `buildPendingRecords`, and the two-hop cascade into `reindexFiles`/`runBuild`), with zero real-caller changes since every caller already threads the full `IndexerContext` bag.**

## ADR-025-AE — `fitOrDegrade` extracted; `firstChildOfType` undefined sentinel; batch size owned by the embedder (task F6-7, WS-F6, FI-28 + FI-40 + FI-29)

**Outcome (task F6-7, WS-F6, FI-28 + FI-40 + FI-29):** at `ae1fe73` (F6-6b HEAD), three small, unrelated-but-adjacent RAG nits, all behaviour-preserving. **FI-28:** `astChunker.ts`'s `constructFunctionDefinitionChunk` duplicated its degrade tail verbatim at both the isInClass branch (after the `combined` check) and the plain non-class branch — `funcText` fits → return it; else the first signature line + collapsed body ("minimal") fits → return that; else return the bare collapsed body. **FI-40:** `firstChildOfType` was typed `SyntaxNodeLike | null` (`return … ?? null`) when every one of its two call sites already tests the result with plain truthiness (`if (block)` / `if (grandChild)`) and the rest of the module already standardizes on `undefined` as its "nothing found" sentinel (`extractOwnName`, `smartCollapsedChunks`'s constructor lookup, `buildSymbolPath`'s loop). **FI-29:** the embed batch size lived in TWO places that had to be kept in sync by convention alone — `buildPipeline.ts`'s own `EMBED_BATCH_SIZE = 64` module constant (used by `embedAndSwap`'s batch loop) and `HttpEmbedder`'s private `batchSize` (used by its own per-HTTP-request batching) — with no code tying them together.

1. **`fitOrDegrade(funcText, signature, collapsedBody, max): string`** — a new module-private function in `astChunker.ts`, placed directly above `constructFunctionDefinitionChunk`, containing EXACTLY the shared tail (verbatim, no logic change): `estimateTokenCount(funcText) <= max` → return `funcText`; else compute `minimal` from `signature`'s first line + `collapsedBody` and return it if it fits; else return `collapsedBody`. Both call sites in `constructFunctionDefinitionChunk` now read `return fitOrDegrade(funcText, signature, collapsedBody, maxChunkTokens);` — the isInClass branch calls it only after its own `combined` (class-header-prefixed) form fails to fit; the plain branch calls it as its entire body. **Zero behaviour change** — same three-rung ladder, same order, same values, just named and shared instead of copy-pasted.
2. **`firstChildOfType`'s return type flips to `SyntaxNodeLike | undefined`**, and its body drops the `?? null` fallback (`Array.prototype.find` already returns `undefined` for "no match", so the `?? null` was purely a re-wrap). Re-grounded EVERY call site in the file (both are inside `collapseChildren`): `const block = firstChildOfType(node, blockTypes); if (block) { … }` and `const grandChild = firstChildOfType(child, collapseBlockTypes); if (grandChild) { … }` — both already used truthiness, not an explicit `!== null`/`=== null` comparison, so **neither call site needed an edit**; `undefined` is exactly as falsy as `null` under `if (x)`. A repo-wide grep confirmed these are the only two call sites of `firstChildOfType` in `src/`.
3. **`Embedder` (`embedder.ts`) gains a required `readonly batchSize: number` field.** `HttpEmbedder.batchSize` (already computed as `opts.batchSize ?? 64` in the constructor) changes `private readonly` → bare `readonly` (public) so the class satisfies the widened interface and so `buildPipeline.ts` can read it; its own internal per-HTTP-request batching loop (`embed`'s `for (i += this.batchSize)`) is untouched. `buildPipeline.ts` DELETES its `EMBED_BATCH_SIZE = 64` module constant entirely; `embedAndSwap`'s batch loop (re-anchored post-F6-6 at `:534-535`) becomes `for (let i = 0; i < records.length; i += ctx.embedder.batchSize) { const batch = records.slice(i, i + ctx.embedder.batchSize); … }` — `ctx.embedder` was already in `embedAndSwap`'s `Pick` (F6-4/ADR-025-AB), so no Pick widening was needed. The batch size is now single-sourced on whichever `Embedder` a caller wires in, with `HttpEmbedder`'s default (64) the only place "64" is still spelled out in production code.
4. **Fixture edits — every fake `Embedder` in the tree gains `batchSize` (mechanical, required by the now-wider interface); the F6-1 golden's is the one DECLARED exception with semantic weight.** Re-grounded every `Embedder`-typed object literal and every module-level `HttpEmbedder` mock in `src/` (a `type Embedder`/`import.*Embedder` grep across the whole repo, cross-checked against every fake-embedder helper): (a) **`buildPipeline.order.test.ts`'s `buildHarness` embedder** (the F6-1 golden, ADR-025-Y) gains `batchSize: 64` — exactly the ONE change this task's own forward-compatibility note (critic M-7, written into that file at F6-1 time) authorized; 64 preserves the golden's pinned 2-batch (64 + 6) split, and every ordered-call/manifest assertion in all three of its cases (`a`/`b`/`c`) stayed byte-identical (re-run green, 0 edits to any `expect(...)`). (b) **`indexer.test.ts`'s `vi.mock('./embedder', …)` class** (`HttpEmbedder: class { embed = embedMock; }`) gains `batchSize = 64;` — this one is NOT merely a type-satisfaction fixture: since `vi.mock` substitutes the real `HttpEmbedder` at runtime, `buildPipeline.ts`'s new `ctx.embedder.batchSize` read would otherwise see `undefined` inside every one of this file's ~50+ tests that exercise a real `build()`/`watch()` cycle (`i += undefined` is `NaN`, an infinite loop) — 64 matches `HttpEmbedder`'s own real default and keeps this file's explicit 64/65-chunk batch-boundary tests (e.g. "Rev-1 A3: a file whose OWN chunks span two embed batches…") passing unchanged. `indexer.embedderArming.test.ts` deliberately drives the REAL `HttpEmbedder` (no `vi.mock('./embedder', …)`, per its own header comment) so it needed no fixture edit at all — the real class's new-public `batchSize` field is populated by its existing constructor logic. (c) **`src/mcp/search.test.ts`'s four `Embedder`-typed fakes** (the `fakeEmbedder()` helper plus three inline literals in the A-05 Qwen3-instruction-prefix describe block) each gain `batchSize: 64` — `runCodebaseSearch` never reads it (a single-query embed call, not a batched index build), so this is a pure type-satisfaction fixture with zero behavioural weight; no assertion in that file changed.
5. **Mutation checks (actually re-run against the live code, not just asserted in prose).** (a) Reordering `fitOrDegrade` to return `collapsedBody` unconditionally first (a temporary `return collapsedBody;` inserted as the function's first line): re-ran `npx vitest run src/rag/chunk/astChunker.test.ts` and got exactly 2 failures — "collapses an oversized top-level function…" (expected `'function foo() { ... }'`, got `'{ ... }'`) and "collapses an oversized class but still recurses into its methods…" (expected `'methodA() { ... }'`, got `'{ ... }'`) — then reverted; re-ran clean (12/12). (b) Setting the F6-1 golden fake's `batchSize` to `1000` (temporary edit, one batch of 70 instead of two): re-ran `npx vitest run src/rag/buildPipeline.order.test.ts` and got **all 3** cases failing, not just case (a) — case (a)'s `calls` collapses to one `embed:70`/`upsert:70` pair instead of the pinned two-batch sequence; case (b) never rejects at all (the simulated failure was scripted for embed CALL 2, which no longer exists at `batchSize: 1000`); case (c)'s dispose-sequence assertion also flips (`embed:70` where `embed:64`+`embed:6` was pinned) — then reverted; re-ran clean (3/3). Both mutations confirm the extraction/single-sourcing is load-bearing, not decorative.
6. **`any`/`!`/spread/new-`as`-cast — none new.** No cast of any kind appears in this task's diff (FI-40's `?? null` removal is a pure deletion, not a cast change). No `catch (e: any)` — this task adds no new catch blocks. No new non-null assertions. No `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spread.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `astChunker.ts`, `embedder.ts`, `buildPipeline.ts`, and their test files are all non-frozen.

**Byte-scan (all 6 touched files):** `astChunker.ts`, `embedder.ts`, `buildPipeline.ts`, `buildPipeline.order.test.ts`, `indexer.test.ts`, `search.test.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all six).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`ae1fe73`, F6-6b HEAD; F6-6b strengthened 2 existing `indexer.test.ts` assertions with no test-count change, so this carries forward ADR-025-AD's own after-count unchanged) = **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: 0 (pure extraction/sentinel-flip/single-sourcing — no new tests were needed or added; every RED-first signal for this task's mutation checks above was verified transiently against the EXISTING suite, not committed as new cases).

**★ FI-28 closed.** `fitOrDegrade` replaces the duplicated degrade-ladder tail at both `constructFunctionDefinitionChunk` call sites with one verbatim shared function; `astChunker.test.ts`'s 12 tests are byte-identical (0 assertion edits), and a temporary order-mutation was confirmed to flip 2 of them before being reverted.
**★ FI-40 closed.** `firstChildOfType` returns `SyntaxNodeLike | undefined` (the module's majority sentinel already used everywhere else); its `?? null` fallback is gone, and both call sites needed no edit since they already used truthiness, not an explicit `null` comparison.
**★ FI-29 closed.** The embed batch size is single-sourced on whichever `Embedder` a caller wires in (`Embedder.batchSize`, `readonly number`, required); `HttpEmbedder.batchSize` is now public (default 64, unchanged); `buildPipeline.ts`'s `EMBED_BATCH_SIZE` module constant is deleted; `embedAndSwap` batches by `ctx.embedder.batchSize`. Every fake `Embedder` across `src/` (`buildPipeline.order.test.ts`'s F6-1 golden, `indexer.test.ts`'s runtime `vi.mock`, `search.test.ts`'s four fakes) was re-grounded and given `batchSize` — the F6-1 golden's `64` is the one semantically-load-bearing edit (preserves its pinned 2-batch shape, confirmed by a batchSize→1000 mutation flipping it), every other addition is a mechanical type-satisfaction fixture with 0 assertion changes.

## ADR-025-AF — `hybridSearch` reads as five named steps (task F6-8, WS-F6, FI-30)

**Outcome (task F6-8, WS-F6, FI-30):** at `b731176` (F6-7 HEAD), `LanceDBStore.ts`'s `hybridSearch` (`:438-521`) was a ~85-line method mixing table-resolution, predicate-building, the dual-leg `Promise.allSettled` query, per-leg row accounting, the one-shot FTS repair, and fusion into a single body. Four private helpers are extracted VERBATIM from the inline code, so the method body now reads as five named steps ending in `fuseHybridRows`. Purely behaviour-preserving — no test assertion changed.

1. **`resolveTable(): Promise<lancedb.Table>`** — the reopen-or-throw step (pre-fix `:444-451`): `if (!this.table) await this.tryReopenTable(); if (!this.table) throw new IndexNotReadyError(); return this.table;`. Returning the non-null table directly (rather than leaving the caller to re-read `this.table`) is what preserves TypeScript's not-undefined narrowing across `hybridSearch`'s later `await Promise.allSettled(...)` — a fresh local bound to this method's declared `Promise<lancedb.Table>` return type is narrowed by its own type, whereas narrowing on the mutable field `this.table` does not persist across an `await` point (the pre-fix code's own `const table = this.table;` comment explained exactly this; the comment now lives on `resolveTable` instead). **No `as`-cast anywhere in this extraction** — the narrowing falls out of the method's return type by construction.
2. **`buildPredicate(filter?: SearchFilter): string`** — the predicate-building step (pre-fix `:457`), verbatim: `return filter?.language ? \`language = '${escapeSqlLiteral(filter.language)}'\` : 'true';`.
3. **`accountRows(raw: unknown[]): { rows: StoredRow[]; dropped: number }`** — the per-leg row-accounting step, verbatim: `const rows = raw.filter(isStoredRow); return { rows, dropped: raw.length - rows.length };`. No `label` parameter was added (the pre-fix code never labelled per-leg drops — it summed both legs' drops and called `warnMalformedRowsOnce` exactly once), so the signature stays exactly what the behaviour needs.
4. **`attemptFtsRepairOnce(table: lancedb.Table, reason: unknown): void`** — the one-shot FTS repair step (pre-fix `:499-511`), verbatim: sets `this.ftsRepairAttempted = true`, logs the degrade-visibly line (`reason instanceof Error ? reason.name : 'unknown'`), then fires the fire-and-forget `table.createIndex('content', { config: lancedb.Index.fts() })` repair with its own failure-logging `.catch`. The `ftsRepairAttempted` gate stays at the CALL SITE (`else if (!this.ftsRepairAttempted) this.attemptFtsRepairOnce(table, ftsOutcome.reason);`), exactly preserving the once-per-instance semantics — the caller decides whether to invoke the helper at all, so the flag flips to `true` at most once per instance regardless of how many searches run.
5. **`hybridSearch`'s body becomes five named steps:** `const table = await this.resolveTable();` → `const predicate = this.buildPredicate(filter);` → the verbatim `Promise.allSettled([...])` dual-leg query (both leg expressions untouched, including the multi-line `nearestToText` chain's formatting) → the vec-rejected-throws-loud check (`if (vecOutcome.status === 'rejected') throw vecOutcome.reason;`) followed by `const { rows: vecRows, dropped: vecDropped } = this.accountRows(vecOutcome.value);` → the fts-fulfilled/degrade branch (`if (ftsOutcome.status === 'fulfilled') ({ rows: ftsRows, dropped: ftsDropped } = this.accountRows(ftsOutcome.value)); else if (!this.ftsRepairAttempted) this.attemptFtsRepairOnce(table, ftsOutcome.reason);`) → the combined drop accounting (`if (vecDropped + ftsDropped > 0) this.warnMalformedRowsOnce(vecDropped + ftsDropped);`) → `return fuseHybridRows(vecRows, ftsRows, k);`. `candidateLimit` is computed inline exactly as before (`Math.max(k * 3, 50)`), unchanged.
6. **Mutation checks (actually re-run against the live code, not just asserted in prose).** (a) Removed `this.ftsRepairAttempted = true;` from `attemptFtsRepairOnce` (so the flag never flips): re-ran `npx vitest run src/rag/store/LanceDBStore.test.ts` and got exactly 2 failures — "attempts the lazy createIndex repair at most once across two searches, even when both fail" (expected `createIndex` called 1 time, got 2) and "logs the FTS failure exactly once per store instance, not once per search" (expected `console.error` called 1 time, got 5) — then reverted; re-ran clean (23/23). (b) Made the FTS-rejected branch `throw ftsOutcome.reason;` instead of calling `attemptFtsRepairOnce` (degrade → throw): re-ran the same file and got 3 failures, including "degrades to vector-only results when the FTS leg rejects, instead of killing the whole search" (now throws `FTS index missing` instead of returning vector-only hits) plus the two repair-once tests (the repair function is never reached at all under this mutation) — then reverted; re-ran clean (23/23, byte-identical file confirmed via `diff` against the pre-mutation backup).
7. **`any`/`!`/spread/new-`as`-cast — none new.** No cast of any kind appears in this task's diff (`resolveTable`'s return preserves the narrowing without one). No `catch (e: any)` — this task adds no new catch blocks. No new non-null assertions. No `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spread. No literal `any` type anywhere in the touched method or its four new helpers (`accountRows`'s `raw: unknown[]` parameter accepts `.toArray()`'s `any[]` return by ordinary structural assignability at the call site, exactly as the pre-fix code's own `const vecRaw: unknown[] = vecOutcome.value;` local annotation did — the squelch-point just moved from a local variable to a parameter's declared type).

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `LanceDBStore.ts` is non-frozen; its test file (`LanceDBStore.test.ts`) is untouched (0-EDIT, per the brief's behaviour-preserving requirement).

**Byte-scan (1 touched file):** `LanceDBStore.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**Count discipline:** baseline (`b731176`, F6-7 HEAD, per ADR-025-AE's own after-count) = **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: 0 (pure extraction — no test was added, edited, or removed; `LanceDBStore.test.ts` is 0-EDIT).

**★ FI-30 closed.** `hybridSearch` delegates to `resolveTable`/`buildPredicate`/`accountRows`/`attemptFtsRepairOnce` and reads as five named steps ending in `fuseHybridRows`. The vec-rejected-throws-loud / fts-rejected-degrades-silently split, the `ftsRepairAttempted` once-per-instance gate, and the combined dropped-row accounting into a single `warnMalformedRowsOnce` call are all byte-identical to the pre-fix behaviour — proven by the full `LanceDBStore.test.ts` suite (23 tests, including the FTS-degrade, repair-once ×2, and malformed-row tests) staying 0-EDIT and green, plus two live mutations (the repair-once gate and the vec-loud/fts-silent split) that were confirmed to flip the expected tests before being reverted. `resolveTable` returns `this.table` directly rather than via any cast, preserving the not-undefined narrowing across the caller's `await` by construction — no `as`-cast anywhere in the diff.

## ADR-025-AH — one login-path locator core; llama.cpp and pipx locators become callers (task F8-1, WS-F8 kickoff, FI-10)

**Outcome (task F8-1, WS-F8 kickoff, FI-10):** at `c702969` (WS-F6-closed HEAD), `llamaCppLocator.ts`'s `findLlamaServerPath`/`probeAbsoluteCandidates` and `pipxLocator.ts`'s `findPipxPath`/`probeAbsoluteCandidates` were a documented verbatim clone of each other's step-0 login-shell lookup + absolute-candidate probe (`llamaCppLocator.ts`'s own header called itself "a close clone of `pipxLocator.ts`'s probe pattern"), and each file separately declared a byte-identical module-private `PROBE_TIMEOUT_DETAIL` constant. Purely behaviour-preserving — no test assertion changed, no result kind collapsed.

1. **`locatorShared.ts` gains the shared core.** `LocateOnLoginPathSpec` (`{ binary: string; candidates: readonly string[]; timeouts: { step0: number; retry: number; candidate: number }; captureVersion: boolean }`) formalizes the per-locator specifics; `LoginPathLookup` (`{ kind: 'found'; path: string; version?: string } | { kind: 'missing' } | { kind: 'probe-timeout' }`) formalizes the generic result both locators' local `*Lookup` types already used verbatim. `locateOnLoginPath(exec, spec, cwd, signal): Promise<LoginPathLookup>` is the step-0 `command -v <spec.binary>` lookup (`spec.timeouts.step0`, a TIMEOUT-only retry at `spec.timeouts.retry`, a clean miss ending the lookup immediately as `missing`) plus, only after BOTH attempts time out, its module-private `probeAbsoluteCandidates` helper — `spec.candidates` probed in the caller's own PATH-precedence order at `spec.timeouts.candidate`, each candidate's own `--version` call doubling as its presence check and, when `spec.captureVersion` is set, its version source too. Both functions are moved verbatim from whichever locator's copy was read first (byte-for-byte identical between the two, confirmed by diff before this task touched either file) — no logic changed, only the hardcoded binary/candidates/timeouts/version-capture replaced by `spec` reads. `PROBE_TIMEOUT_DETAIL` (ground-truthed: was declared separately, word-for-word identically, in both `pipxLocator.ts` and `llamaCppLocator.ts` — neither file previously imported it from the other or from `locatorShared.ts`) is now declared exactly once here and exported.
2. **`llamaCppLocator.ts` becomes a caller.** `llamaServerSpec()` builds `{ binary: 'llama-server', candidates: [~/.local/bin/llama-server, /usr/local/bin/llama-server, /usr/bin/llama-server], timeouts: { step0: STEP0_TIMEOUT_MS, retry: STEP0_RETRY_TIMEOUT_MS, candidate: ABSOLUTE_CANDIDATE_TIMEOUT_MS }, captureVersion: true }` — its own module constants (`5_000`/`10_000`/`2_000`) are untouched, only now read into the spec instead of hardcoded inline. `locateLlamaServer` calls `locateOnLoginPath(exec, llamaServerSpec(), cwd, signal)` and maps: `probe-timeout` → `{ ok: false, reason: 'probe-timeout', detail: PROBE_TIMEOUT_DETAIL }` (now the shared constant); `missing` → `{ ok: false, reason: 'not-found', detail: "'command -v llama-server' found no llama-server..." }` (this locator's own detail string, byte-identical to pre-fix, UNCHANGED — `not-found` is never collapsed into `pipx-missing` or vice versa); `found` → its existing best-effort `tryGetVersion` step-1 fallback when `lookup.version` is absent, unchanged. `LlamaCppLocateResult`'s shape is UNCHANGED.
3. **`pipxLocator.ts` becomes a caller.** `pipxSpec()` builds `{ binary: 'pipx', candidates: [~/.local/bin/pipx, /usr/bin/pipx], timeouts: { step0: PIPX_STEP0_TIMEOUT_MS, retry: PIPX_STEP0_RETRY_TIMEOUT_MS, candidate: ABSOLUTE_CANDIDATE_TIMEOUT_MS }, captureVersion: false }` — same untouched module constants, now read into the spec. `locatePipx` calls `locateOnLoginPath(exec, pipxSpec(), cwd, signal)` and maps: `probe-timeout` → `{ ok: false, reason: 'probe-timeout', detail: PROBE_TIMEOUT_DETAIL }`; `missing` → `{ ok: false, reason: 'pipx-missing', detail: "'command -v pipx' found no pipx..." }` (this locator's own detail string, byte-identical to pre-fix, UNCHANGED); `found` → proceeds to its EXISTING, entirely untouched `gatePython` python-suitability gate, which still yields `ok:true` or `{ reason: 'python-unsuitable' }`. `PipxLocateResult`'s shape and the python gate are UNCHANGED. **The divergent result kinds are NOT collapsed** — `not-found` (llamacpp) and `pipx-missing`/`python-unsuitable` (pipx) stay each locator's own domain vocabulary; only the generic `found`/`missing`/`probe-timeout` triad crosses the shared-core boundary.
4. **Both locator test files, 0-EDIT and green — the load-bearing proof.** `llamaCppLocator.test.ts` (22 tests) and `pipxLocator.test.ts` (28 tests) are untouched (`git diff --stat` confirms only the three production files changed); both suites pass unmodified, including every exec-call-sequence assertion (the scripted `ExecLookup`'s recorded `calls[]` order/`timeoutMs`/`command` per call), the PATH-precedence candidate-probe-order assertions, the `AbortSignal`-threaded-into-every-exec-call assertions (TC-5/AU-28), and each file's own REAL `execFile`-timeout pin test against `isExecTimeout` (Global Constraint 4, still re-exported from `locatorShared.ts` unchanged). This is the faithful-extraction proof: an exec-spy-ORDER test cannot pass against a core that reordered, retimed, or restructured the two-attempt-then-fallback recipe.
5. **Mutation check (actually re-run against the live code, not just asserted in prose).** Swapped `locateOnLoginPath`'s step-0/retry timeout usage (first attempt reads `spec.timeouts.retry`, the retry reads `spec.timeouts.step0` — a temporary edit): re-ran `npx vitest run src/host/setup/llamaCppLocator.test.ts src/host/setup/pipxLocator.test.ts` and got exactly 2 failures — llama.cpp's "timeout on first attempt → retries once at 10s → success" (`expected 10000 to be 5000`) and pipx's "timeout on first attempt -> retries once at 10s -> success" (`expected 10000 to be 5000`), each locator's own sequence test pinning the identical step0-then-retry ordering — then reverted (restored from a pre-mutation backup, diffed clean against the intended file); re-ran clean, both suites green (50/50).
6. **`any`/`!`/spread/new-`as`-cast — none new.** No cast of any kind appears in this task's diff. No `catch (e: any)` — no new catch blocks besides the ones moved verbatim from the two locators (same shape as before: bare `catch (err)`/`catch (firstErr)`/`catch (secondErr)`, narrowed via `signal?.aborted`/`isExecTimeout`, never `any`-typed). No new non-null assertions. No `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spread — the only spreads in the diff are the pre-existing conditional-key idiom (`...(signal !== undefined ? { signal } : {})`, `...(version ? { version } : {})`) moved verbatim from both locators, unchanged in shape.

**Frozen-zone check.** No frozen files touched — `locatorShared.ts`, `llamaCppLocator.ts`, and `pipxLocator.ts` are all non-frozen; neither test file was touched at all.

**Byte-scan (3 touched files):** `locatorShared.ts`, `llamaCppLocator.ts`, `pipxLocator.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all three).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`c702969`, WS-F6-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AG`'s own after-count) = **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: 0 (pure extraction — no test was added, edited, or removed; both locator test files are 0-EDIT).

**★ FI-10 closed.** `locateOnLoginPath` (+ the single `PROBE_TIMEOUT_DETAIL`) now live in `locatorShared.ts`; `llamaCppLocator.ts` and `pipxLocator.ts` are callers building their own `spec` (candidate list, timeouts, `captureVersion`) and mapping the shared core's generic `found`/`missing`/`probe-timeout` to their own domain result kinds (`not-found` vs `pipx-missing`/`python-unsuitable`), keeping the pipx python gate and the llama.cpp version step entirely per-locator. Both locator test files stayed 0-EDIT and green — exec-call order, result kinds, and `detail` strings all byte-identical to pre-fix — and a live step0/retry-order mutation was confirmed to flip exactly one sequence test per locator before being reverted.

## ADR-025-AI — httpStream primitives + one shared errorMessage; frozen twins kept by rule (task F8-2, WS-F8, FI-11)

**Outcome (task F8-2, WS-F8, FI-11):** at `84610f2` (F8-1-closed HEAD), the naive `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` idiom was duplicated, byte-identically, across a re-grep-confirmed **17** files (the brief's OD-E table named 16; `src/autocomplete/context/contextService.ts:178` surfaced as a 17th on re-grep, ground-truthed and included), and `ollamaClient.ts`'s HTTP-stream primitives (`joinUrl`, `readWithAbort`/`StreamReadResult`, `readBodyBounded`, `abortError`) were duplicated in `remoteProbe.ts` (`joinUrl` only) and in the FROZEN `ggufIngest.ts` (all four except `readBodyBounded`, which has no twin there). Purely behaviour-preserving — every unified call site produces the exact same string it produced before this task.

1. **`src/shared/errorMessage.ts`** carries the one shared `errorMessage(err: unknown): string` export — deliberately distinct from `src/shared/errorText.ts`'s richer `describeError` (JSON-RPC `.data` folding, home-path redaction, its own 13 pre-existing adopters); this task does not touch that module or its adopters.
2. **Byte-identity gate, run BEFORE unifying (all 17 confirmed identical, none flagged for divergence):** each of the 17 copies — `checkpointActions.ts:248`, `ControlDispatcher.ts:552`, `mcpAdminHandler.ts:758`, `panelDataCoordinator.ts:273`, `sessionScopeActions.ts:187`, `skillsAdminHandler.ts:528`, `OneShotRunner.ts:499`, `SessionController.ts:1766`, `HermesDashboardClient.ts:568`, `HermesDashboardManager.ts:406`, `panelSources.ts:515`, `ollamaClient.ts:358`, `provisionRunner.ts:825`, `remoteProbe.ts:196`, `SetupController.ts:2427`, `TalariaViewProvider.ts:1377`, `contextService.ts:178` — was read in full and confirmed byte-for-byte `return err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);` before its local definition was deleted and an `import { errorMessage } from '<relative path to>/shared/errorMessage'` added in its place. `contextService.ts` (under `src/autocomplete/`) already had six sibling files importing from `src/shared/` (`shell.vscode.ts`, `backend.ts`, `OllamaFimBackend.ts`, `LlamaCppInfillBackend.ts`, `http.ts`, `config.ts`, `secretScanner.ts`) and `contextPurity.test.ts`'s mechanical ban covers only `vscode`/`node:fs` — the new import violates neither.
3. **NEW `src/host/setup/httpStream.ts`** carries `joinUrl`, `readWithAbort` + `type StreamReadResult`, `abortError`, and `readBodyBounded` moved VERBATIM out of `ollamaClient.ts`, with ONE signature change: `readBodyBounded(response, maxBytes, label)` gains a `label` parameter so its two failure reasons read `` `${label} had no readable body` `` / `` `${label} exceeded ${maxBytes} bytes without completing` ``. `ollamaClient.ts`'s sole call site (`probeOllama`) passes `'response'`, reproducing its own pre-extraction text byte-for-byte. `remoteProbe.ts` has no `readBodyBounded`/`readWithAbort`/`abortError` call of its own (only its now-removed local `joinUrl`, called from `probeLlamaCppHealth`/`probeOpenAiModels`) — it imports `joinUrl` directly and reaches the shared `readBodyBounded` only indirectly, through `probeOllama` (`probeOllamaTags`), which is exactly the path `remoteProbe.test.ts`'s `ollamaTagsResponse` fixture already exercised pre-refraction; that indirect path's `detail` text is unchanged (still `` `Ollama /api/tags ${raw.reason}` `` with `raw.reason` = the `'response'`-labelled text above).
4. **Frozen twins kept by RULE, not edited.** `ggufIngest.ts` (`:676`/`:683`/`:707`/`:719`) and `hfDigest.ts` are **0-diff** — confirmed by `git status --short` naming neither file among this task's changes. NEW `src/host/setup/httpStream.parity.lock.test.ts` enforces the frozen twins mechanically: a marker-based extractor (the same STABLE-TEXT-MARKER technique `secretPaths.freeze.test.ts` uses, generalized to compare two LIVE files instead of one file against a pinned hash) pulls `StreamReadResult`/`readWithAbort`/`abortError`/`joinUrl` out of both `httpStream.ts` and `ggufIngest.ts`, strips the ONE sanctioned difference (`httpStream.ts`'s leading `export ` token — the frozen twins are module-private), and asserts the remainder textually IDENTICAL. `readBodyBounded` has NO frozen twin in scope: `hfDigest.ts`'s `readTreeBodyBounded` (`:103`) differs BY DESIGN (its own reason strings, no `label` param, a 4 MiB tree-specific cap) and is excluded, not force-parity'd — a dedicated test asserts the two spans are NOT equal and that the frozen twin carries no `label`, so the exclusion is a positive, checked fact rather than a silent omission.
5. **RED-first proof, both the automated in-file assertion AND a live external mutation (both actually run, not merely asserted in prose).** The lock file's own `'RED-first proof'` test mutates the extracted `readWithAbort` span in-memory (`onAbort` → `onAbortX`) and asserts the mutated text no longer equals the frozen twin. Separately, and per this task's mandate, a REAL one-token edit was planted directly in `src/host/setup/httpStream.ts` (`const onAbort` → `const onAbortX` in the declaration only, usages left unrenamed) and `npx vitest run src/host/setup/httpStream.parity.lock.test.ts` was re-run: **2 of 7 tests failed** (the `readWithAbort` row of the `it.each` parity table, and the RED-first-proof test's own pre-mutation sanity assertion — both failing on exactly the mutated span, nothing else) — proving the lock is live, not vacuous. The file was then restored from a pre-mutation copy and the same command re-run: **7/7 green**, byte-identical to the pre-mutation state.
6. **The 4 HTTP test files — 0-EDIT and green.** `ollamaClient.test.ts`, `remoteProbe.test.ts`, `ggufIngest.test.ts`, `hfDigest.test.ts` (the suites pinning `exceeded`/`no readable body`/cancel-on-every-exit) are untouched (absent from `git status --short`) and pass unmodified — `ollamaClient.test.ts`'s `'an over-cap body degrades to {running:false} with a cap-naming detail'` and `'a 200 with NO readable body degrades to...'` still see `'exceeded'`/`'no readable body'` in `status.detail`, proving the `label: 'response'` reconstruction is exact.
7. **Boundary checklist preserved.** `readBodyBounded`'s cap-before-parse (the `received > maxBytes` check runs BEFORE `JSON.parse`, unchanged) and cancel-on-every-exit (`finally { await reader.cancel().catch(() => {}); reader.releaseLock(); }`, unchanged) both moved verbatim; `readWithAbort`'s abort-races-an-in-flight-read behavior is unchanged byte-for-byte (confirmed by the parity lock itself, which would fail on any behavioural drift in that function's body).
8. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none new.** No cast of any kind appears in this task's diff (grep-confirmed across every touched file's diff hunk). No `catch (e: any)`. No new non-null assertions. No `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spread.

**Byte-scan (20 touched files):** the 17 `errorMessage`-unification sites + `httpStream.ts` + `httpStream.parity.lock.test.ts` + `errorMessage.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all twenty).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`84610f2`, F8-1-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AH`'s own after-count) = **348 test files passed, 7646 passed | 15 skipped (7661), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **349 test files passed, 7653 passed | 15 skipped (7668), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: +1 test file / +7 tests — exactly `httpStream.parity.lock.test.ts`'s 7 new cases; every one of the 17 unification sites' own existing test suites (where one exists) is unchanged in count and green, and the 4 HTTP test files are literally 0-EDIT.
