## ADR-025-AJ — `ProvisionDeps` ISP seam; `GgufIngestSpec` lives in its own leaf (task F8-3, WS-F8, FI-17)

**Outcome (task F8-3, WS-F8, FI-17):** at `051ca8d` (F8-2-closed HEAD), `ProvisionRunner`'s constructor took the WHOLE `SetupControllerDeps` bag (17 members) though its body reads only 7 of them off `this.deps` — an Interface-Segregation smell — and `GgufIngestSpec` lived in `SetupController.ts` even though the FROZEN `ggufIngest.ts:2` imports it from there, a textual dependency inversion (the frozen engine depending on the god-file for its own type). Both halves of OD-D's zero-touch default landed. Purely a type-level refactor — `tsc` is the test; zero runtime behaviour changed.

1. **`ProvisionDeps` Pick, ground-truthed by grep.** `grep 'this\.deps\.' src/host/setup/provisionRunner.ts` returned exactly 11 call sites resolving to 7 distinct members: `registry` (`:283`, `:565`), `verifyHfDigest` (`:377`, `:486`), `ingestGguf` (`:391`, `:534`), `resolveLfsOid` (`:500`), `checkedStoreDest` (`:673`, `:689`), `downloadGgufToStore` (`:704`), `pullModel` (`:754`) — matching the brief's named 7 exactly, not more, not fewer. `export type ProvisionDeps = Pick<SetupControllerDeps, 'checkedStoreDest' | 'downloadGgufToStore' | 'ingestGguf' | 'pullModel' | 'registry' | 'resolveLfsOid' | 'verifyHfDigest'>;` was added directly above `ProvisionRunnerPort` in `provisionRunner.ts`; the constructor's `private readonly deps: SetupControllerDeps` narrowed to `private readonly deps: ProvisionDeps` — its only two parameters (`port`, `latches`) untouched.
2. **`SetupController`'s `new ProvisionRunner(this.deps, …)` call site (`:660`) is a literal 0-byte diff** — confirmed by re-reading the constructor call after the edit and by the diff itself touching only the imports block and the interface-to-re-export swap, nowhere near the call site. `this.deps` there is still the FULL `SetupControllerDeps`, which structurally satisfies the narrower `ProvisionDeps` Pick with no cast.
3. **`GgufIngestSpec` moved VERBATIM (byte-identical, `allowedRepoFiles?` included) into NEW `src/host/setup/ggufIngestSpec.ts`.** The `git diff` on `SetupController.ts` shows the removed interface body (14 lines, `SetupController.ts:259-273` on the pre-task HEAD) and the added file's body are the same text — confirmed by direct comparison of the diff hunk against the new file's content, not merely eyeballed. `SetupController.ts` replaces the interface with `export type { GgufIngestSpec } from './ggufIngestSpec';` exactly as the brief specifies — a type-only re-export, erased by `verbatimModuleSyntax`, so it creates no runtime edge from `SetupController.ts` to the new leaf.
4. **`SetupController.ts` ALSO still uses `GgufIngestSpec` locally** (`SetupControllerDeps.ingestGguf(spec: GgufIngestSpec, …)`, `:357`) — a re-export statement (`export type {X} from 'm'`) does NOT introduce `X` into the re-exporting file's own scope, so a separate `import type { GgufIngestSpec } from './ggufIngestSpec';` was added to the top import block (next to the existing `import type { GgufStoreSpec } from './ggufIngest';`) purely to keep that internal usage resolving; the two declarations (the top-of-file `import type` and the re-export near the interface's old location) coexist without conflict since only the `import` creates a local binding.
5. **Frozen `ggufIngest.ts`/`hfDigest.ts` — 0-diff, confirmed by `git diff -- src/host/setup/ggufIngest.ts` / `hfDigest.ts` both returning empty.** `ggufIngest.ts:2`'s `import type { GgufIngestSpec } from './SetupController'` keeps resolving through `SetupController.ts`'s new re-export, unedited. `ggufIngest.test.ts` — the ONE other importer of `GgufIngestSpec` from `'./SetupController'` found by a repo-wide grep (besides `ggufIngest.ts` itself) — also needed no edit and stayed green for the same reason.
6. **Mutation proof, actually run.** `verifyHfDigest` was temporarily dropped from the `ProvisionDeps` Pick (leaving the other 6) and `npx tsc --noEmit` was re-run: **2 errors**, both `TS2339: Property 'verifyHfDigest' does not exist on type 'ProvisionDeps'` at `provisionRunner.ts:396` and `:505` — the exact two real read sites, nothing else broken. The file was then restored from a pre-mutation copy and `npm run check-types:all` re-run clean (tsc 0, both host and webview) to confirm the restore was exact.
7. **Provision spy banks 0-EDIT and green.** `SetupController.provisionModel.fixtures.test.ts`, `SetupController.provisionModel.test.ts`, and `ggufIngest.test.ts` are all absent from `git status --short` (untouched) and passed in the full-gate run — `this.deps` (built as a full `SetupControllerDeps` object in every fixture) still structurally satisfies the narrower `ProvisionDeps` the constructor now declares.
8. **No runtime import cycle.** `provisionRunner.ts`'s only cross-import of `SetupController.ts` is the pre-existing `import type { SetupControllerDeps }` (type-only, unchanged by this task); it does not import `GgufIngestSpec` at all — `ProvisionDeps` derives it structurally via `Pick`, needing no reference to the moved interface. `SetupController.ts`'s new re-export of `GgufIngestSpec` is likewise type-only. Both edges are erased by `verbatimModuleSyntax` at compile time — zero new runtime dependency edges either direction.
9. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none new.** No cast of any kind appears in this task's diff. No `catch (e: any)`. No new non-null assertions. No `{ ...x, }` trailing-comma spread. The diff is exactly: one new `import type` line + a 20-line `ProvisionDeps` Pick block + a one-word ctor-type change in `provisionRunner.ts`; one new `import type` line + swapping a 14-line interface body for a 5-line type-only re-export comment+statement in `SetupController.ts`; one new 27-line leaf file.

**Byte-scan (3 touched files):** `SetupController.ts`, `provisionRunner.ts`, `ggufIngestSpec.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all three).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`051ca8d`, F8-2-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AI`'s own after-count) = **349 test files passed, 7653 passed | 15 skipped (7668), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **349 test files passed, 7653 passed | 15 skipped (7668), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: 0 — exactly as expected for a pure type-level refactor (no test added, edited, or removed; the 3 provision-adjacent test files are 0-EDIT).

**★ FI-17 closed (both halves of OD-D's zero-touch default).** `ProvisionRunner`'s ctor now declares a minimal-and-sufficient `ProvisionDeps = Pick<SetupControllerDeps, 7>`, closing the ISP smell fully — proven minimal (a dropped member fails tsc at exactly its two real read sites) and sufficient (all 897 `src/host/setup/` tests plus the full 7653-test gate stay green). `GgufIngestSpec` now lives in its own leaf, `ggufIngestSpec.ts`, re-exported from `SetupController.ts` so the frozen `ggufIngest.ts:2` import resolves unchanged and the file stays 0-diff. The TEXTUAL upward import from the frozen file's perspective (`ggufIngest.ts` still names `'./SetupController'`, not `'./ggufIngestSpec'` directly) remains, exactly as OD-D's ruling anticipated and records as the accepted frozen-zone exception — the frozen file cannot be edited to point at the new leaf directly without breaking the 0-diff invariant this task is bound by.

## ADR-025-AK — dead forward-carry of the verified file-set into never-read `GgufIngestSpec.allowedRepoFiles` removed; field self-documented like its frozen twin (task F8-6, WS-F8, FI-34, `[SEC]`-adjacent)

**Outcome (task F8-6, WS-F8, FI-34):** at `30b9b18` (F8-3-closed HEAD), `provisionRunner.ts` threaded the ALREADY-verified `allowedRepoFiles` file-set forward into `GgufIngestSpec.gguf.allowedRepoFiles` (`ggufIngestSpec.ts`, moved there by F8-3) on every hf-ingest call — a field the frozen ingest sink `ingestGguf` (`ggufIngest.ts:277`) never reads (census: the only two occurrences of `allowedRepoFiles` in that file are the doc comment and the declaration on the `GgufStoreSpec` sibling type, zero in `ingestGguf`'s body). The carry invited exactly the false read the frozen `GgufStoreSpec` twin's own doc already warns against for its sibling field: "a caller must not read its presence as the sink enforcing the exact file set." Enforcement happens upstream, once, in `verifyHfDigest`'s ⚠ S-F4 exact-file-set equality — this task only stops a dead value from riding an extra hop downstream and self-documents the field so the same false read can't recur on this twin either. Purely a dead-code removal + a doc-comment addition — zero runtime behaviour change (the sink never read the value it was being handed).

1. **`ggufIngestSpec.ts` — the field KEPT (optional), its doc comment REPLACED.** The old comment ("meaningful only in `pinned` mode … `live-oid` mode passes none") became actively FALSE the moment this task's `provisionRunner.ts` edit landed — it described a forward-carry that no longer happens — so it was replaced, not merely appended to. The new comment mirrors the frozen `GgufStoreSpec.gguf.allowedRepoFiles` doc (`ggufIngest.ts:171-179`) sentence-for-sentence in substance: `ingestGguf` never reads it; exact-file-set enforcement happens UPSTREAM in `verifyHfDigest` (⚠ S-F4) BEFORE ingest; the field exists only for shape parity with `GgufStoreSpec.gguf.allowedRepoFiles`; a caller must not read its presence on `GgufIngestSpec` as "the sink enforces the file set." The field itself (`allowedRepoFiles?: readonly string[]`) is untouched — removing it would break the FROZEN-sink fixture in `ggufIngest.test.ts` (which builds a `GgufIngestSpec` literal carrying it) and would falsify `GgufStoreSpec`'s own shape-parity pointer, which names this exact field.
2. **`provisionRunner.ts` — five dead-carry sites removed, re-grounded on the LIVE HEAD (line numbers drifted from the brief's `051ca8d` citations, as expected post-F8-3):** `resolvePinnedDigest`'s return type (`:497-499` pre-edit) dropped the `allowedRepoFiles: readonly string[] | undefined` member, narrowing to `{ ok: true; expected: string } | { ok: false; reason: string }`; its `return` (`:511` pre-edit) dropped `allowedRepoFiles: pinnedSpec.spec.allowedRepoFiles`. `runOllamaIngest`'s `args` type (`:533` pre-edit) dropped the same member; its destructure dropped `allowedRepoFiles`. The `ingestGguf(...)` spec build's conditional spread `...(allowedRepoFiles !== undefined ? { allowedRepoFiles } : {})` (`:562` pre-edit) was removed ENTIRELY — not replaced by any other spread. Both `runOllamaIngest({...})` call sites dropped their `allowedRepoFiles` field: the pinned arm's `allowedRepoFiles: digest.allowedRepoFiles,` and the live-oid arm's `allowedRepoFiles: undefined,`. Net: `ingestGguf` (now `provisionRunner.ts:550`) is called with a `gguf` object carrying exactly `{hfRepo, file, quant, sha256, approxBytes}` — no `allowedRepoFiles` key at all, on either arm.
3. **The LIVE S-F4 path is BYTE-UNCHANGED — confirmed, not assumed.** `pinnedVerifySpec` (`provisionRunner.ts:207-220`), which builds the `HfGgufSpec` carrying `allowedRepoFiles: NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.allowedRepoFiles` for `verifyHfDigest`, and both `await this.deps.verifyHfDigest(...)` call sites (`:397`, `:503`) are untouched — `git diff` on the surrounding hunks shows no line change there. `hfDigest.ts` (the S-F4 check itself) and `ggufIngest.ts` (the frozen sink) are BOTH 0-diff: `git diff -- src/host/setup/ggufIngest.ts src/host/setup/hfDigest.ts` returns empty. `hfDigest.test.ts`, `ggufIngest.test.ts`, and `registry.test.ts` are all absent from `git status --short` (0-EDIT) and all pass.
4. **The one legitimate test edit — `SetupController.provisionModel.fixtures.test.ts`.** Of the four `allowedRepoFiles` sites the brief flagged for inspection (comment `:445-446`, `verifyArgs` `:452`, `ingestArgs` `:464` pre-edit, `verifyArgs` `:553`), exactly ONE was an `ingestGguf`-arg expectation and was dropped (the ollama-pinned happy-path test's `ingestArgs` assertion). The other two `allowedRepoFiles` lines are `verifyArgs` assertions — they pin what `verifyHfDigest` receives (the LIVE S-F4 wiring), not what `ingestGguf` receives, and both stayed UNTOUCHED. The `:445-446` comment ("SC-5: the pre-flight runs the FULL exact-file-set check…") sits directly above the `verifyArgs` assertion it was always describing, is still 100% accurate post-edit (it never claimed `ingestGguf` also carries the field), and needed no correction; a new comment was added instead directly above the trimmed `ingestArgs` block explaining the omission ("`ingestGguf` never reads `gguf.allowedRepoFiles`… the spy bank pins that the field is NOT forwarded"). No other expected field (`hfRepo`/`file`/`quant`/`sha256`/`approxBytes`/`ollamaCreatedName`) and no `verifyHfDigest`/S-F4 assertion anywhere in the file changed.
5. **TDD red→green, actually run.** The fixture-expectation edit was made FIRST and run alone against the pre-edit `provisionRunner.ts`: 1 failure (the happy-path test), diff showing the received `ingestArgs` still carrying the 3-entry `allowedRepoFiles` array the expectation no longer asserted — the RIGHT failure, for the right reason. The five `provisionRunner.ts` sites were then removed; the same test file re-run: 24/24 pass. Full `src/host/setup/` suite: 897/897 pass, tsc 0.
6. **Mutation (a) — re-introduce the dead carry — RUN, FLIPPED, RESTORED.** `allowedRepoFiles: NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.allowedRepoFiles` was temporarily re-added to the `ingestGguf` spec build (`:550` area) with the fixture expectation left trimmed: the happy-path test failed exactly as predicted (received object carries the 3-entry array the expectation no longer has), 23/24 pass in that file. Reverted; re-run confirmed 24/24 and a clean `git diff` on `provisionRunner.ts` matching the pre-mutation state.
7. **Mutation (b) — break the LIVE path — RUN, and a REAL finding: the brief's target file was wrong.** `pinnedVerifySpec`'s `allowedRepoFiles: NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.allowedRepoFiles` was temporarily changed to `allowedRepoFiles: []`. **`hfDigest.test.ts` stayed 100% green (27/27)** — that file is fully self-contained (imports only `verifyHfDigest`/`resolveLfsOid` from `./hfDigest`, builds its own local `HfGgufSpec`-shaped fixture, never calls into `provisionRunner.ts`), so a `provisionRunner.ts`-only mutation cannot reach it; the brief's "hfDigest.test.ts's S-F4 test must FAIL" claim does not hold for THIS mutation site. What DID flip, exactly as the underlying security property requires: the fixtures suite's two UNTOUCHED `verifyArgs` assertions (the ollama-pinned happy-path test and the llamacpp-pinned test), both pinning that `verifyHfDigest` receives the FULL 3-entry `allowedRepoFiles` set — 2 of 24 tests failed with the exact expected-vs-received diff (`[]` vs the 3-entry array). This is a stronger proof than the brief anticipated: it demonstrates the actual `pinnedVerifySpec`→`verifyHfDigest` WIRING inside `provisionRunner.ts` is live-tested, not merely that `hfDigest.ts`'s own unit tests are independently green (which they always would be, being self-contained). Reverted; re-run confirmed 24/24, `hfDigest.test.ts` 27/27 throughout, and a clean `git diff` on `provisionRunner.ts` matching the pre-mutation state byte-for-byte.
8. **`ingestGguf` frozen-sink body — 0 reads of `.allowedRepoFiles`, re-confirmed on THIS HEAD.** `grep -n allowedRepoFiles src/host/setup/ggufIngest.ts` returns exactly 2 lines (the `GgufStoreSpec` doc comment and its declaration, both inside the sibling type, both above the `ingestGguf` function at `:277`) — zero occurrences inside the function body itself.
9. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none new.** The full diff across all three touched files is: two doc-comment blocks (one replaced, one added), five deletions of an `allowedRepoFiles` type member/return field/destructure entry/call-site field (four such deletions plus the conditional-spread line removed outright), and one dropped line in a test's expected-object literal. No cast of any kind, no `catch (e: any)`, no new non-null assertion, no `{ ...x, }` spread — the ONE spread that existed on this path (the conditional `...(allowedRepoFiles !== undefined ? { allowedRepoFiles } : {})`) was deleted, not replaced by another.

**Byte-scan (3 touched files):** `ggufIngestSpec.ts`, `provisionRunner.ts`, `SetupController.provisionModel.fixtures.test.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (all three).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`30b9b18`, F8-3-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AJ`'s own after-count, unchanged by F8-3's pure type-level refactor) = **349 test files passed, 7653 passed | 15 skipped (7668), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **349 test files passed, 7653 passed | 15 skipped (7668), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: 0 — exactly as expected for a dead-carry removal (no test added or removed; one existing `toEqual` array literal lost one line). The known `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` Windows-teardown flake did NOT appear in this run.

**★ FI-34 closed.** `GgufIngestSpec.gguf.allowedRepoFiles?` SURVIVES (optional, byte-identical field declaration) but is (a) no longer populated by `provisionRunner` on either the pinned or live-oid arm, and (b) documented — mirroring the frozen `GgufStoreSpec` twin word-for-word in substance — as never-read-by-the-sink with enforcement upstream in `verifyHfDigest`. `ingestGguf` is now called without `gguf.allowedRepoFiles` on every call site (behaviour-neutral: the sink never read it). The LIVE `pinnedVerifySpec`→`verifyHfDigest` S-F4 exact-file-set check is 100% intact and byte-unchanged; `hfDigest.test.ts`/`registry.test.ts`/`ggufIngest.test.ts` all stayed 0-EDIT and green throughout. Because the field survives, the frozen `ggufIngest.ts:175` `GgufStoreSpec` doc's shape-parity pointer to `GgufIngestSpec.gguf.allowedRepoFiles` REMAINS TRUE — no stale pointer was created by this task.

## ADR-025-AL — one `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions` guard for the duplicated fail-closed ladder (task F8-4, WS-F8, FI-22 setup-half, `[SEC]`-adjacent)

**Outcome (task F8-4, WS-F8, FI-22 setup-half):** at `e6e1c8d` (F8-6-closed HEAD), the SAME two-check pinned-Ollama fail-closed precondition ladder — `if (sha256 === '') return { ok: false, reason: NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE };` then `if (!isLoopbackEndpoint(endpoint)) return { ok: false, reason: NEXT_REMOTE_ENDPOINT_REFUSAL };` — was duplicated verbatim inline in two places: `handleVettedIngest` (the NEXT/dedicated-FIM path, `:373-379`) and `provisionOllama`'s pinned arm (the catalog path, `:602-603`). The splitter half of FI-22 (the `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` triplication) was already closed in WS-F9 (`ADR-025-U`); this is the setup-half — "drift prevented by a lock-test, not by extraction" — the fix is ONE shared guard so there is a single maintenance point for these two security refusals.

1. **NEW pure module-level function `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions(sha256, endpoint)`** added to `provisionRunner.ts` immediately after its neighbour `isLoopbackEndpoint` (`:128-134`). Body is a byte-for-byte lift of the duplicated ladder: `if (sha256 === '') return { ok: false, reason: NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE }; if (!isLoopbackEndpoint(endpoint)) return { ok: false, reason: NEXT_REMOTE_ENDPOINT_REFUSAL }; return { ok: true };` — EXACT beta.5 vetted order preserved (empty-pin BEFORE loopback). A pure module function, not a method (no `this`), matching `isLoopbackEndpoint`'s own style. Exported so the new focused unit test can call it directly and pin the order.
2. **`NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE` and `NEXT_REMOTE_ENDPOINT_REFUSAL` gained `export`** (previously module-private `const`s, `:68`/`:71`) — the minimal export needed for the new unit test to assert against the real strings instead of re-typing them. No other symbol was exported.
3. **`handleVettedIngest`** — the two inline `if`s (`:373-379`) became `const pre = refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions(NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.sha256, endpoint); if (!pre.ok) return pre;`. The surrounding "(a)+(b)" comment now points at the shared helper instead of re-explaining both checks; the rest of the method (latch, `verifyHfDigest`, the Tier-1 modal, `ingestGguf`) is byte-unchanged.
4. **`provisionOllama`'s pinned arm** — the two inline `if`s (`:602-603`) became `const pre = refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions(cell.verify.sha256, validated.url); if (!pre.ok) return pre;`. The rest of the arm (`resolvePinnedDigest`, `composePinnedOllamaModal`, `runOllamaIngest`) is byte-unchanged.
5. **Deliberately left inline, per the brief's scope fence:** `provisionLlamacpp`'s pinned arm (`:666`) checks ONLY the empty pin — it is a store/file-placement path with no endpoint argument at all, so there is nothing to force through a two-check ollama-named helper; its single `if (cell.verify.sha256 === '') return { ok: false, reason: NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE };` is untouched. Both `live-oid` arms (`provisionOllama`'s `:625`, `provisionLlamacpp`'s own resolve path) check ONLY loopback (no empty-pin concept in that verify mode) and are also untouched. `git diff` confirms zero lines changed in either of these three sites.
6. **NEW focused unit test `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions.test.ts`** (4 cases) gives the ORDER teeth the behavioural tests structurally cannot: each behavioural test triggers only ONE of the two checks at a time, so none of them pins which reason wins when BOTH conditions fail simultaneously. The four cases: empty pin + loopback → `NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE`; non-empty pin + non-loopback → `NEXT_REMOTE_ENDPOINT_REFUSAL`; **empty pin + non-loopback → `NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE`** (the ORDER proof — empty-pin wins even though the endpoint is also bad); non-empty pin + loopback → `{ ok: true }`. Loopback fixture `http://127.0.0.1:11434/`, non-loopback fixture `http://10.0.0.5:11434/` (both real URLs, consistent with the behavioural suites' own `127.0.0.1`/private-IP fixtures). The two refusal reasons are imported from `./provisionRunner`, never re-typed as literal strings.
7. **TDD red→green, actually run.** The test file was written first, importing the not-yet-existing `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions`; all 4 cases failed with `TypeError: refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions is not a function` — the right failure, for the right reason. The helper + two call-site replacements were then added; the same file re-run: 4/4 pass. Full `src/host/setup/` suite: 901/901 pass (897 prior + 4 new), tsc 0 (host + webview).
8. **Mutation 1 — ORDER swap, RUN, FLIPPED, RESTORED.** The helper's two `if`s were temporarily swapped (loopback checked BEFORE empty-pin). The new unit test's "empty pin + non-loopback → `NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE`" case failed exactly as predicted — received `NEXT_REMOTE_ENDPOINT_REFUSAL` instead (3/4 pass in that file). Reverted; re-run confirmed 4/4 and a `git diff` on `provisionRunner.ts` matching the pre-mutation state.
9. **Mutation 2 — reason swap, RUN, FLIPPED, RESTORED.** The helper's empty-pin branch was temporarily changed to return `NEXT_REMOTE_ENDPOINT_REFUSAL` instead of `NEXT_DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE` (proving the callers still surface the right reason, not just the right ORDER). Full `src/host/setup/` suite: 5 failures across 3 files — `SetupController.provisionModel.test.ts` (both `sweep-next` empty-pin cases, ollama and llamacpp arms), `SetupController.provisionModel.fixtures.test.ts`, and `SetupController.test.ts`'s own T13 refusal-order block (both the base and the uppercase-variant vetted-name cases) — every one expecting `DOWNLOAD_UNAVAILABLE`-shaped copy and receiving the remote-endpoint line instead. This is a STRONGER result than the brief's "the behavioural refusal test flips" (singular) — it demonstrates BOTH callers (`handleVettedIngest` AND `provisionOllama`'s pinned arm) are live-tested against the extracted helper's actual reason values, not merely order. Reverted; re-run confirmed 901/901 and a `git diff` on `provisionRunner.ts` matching the pre-mutation state byte-for-byte.
10. **`any`/`!`/spread/`as`-cast — none new.** The full diff across `provisionRunner.ts` is: two doc-comment amendments plus two `export` keywords on the existing refusal consts, one new exported pure function (a lift of the pre-existing ladder, unchanged predicates/reasons/order), and two call sites each replacing a two-line inline ladder with a two-line helper call. No cast of any kind, no `catch (e: any)`, no new non-null assertion, no `{ ...x, }` spread. The new test file uses no `any`/`as`/`!`/spread either.

**Byte-scan (2 touched files):** `provisionRunner.ts`, `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions.test.ts` (new) — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0 (both).

**Both provision behavioural test files stayed 0-EDIT.** `git status --short` shows only `M src/host/setup/provisionRunner.ts` and the new test file — neither `SetupController.provisionModel.test.ts` nor `SetupController.provisionModel.fixtures.test.ts` appears, and both passed unchanged throughout (their "non-loopback → remote refusal BEFORE verifyHfDigest/modal/ingest" and "empty pin → unavailable, nothing runs" tests, for both ollama and llamacpp, are exactly as they were pre-task).

**Count discipline:** baseline (`e6e1c8d`, F8-6-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AK`'s own after-count) = **349 test files passed, 7653 passed | 15 skipped (7668), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **350 test files passed, 7657 passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview).** Delta: +1 test file, +4 tests — exactly the new `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions.test.ts` and its 4 cases; no existing test was added, removed, or altered. The known `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` Windows-teardown flake did NOT appear in this run.

**★ FI-22 (setup-half) closed** — the splitter half (`DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` triplication) was already closed by `ADR-025-U` (WS-F9), completing the finding. `refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions` is now the single source of the pinned-Ollama fail-closed ladder; `handleVettedIngest` and `provisionOllama`'s pinned arm are its two callers, both refusing with byte-identical reasons in the byte-identical vetted order, BEFORE any modal/verify/ingest, exactly as before. `provisionLlamacpp`'s empty-pin-only arm and both `live-oid` arms were correctly left inline — none has the two-check shape the helper encodes. The new unit test closes the coverage gap the behavioural suites structurally cannot: which reason wins when BOTH the pin and the endpoint are bad. Both mutations (order-swap, reason-swap) were run, flipped the expected tests, and were restored byte-identically.

## ADR-025-AM — `composeDedicated` extracted, splitting the settings-read layer from the registry-pin card layer in `composeNextEditBlock` (task F8-5, WS-F8, FI-32)

**Outcome (task F8-5, WS-F8, FI-32):** at `4282b1f` (F8-4-closed HEAD), `statusBlocks.ts`'s `composeNextEditBlock` (`:85-151`, ~66 L) mixed TWO abstraction levels in one function: (1) a **settings-read layer** — reads the user's configured NEXT connection from `reader.getSetting` (`backend`/`endpoint`/`model`/`dedicatedBackendId`), coercions, `dedicatedConfigured`, and the two conditional wire spreads on the return; (2) a **registry-pin card layer** — the `dedicated` object, computed PURELY from the `NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL` registry pins (displayName, modelDefaults, downloadReady, downloadApproxBytes, warning, guided vllm/llamacpp commands), with nothing to do with settings. This task extracts layer 2 into its own pure function so each function works at ONE abstraction level.

1. **NEW pure function `composeDedicated(): NonNullable<SetupData['nextEdit']['dedicated']>`** added to `statusBlocks.ts`, immediately below `composeNextEditBlock`. Parameterless — reads `NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL` directly, no injected argument (a faithful move; keeping it parameterless minimises the diff and lets the golden prove equivalence on its own).
2. **Moved into it, VERBATIM:** the `const downloadReady = (NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.sha256 as string) !== '';` line, **including the pre-existing `as string` cast, byte-identical** (a FAITHFUL MOVE of an existing cast, not a new one — not "fixed" or removed); and the entire `const dedicated: NonNullable<SetupData['nextEdit']['dedicated']> = { … };` object literal — `displayName`, `modelDefaults` (`ollama`/`openaiCompat`), `downloadReady`, `downloadApproxBytes`, `warning`, and the `guided` block INCLUDING the conditional `...(downloadReady ? { llamacpp: … } : {})` object-spread (pre-existing, moved verbatim — not the banned `{ ...identifier, }` spread, not reworked). The `downloadReady`/`dedicated` doc-comment (T13 §4.2) moved with the code, reworded into `composeDedicated`'s own JSDoc.
3. **`composeNextEditBlock`** — the moved `downloadReady` + `dedicated` construction is gone; its return object's `dedicated` field is now `dedicated: composeDedicated(),`. Everything else — the settings reads (`nextBackend`/`nextEndpoint`/`nextModel`), `dedicatedConfigured`, the `nextDedicatedBackendId` coercion, and both conditional wire spreads (`...(nextDedicatedBackendId !== undefined ? … : {})`, `...(nextSource === 'generic' && !genericSupported ? … : {})`) — is byte-unchanged (the settings layer stays exactly where it was).
4. **`downloadReady` scope confirmed single-use.** Grepped `downloadReady` in the pre-change file: all 4 occurrences (declaration + 3 reads: `modelDefaults.ollama`, the `downloadReady` shorthand key, the `guided.llamacpp` conditional) were inside the `dedicated` object construction — none outside it. The whole symbol moved cleanly with no dangling read left in `composeNextEditBlock`; post-change grep confirms `downloadReady` no longer appears anywhere in that function.
5. **TDD-flavoured verification (behaviour-preserving move, no red phase — nothing to make newly pass).** `npx vitest run src/host/setup/`: 24 files, 901/901 pass (unchanged count — pure move, no new test per the brief's YAGNI guidance; the shipping-state golden already pins the extracted card, so a redundant "composeDedicated() deep-equals the card" unit test was deliberately NOT added). `SetupController.statusGolden.test.ts` stayed **0-EDIT** throughout (`git status --short` / `git diff --stat` show only `statusBlocks.ts` touched).
6. **Mutation, RUN, FLIPPED, RESTORED.** `composeDedicated`'s `downloadApproxBytes: NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.approxBytes` was temporarily changed to `... + 1`. Re-running `SetupController.statusGolden.test.ts` alone: **10/10 tests failed**, every diff pinpointing `downloadApproxBytes: 4680000000` (expected) vs `4680000001` (received) inside `nextEdit.dedicated` — proof the golden's `BASE_DEDICATED_NEXT`/`dedicated` assertions pin the card `composeDedicated()` now produces, not a stale copy. Reverted; re-ran `src/host/setup/`: 901/901 pass again, `git diff --stat` on `statusBlocks.ts` identical before/after the mutate-restore cycle (42 lines changed against the F8-4 baseline, same as pre-mutation).
7. **`as`/`any`/spread discipline — none new.** `grep -n " as "` on the final file: `as const` (`:37`, pre-existing enum tuple), `as readonly string[]` (`:45`, pre-existing), and the one `as string` cast (`:128`, now inside `composeDedicated`, moved verbatim from its old `:110`) — **net count of `as string` casts is still 1**, only its location changed. No `any` (grep hits are prose — "free of any", "breaks any cycle" — not the TypeScript type). No new `!`. The only spread inside the moved code is the pre-existing conditional-object `...(downloadReady ? { llamacpp: … } : {})` (byte-identical to its old form) — no new `{ ...x, }` identifier spread was introduced anywhere in the file (the file's other 9 spreads, listed by grep, are all pre-existing conditional-key-omission patterns in `composeNextEditBlock`, `composeRagBlock`, `composeAgentBlock`, `composeOsBlock`, untouched).
8. **Byte-scan (1 touched file):** `statusBlocks.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.
9. **The `downloadReady=true` branch remains a PRE-EXISTING coverage gap** (the shipping empty-pin (`NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL.gguf.sha256 === ''`) never exercises `guided.llamacpp`/`modelDefaults.ollama`'s truthy arms; the statusGolden test file's own comment at `:182-186` already documents this as intentionally un-pinned, deferred to the pinned-mode `provisionModel` tests). This task does NOT expand coverage for that gap — reaching it would require mocking the `NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL` registry const, explicitly out of scope per the brief's YAGNI note; it stays in the coverage backlog, unchanged by this extraction.

**Count discipline:** baseline (`4282b1f`, F8-4-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AL`'s own after-count) = **350 test files passed, 7657 passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. Full-gate run after this task: **350 test files passed** (1 flaked on first run — see below) **| tsc 0 (host + webview), no type errors.** The known `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` Windows-teardown flake (`ENOTEMPTY` on `afterEach`'s `rmSync`, not an assertion failure, unrelated file/subsystem) DID appear on the full-gate run (1 failed / 349 passed of 350 files, 7656 passed + 1 flaked | 15 skipped of 7672); isolate-running `npx vitest run src/rag/indexer.test.ts` alone immediately after: **59/61 pass (2 skipped), 0 failures** — confirming the flake, not a regression from this task. Delta vs baseline: **0 test files, 0 tests** — exactly the pure-move expectation (no test added or removed).

**★ FI-32 closed.** `composeDedicated()` (pure, parameterless, reads `NEXT_DEDICATED_MODEL` directly) now holds the registry-pin card in its own function; `composeNextEditBlock` calls it and retains only the settings-read layer (reads, coercions, `dedicatedConfigured`, both conditional wire spreads). The pre-existing `as string` cast and the conditional `guided.llamacpp` object-spread moved verbatim — no new cast, no new spread, net counts unchanged. `SetupController.statusGolden.test.ts` stayed 0-EDIT and green throughout, and the `downloadApproxBytes` mutation flipped its `dedicated` assertions exactly as predicted, proving the golden pins the extracted card's output, not merely its shape.

## ADR-025-AN — FI-41 — `SetupController` residual breadth: closed by evidence; `SetupCommandRouter` deferred (task F8-7, WS-F8 — LAST task of WS-F8, DOC-ONLY, closes OD-L)

**Outcome (task F8-7, WS-F8, FI-41 / OD-L):** at `020ec8a` (F8-5-closed HEAD), re-measured — not copied — on the live HEAD. FI-41 read: "`SetupController` residual breadth ~2438 L (15 `handle*` + `status()` + memo + throttle) — responsibility-*count*, not god-method length; seams clean. Optional `SetupCommandRouter`." OD-L's own "Default" column already named the closure shape: "Close-by-evidence: this ADR records the post-GD.2b module map + the FI-17 ISP seam as the closure; row flips to `✅ Closed (by-evidence — no code change)`." This task performs that measurement and applies the adversarial honesty test from the brief before accepting it. All three legs hold — see the Decision below — so FI-41 closes BY EVIDENCE: no code change, `SetupCommandRouter` stays deferred and optional.

**1. Total line count.** `wc -l src/host/setup/SetupController.ts` → **2470**. (Was 3448 pre-remediation → 2438 after WS-GD.2b → 2470 now; the +32 came from WS-F8's own doc/refactor tasks (F8-1/F8-3/F8-4/F8-6) touching this file with net-additive JSDoc and small extractions, not new responsibilities.)

**2. `handle*`/`status()` inventory.** `grep -n "^\s*\(private\|public\|protected\)\?\s*\(async\s\+\)\?handle[A-Za-z0-9_]*\s*("` over the file finds **16 `handleXxx` methods plus the `handle()` dispatcher itself = 17** — this reconciles exactly with OD-L's own context column ("17 `handle*`"); the original finding text's "15" undercounts by 2, corrected here. Per-method code-line count via an `awk` pass over each `[start, next-method-start)` span (comment lines match `^\s*(//|\*|/\*)`, blank `^\s*$`, remainder = code):

| method | span (lines) | total | code | comment | blank |

| `handleOpenInstallTerminal` | 1689–1750 | 62 | 29 | 29 | 4 |

| `handleSetTunable` | 1930–1961 | 32 | 11 | 19 | 2 |
| **Σ (18 rows)** | | **909** | **547** | **297** | **65** |

Two `setup.*` cases (`setup.pullModel`, `setup.provisionModel`) dispatch straight to `this.provision.handlePullModel`/`handleProvisionModel` — those handler BODIES live in `ProvisionRunner` (`provisionRunner.ts`), not this file, so they are correctly absent from the table above; counting them here would double-count a collaborator's own responsibility as the façade's. **547 code lines across an 2470-line file is ≈22%** — the remaining ≈78% is JSDoc (this codebase's T-numbered rationale-comment convention, visible above in `handleInstall`/`handleRecheck`'s comment columns), field declarations carrying their own multi-paragraph docs (`llamaCppMemo`, `hermesDiscoveryMemo`, `statusChangedEmitter`), 17 private single-purpose helpers below the handlers (`resolveOs`, `computeOsResolution`, `kickLlamaCppProbe`, `rekickLlamaCppProbe`, `composeLlamaCppRuntime`, `safeScanStorePresence`, `projectCatalogModel`, `onInstallEvent`, `writeSettingsBatch`, `composeAgentLocalModel`, `composeSavedAgentEntry`, `kickHermesDiscovery`, `computeAgentPhase`, `projectBackend`, `safeProbeOllama`, `redact`, `pushProgress`), and module-level pure functions at the file's tail (`composeAgentGuidance`, `validateBootstrapTarget`, `validateRecheckScope`, `deriveHermesAcpPath`, `str`, `bool`, `isNonNegativeNumber`, `isPositiveInteger`, `isNonNegativeInteger`, `validateTunableValue`). The file's BREADTH is real (many named responsibilities); its DENSITY is low — no method does a lot of work per line.

**3. Largest method's density + delegation — `handleInstall` (77 code lines, the largest `handle*` by code-line count; `status()` at 74 is a close second but is not itself a `handle*`).** Read in full and grepped for delegation call sites over the extracted method body (`this.latches.` / `this.deps.` / `this.host.` / `this.provision.` / `this.dispatcher.` / `this.port.` / the probe memos):
- `this.latches.` → **3** (`has`, `arm`, `release` — the single-flight guard)
- `this.deps.` → **3** (`registry.getBackend`, `locatePipx`, `installHermes`)
- `this.host.` → **6** (`showModal`, `updateSettingGlobal` ×3, `globalState.update`, `offerReload`)
- `this.provision.` / `this.dispatcher.` / `this.port.` / the probe memos → **0** (not used by this method)
- **12 delegation call sites in 77 code lines — ≈1 delegated call per 6.4 code lines.** The method's own control flow is: two early-return guards (already-running latch; no pipx recipe), arm the latch, show the confirm modal (`this.host.showModal`, early-return on decline), delegate the pipx locate (`this.deps.locatePipx`, early-return + `redact`/`bumpStatus` bookkeeping on failure), delegate the install (`this.deps.installHermes`, wiring its progress callback to the ALREADY-extracted `onInstallEvent`, early-return on failure), delegate the three settings writes + one `globalState` write (`this.host.*`, early-return on failure), then bookkeeping (`awaitingReload`, `bumpStatus`, `offerReload`) and a `finally` releasing the latch. Every branch is a flat early-return; nesting never exceeds the two `try/catch` blocks the two awaited, rejectable calls demand; there is no loop and no re-implementation of what `locatePipx`/`installHermes`/`updateSettingGlobal` themselves do. This is an ORCHESTRATION method — a linear guard → confirm → delegate ×3 → bookkeeping pipeline — not a monolith doing the work inline.
- `status()` (74 code lines, for comparison) delegates similarly: `this.host.` ×9, `this.deps.` ×7, plus 7 imported pure `compose*`/`compute*` functions from `statusBlocks.ts` (`composeAgentBlock`, `composeFimTuning`, `composeNextEditBlock`, `composeRagBlock`, `composeOsBlock`, `computeProviderCard`, `computeReady`) and 8 small private single-purpose helpers (`resolveOs`, `safeProbeOllama`, `safeScanStorePresence`, `projectBackend`, `projectCatalogModel`, `computeAgentPhase`, `composeLlamaCppRuntime`, `composeAgentLocalModel`). Its "work" is composing a `SetupData` object literal out of ~16 already-delegated field values, not computing any of them itself.
- `handleSetTunable` (11 code lines) is the sharpest example at the OTHER end: it validates a key against `TIER2_TUNABLE_KEYS`, then hands the ENTIRE per-key validation switch to a module-level pure function, `validateTunableValue` (`:2431`, extracted outside the class, at the file's tail) — zero validation branching lives in the handler itself.

**4. Extracted-collaborator inventory** (`wc -l`) — confirms the heavy responsibilities already live outside this file, in cleanly-seamed modules:
- `provisionRunner.ts` — **865 L** — the `ProvisionRunner` class (held as `this.provision`, constructed once in the constructor) owns the ENTIRE provision/pull family: `handlePullModel` (`:323`), `handleProvisionModel` (`:484`), `handleVettedIngest` (`:397`), `provisionOllama` (`:593`), `provisionLlamacpp` (`:678`), plus the module-level pure guards `isHostSourcedModel`, **`refusePinnedOllamaPreconditions`** (F8-4, FI-22 setup-half, `ADR-025-AL`), `isAllowlistedHfOwner`, `assertProvisionSources`, `canonicalPullLatchId`. `SetupController.handle()` reaches exactly 2 of these (`setup.pullModel`, `setup.provisionModel`) via a ONE-LINE delegation each — none of `ProvisionRunner`'s logic, including F8-4's extracted fail-closed ladder, is duplicated or re-implemented in `SetupController.ts`.
- `statusBlocks.ts` — **297 L** — every `compose*`/`compute*` pure function `status()` calls, including F8-5's `composeDedicated` extraction inside `composeNextEditBlock` (`ADR-025-AM`).
- `settledProbeMemo.ts` — **93 L** — the generic `SettledProbeMemo<T>` class, instantiated TWICE (`llamaCppMemo`, `hermesDiscoveryMemo`) rather than two hand-rolled kick/invalidate/rekick/supersede state machines inline.
- `latchRegistry.ts` — **51 L** — the `LatchRegistry` class backing `this.latches` (the single-flight `install:<id>`/`pull:<model>`/`recheck` guard, `dispose()`-reachable).
- `modalText.ts` — **63 L** — `redactForModal`/`refuseUnsafeModalText`, the modal-copy safety helpers.
- `ggufIngestSpec.ts` — **34 L** — `GgufIngestSpec`'s own leaf (F8-3, FI-17 ISP seam, `ADR-025-AJ`; `SetupController.ts` only imports the TYPE).
- `src/host/util/mutationGate.ts` — **82 L** — the generic `MutationGate` (`createMutationGate`) backing `this.lifecycle`; shared with other controllers, not setup-specific.
- **Σ setup-specific collaborators (excluding the shared, generic `MutationGate`): 865+297+93+51+63+34 = 1403 L** of setup-domain logic living outside `SetupController.ts` — nearly 60% as much code as the façade itself, none of it duplicated inside it.
- **Correction to the brief's guessed grep list:** `this.dispatcher.` and `this.port.` do NOT exist as fields on this class (confirmed: 0 hits for both, and no such field in the constructor). `ControlDispatcher` — WS-GD.2a's decomposition target — lives in `src/host/backend/control/ControlDispatcher.ts` and belongs to the UNRELATED backend/control-message god-unit; it is never imported by, or related to, `SetupController.ts`. The real delegate fields on this class are `this.host` (`SetupHost`), `this.deps` (`SetupControllerDeps`), `this.provision` (`ProvisionRunner`), `this.latches` (`LatchRegistry`), `this.lifecycle` (`MutationGate`), and the two `SettledProbeMemo` fields.

**5. The dispatch site** — two layers, both already thin:
- **Outer:** `TalariaViewProvider.handleSetupMethod` (`TalariaViewProvider.ts:1122–1144`, 22 lines) — routes `'setup.status'` to `controller.status()`, every other `SetupMethod` to `controller.handle(method, params)`, then does ONE post-mutation panel-refresh push. This already IS the per-domain router pattern (mirroring `ControlDispatcher`'s own "dispatch → refetch → push" contract for the unrelated control-message domain, per its own doc comment at `:1134-1137`) — the outer layer a `SetupCommandRouter` might be imagined to replace is not inside `SetupController.ts` at all; it already lives one file up.
- **Inner:** `SetupController.handle()` (`:1019–1075`, 51 code lines) — a flat `switch (method)` over the closed `SetupMethod` union, one arm per case: `return this.handleXxx(params)` (13 sites) or `return this.provision.handleXxx(params)` (2 sites, already routed OUT to the collaborator), plus the special-cased no-op `'setup.status'` arm (kept only so the switch stays exhaustive — `tsc` refuses to compile if a `SetupMethod` case is left unhandled, per the `TE-4` doc comment at `:1023-1028`, since there is no `default`). Zero business logic lives in the switch itself.
- **Assessment: a `SetupCommandRouter` would relocate `:1019–1075` to a new file — it would NOT reduce the handler count.** All 16 `handleXxx` bodies still have to live somewhere: either they move WITH the switch into the new router file (a rename of `SetupController` to `SetupCommandRouter`, not a reduction), or they stay behind as `private` methods the router cannot call without those methods becoming `public` — WIDENING this class's public surface, the opposite of an encapsulation improvement. **It would NOT improve cohesion** — today a maintainer changing a `setup.*` method finds the routing arm AND the implementation in the same file (scan a 51-line switch, then read the method); splitting them adds a mandatory cross-file jump for every future edit. **It WOULD add exactly one indirection layer** with no offsetting gain — the case OD-L's own "Cost of the other choice" column already named: "pure responsibility-count reduction, no behaviour value."

**Decision — CLOSE BY EVIDENCE (per OD-L's ruling); honesty test (a)/(b)/(c) all hold:**
(a) **No `handle*` is a god-method of tangled inline logic.** The largest, `handleInstall`, is 77 code lines with 12 delegation call sites across 3 collaborators (≈1 delegation per 6.4 code lines) in a flat guard → confirm → delegate ×3 → bookkeeping pipeline, with no nesting beyond the two awaited calls' own `try/catch`. `status()` (74 code lines) is pure composition of ~16 already-delegated field values via 7 imported `compose*`/`compute*` functions. `handleSetTunable` (11 code lines) delegates its entire validation switch to one module-level pure function. None of the 18 measured methods re-implements another handler's, or a collaborator's, logic inline.
(b) **The heavy responsibilities already live in extracted, cleanly-seamed collaborators** — `ProvisionRunner` (865 L: the entire provision/pull family + F8-4's fail-closed guard), `statusBlocks.ts` (297 L: every status-composition function, including F8-5's `composeDedicated`), `SettledProbeMemo` (93 L: both probe state machines), `LatchRegistry` (51 L), `modalText.ts` (63 L), `ggufIngestSpec.ts` (34 L: F8-3's ISP seam) — 1403 L of setup-specific logic living outside this file, each reached through a narrow, named seam (`this.provision`, `this.latches`, the memo fields, imported `compose*`), none re-duplicated inside `SetupController.ts`.
(c) **A `SetupCommandRouter` would relocate the dispatch switch without reducing the handler count or improving cohesion** — see evidence item 5: the outer per-domain router already exists one layer up (`TalariaViewProvider.handleSetupMethod`); the inner switch is 51 code lines of zero-logic name→method jumps sitting directly beside the methods it names; extracting it either renames the class or widens its private surface, and either way adds one indirection layer for no offsetting gain.

FI-41 closes by-evidence: the residual breadth (17 dispatch-reachable methods in one file) is an accepted, DOCUMENTED trade-off — a cohesive façade whose handlers already delegate their weight to extracted collaborators — not an undecomposed god-class. `SetupCommandRouter` stays a deferred, OPTIONAL future refactor: it remains available if the `handle*` count keeps growing (the finding's own "revisit only if `handle*` grows" trigger, per OD-L's context column) to the point where the SWITCH itself, not the handlers, becomes hard to scan — that threshold is not reached at 17 arms / 51 code lines.

## ADR-025-AO — `dispatchOptional` factors the optional-backend-member dispatch idiom (task F7-1, WS-F7 kickoff, FI-24)

**Outcome (task F7-1, WS-F7, FI-24, ADR-FSU-09):** re-grounded by symbol on `1eae3f4` (F8-7-closed HEAD, the live tip at kickoff) — line numbers matched the brief's estimates exactly, no drift. FI-24 named the "optional backend member → dispatch-else-log" idiom (`if (this.backend.X) { … } else { logger.appendLine('… ignored — backend has no X') }`) as copy-pasted; the finding's own text said "×6" but that count conflated two DIFFERENT patterns. Re-grounding found exactly **4** true instances of the optional-guard idiom — `policy.setPreset` (`:713-719`), `mode.set` (`:727-733`), `tab.load` (`:776-788`), `tab.newSession` (`:790-805`) — plus 2 sites the finding swept in that are NOT the idiom at all: `tab.open` (`:743`) and `switchPanel` (`:812`) are fire-and-forget `.catch`-only calls on `openTab`/`invokeControl`, both NON-OPTIONAL members of the `Backend` interface (always present — no `if (this.backend.X)` guard exists to fold, no else-log to unify). Folding those two would have wrongly imposed an optional-presence check on a method the type system already guarantees exists. Both are left byte-for-byte untouched — confirmed by `git diff`, neither line appears in the diff.

**The new helper** — a private `dispatchOptional` on `TalariaViewProvider`, placed directly above `handleWebviewMessage` (under the existing `// --- webview → backend routing ---` section marker, since all 4 call sites live inside that one switch):
```ts
private dispatchOptional(
  supported: boolean,
  invoke: () => void | Promise<unknown>,
  onMissing: string,
  onFail?: (err: unknown) => string,
): void {
  if (!supported) {
    this.logger?.appendLine(onMissing);
    return;
  }
  const result = invoke();
  if (result instanceof Promise && onFail !== undefined) {
    void result.catch((err) => this.logger?.appendLine(onFail(err)));
  }
}
```
`supported` is evaluated by the CALLER (`this.backend.setPreset !== undefined`, etc.) rather than re-derived inside the helper, so `dispatchOptional` stays generic over any optional member without needing to know its name. The `invoke` thunk always reaches the backend member through OPTIONAL-CALL (`this.backend.setPreset?.(...)`, never a bare `this.backend.setPreset!(...)`) — this is deliberate: even though `supported` being `true` guarantees the member is present at the call, `?.` lets `tsc` narrow the call site itself without a non-null assertion, so the helper's contract needs no unsafe cast on either side. For the two async sites, `invoke`'s return type is `void | Promise<unknown>` because an optional-call on a method returning `Promise<void>` widens to `Promise<void> | undefined`, and TypeScript accepts that under the declared `void | Promise<unknown>` return position without any cast; `result instanceof Promise` then narrows for the `.catch` wiring, discriminating the two sync sites (where `invoke()` returns `undefined`, doing nothing) from the two async sites (where it returns a real `Promise`).

**The 4 rewritten call sites**, each now three-or-four arguments to `dispatchOptional`, with the pre-existing rationale comments preserved (condensed to add a one-line `dispatchOptional`/FI-24 pointer, not rewritten):
- `policy.setPreset` (SYNC, no `onFail`): `this.dispatchOptional(this.backend.setPreset !== undefined, () => this.backend.setPreset?.(message.sessionId, message.preset), \`[policy] setPreset '${message.preset}' ignored — backend has no policy engine\`);`
- `mode.set` (SYNC, no `onFail`): `this.dispatchOptional(this.backend.setCustomMode !== undefined, () => this.backend.setCustomMode?.(message.sessionId, message.modeId), \`[policy] mode.set '${String(message.modeId)}' ignored — backend has no custom-mode engine\`);`
- `tab.load` (ASYNC, `onFail` given): the pre-existing conditional-ARRAY spread building loadTab's optional 4th (title) argument — `...(message.title !== undefined ? ([message.title] as const) : ([] as const))` — moved VERBATIM into the `invoke` thunk, unchanged in any way (not a new `as`-cast, not the banned `{ ...x }` object-spread; it is the same array-spread + `as const` pair that pre-existed this task, confirmed by `git diff` showing that exact line as a context line, never an added `+` line).
- `tab.newSession` (ASYNC, `onFail` given): `this.dispatchOptional(this.backend.newSessionInTab !== undefined, () => this.backend.newSessionInTab?.(message.tabId, message.sessionId), \`[tab.newSession] ${message.tabId} ignored — backend has no newSessionInTab support\`, (err) => \`[tab.newSession] ${message.tabId} failed: ${String(err)}\`);`

**Message byte-identity (review focus (a)).** All 4 `onMissing` strings and both `onFail` closures were diffed character-for-character against the pre-task inline strings — identical, including the `String(message.modeId)` and `String(err)` interpolations. A `git diff` of the production file shows the four template-literal strings appear only as reflowed/reindented `+` lines carrying the SAME characters; no string content changed.

**Sync/async preserved (review focus (b)).** `policy.setPreset`/`mode.set` still call `dispatchOptional` with no `onFail`, so `result instanceof Promise` is never even consulted for a truthy branch when the sync backends' `void`-returning methods run — no accidental Promise wrapping. `tab.load`/`tab.newSession` keep their `.catch` wired exactly as before (defense-in-depth only, per each site's preserved comment — the backend itself never rejects in normal flow).

**Non-null assertion (review focus (d)).** Zero new `!` introduced — confirmed by a targeted diff-scan for a word-character immediately followed by `!` across every added line: no matches. The `?.`-invoke on each optional member plus the boolean `supported` gate together do all the narrowing `dispatchOptional`'s contract needs.

**`TalariaViewProvider.test.ts` 0-EDIT + green (review focus (e)).** `git diff --stat` shows only `src/host/TalariaViewProvider.ts` touched — the test file's diff is empty. `npx vitest run src/host/` (131 files, the full host suite, this file included): **3270 passed | 13 skipped (3283), tsc 0** — identical pass count to a pre-task run of the same command. The four dispatch-pinning suites (`W2-F1 edit-policy preset routing`, `SF-2 (T4b): mode.set routing`, `W4-T5b: tab.load routing`, `W3-T6 (CF-11/D2): tab.newSession routing`) all stayed green with zero edits, including the two tests that assert the EXACT log string byte-for-byte (`'ignores tab.load when the backend has no loadTab support'`'s no-throw check and, more strongly, the `IMP-3` test's `expect(logger.appendLine).toHaveBeenCalledWith('[tab.newSession] tab-2 failed: Error: boom')`) — the latter is a precise byte-identity pin on the `.catch` handler's log line, not merely a no-throw smoke test.

**Mutation (teeth, review focus per the brief's step 4).** Changed `tab.newSession`'s `onFail` closure from `` `[tab.newSession] ${message.tabId} failed: ${String(err)}` `` to `` `[tab.newSession] ${message.tabId} MUTATED: ${String(err)}` ``, reran the `IMP-3` test alone: **FAILED** — `AssertionError: expected "vi.fn()" to be called with arguments: [ Array(1) ]`, diff showing `-  "[tab.newSession] tab-2 failed: Error: boom"` / `+  "[tab.newSession] tab-2 MUTATED: Error: boom"`. This proves the test pins the exact `onFail` string, not just "some" log call. Restored byte-identical; reran `TalariaViewProvider.test.ts` alone: 108/108 passed.

**Count discipline:** baseline (`1eae3f4`, F8-7-closed HEAD) full `npm run gate` = **350 test files passed, 7657 passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview)** — matching `ADR-025-AN`'s own recorded baseline exactly (this task is docs-only from that HEAD, so no drift expected between the two readings). Full-gate run after this task: **350 test files passed (350), 7657 passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview), no type errors.** Delta vs baseline: **0 test files, 0 tests** — the pure-refactor expectation (no test added, none removed, none newly failing). The known `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` Windows-teardown flake did NOT appear on this run.

**Byte-scan (2 touched files):** `TalariaViewProvider.ts` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0. `ADR-025-lens-r2-defer-closeout.md` (this file) — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**`git diff --stat`:** `src/host/TalariaViewProvider.ts` (57 insertions, 38 deletions) + `docs_claude/adr/ADR-025-lens-r2-defer-closeout.md` (this entry) — no test file, no other production file, touched.

**★ FI-24 closed.** `dispatchOptional` single-sources the optional-backend-member guard-else-log idiom (plus the async `.catch`-with-`onFail` wiring) for its 4 true instances; `tab.open`/`switchPanel` — the finding's over-counted "×6" — are confirmed non-optional fire-and-forget calls on always-present `Backend` members and correctly left out of scope, untouched. `TalariaViewProvider.test.ts` stayed 0-EDIT and green throughout, and the `tab.newSession` `onFail`-string mutation flipped its dedicated byte-identity test exactly as predicted, proving the suite pins the extracted helper's output, not merely its shape. This is the FIRST task of WS-F7 (the non-`[CONC]` fix, per the brief's own sequencing note).

**The strongest counter-argument, acknowledged.** 2470 total lines and 17 dispatch-reachable methods IS, on its face, a lot of surface for one class, and the single largest concrete number that could read as "god-method" evidence is `handleInstall`'s 105 TOTAL lines (comments included) — nearly double most of its siblings. Stripped to CODE lines, that method is 77 — comparable to `status()`'s 74, and well under half of `handleRecheck`'s TOTAL of 86 (itself only 35 code lines; the rest is 12 paragraphs of `T5`/`T7`/`R2-1`/`CA-M18` rationale comments explaining WHY each `scope` branch does what it does, per this codebase's own commenting convention — evident in the comment columns of the table in item 2). `handleInstall`'s 12-delegation/77-code-line ratio, and its complete absence of loops, recursion, or duplicated collaborator logic, are why it does not read as a god-method under inspection — its size comes from touching 3 collaborators across a 5-step user-facing flow (confirm → locate → install → persist → notify) with per-step fail-closed error handling, not from doing 3 collaborators' work itself. If a FUTURE change added another step, or started re-implementing e.g. `installHermes`'s own retry logic inline instead of delegating to it, that would be the signal to reopen this finding — it is not present today.

**Count discipline (docs-only, ZERO code/test delta expected):** baseline (`020ec8a`, F8-5-closed HEAD, per `ADR-025-AM`'s own after-count) = 350 test files passed, 7657 passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview). Full-gate run for THIS task, same HEAD, no code touched: **350 test files passed (350), 7657 tests passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview), no type errors** — identical to baseline, confirming zero production/test drift. The known `src/rag/indexer.test.ts` Windows-teardown flake did NOT appear in this run.

**Byte-scan (1 touched file):** `ADR-025-lens-r2-defer-closeout.md` — NUL: 0, U+2028: 0, U+2029: 0.

**`git diff --stat`:** shows only `docs_claude/adr/ADR-025-lens-r2-defer-closeout.md` — no production code, no test file, touched.

**★ FI-41 / OD-L closed (by-evidence, no code change).** `SetupController.ts`'s 2470 lines / 17 dispatch-reachable methods are a documented, evidence-backed trade-off: every heavy responsibility already lives in an extracted, cleanly-seamed collaborator (`ProvisionRunner`, `statusBlocks.ts`, `SettledProbeMemo`, `LatchRegistry`, `modalText.ts`, `ggufIngestSpec.ts`, the shared `MutationGate`), the largest handler is a 77-code-line orchestration pipeline with 12 delegation call sites and zero inline reimplementation, and the dispatch switch itself is a 51-code-line zero-logic jump table already sitting beside the methods it names — the property a `SetupCommandRouter` could not improve without either renaming the class or widening its private surface. `SetupCommandRouter` remains a deferred, optional future refactor, re-triggerable if the `handle*` count keeps growing past this point. This is the LAST task of WS-F8.

## ADR-025-AP — `isDisposed()` predicate replaces 6 `(acpState as string)` casts on the async-mutable ACP lifecycle field (task F7-2, WS-F7, FI-23, ADR-FSU-09, `[CONC]`)

**Outcome (task F7-2, WS-F7, FI-23, `[CONC]`):** re-grounded by symbol on `c515d3b` (F7-1-closed HEAD, the live tip at kickoff) — all 6 named cast sites and their line numbers matched the brief's `1eae3f4` estimates exactly (F7-1 did not touch `ConnectionSupervisor.ts`, so no drift). `acpState: 'idle' | 'starting' | 'ready' | 'respawning' | 'disposed'` (`:133`) is async-mutable: a re-entrant `AcpBackend.dispose()` can flip it to `'disposed'` DURING an `await` inside `startInternal`, and several guards deliberately re-read it after such an `await` to catch that race. But TS control-flow-analysis narrows the field to its last synchronous assignment (`this.acpState = 'starting'` at the old `:384`) across the surrounding `try`/`catch`, so a direct post-await `this.acpState === 'disposed'` compare is a TS2367 no-overlap error — the pre-task code widened it back with `(this.acpState as string)`, 6 times, each with its own "Cast: …" rationale comment.

**The fix.** A new private predicate, placed directly after the `acpState` field declaration (before `acpRespawnAttempts`):
```ts
private isDisposed(): boolean {
  return this.acpState === 'disposed';
}
```
Reading the field through a method-call RESULT (rather than the field itself) escapes CFA narrowing — the method's return type is a plain `boolean`, so the caller's `if (this.isDisposed())` has nothing for TS to have narrowed, and the comparison inside the method body reads the field's FULL five-member union fresh on every invocation. This is the same "escape narrowing via an indirection boundary" mechanism as `ControlChannel`'s own sibling guards, generalized to a named predicate instead of a cast.

**The 6 sites rewritten** (fresh `this.isDisposed()` call at the exact same check point as the old cast-read, no site reordered):
- `startInternal` pre-`resolveHermes`-await re-check (old `:395`) → `if (this.isDisposed()) throw new Error('AcpBackend: disposed');`
- `startInternal` post-connect-phase re-check (old `:438`) → same throw, same message, same point.
- `startInternal`'s catch, guarding the `acpState = 'idle'` reset (old `:454`) → `if (!this.isDisposed()) this.acpState = 'idle';` — confirmed NO `await` sits between this check and the write (the whole catch body is synchronous), so the check-then-write stays atomic exactly as before.
- `startInternal`'s catch, guarding the failure banner (old `:470`) → `if (!wasRespawning && !this.isDisposed()) { … }`.
- `reconnect()`'s catch, guarding the respawn re-arm (old `:1260`) → `if (!this.isDisposed()) { this.acpState = 'respawning'; this.scheduleAcpRespawn(); }`.
- `scheduleAcpRespawn`'s timer-callback catch (old `:1296`) → same guard, same body, same point.

`startInternal`'s pre-narrowing entry check (`:374`, which compiled without a cast even before this task) was ALSO converted to `this.isDisposed()` for consistency, per the brief's explicit low-risk allowance — the two `acpState !== 'respawning'` reads (old `:596`/`:656`, now `:611`/`:671`/`:1197` after the predicate's insertion shifted line numbers) were left untouched, since they test a different state and never carried a cast. Every `throw new Error('AcpBackend: disposed')` message and every guarded write's condition is byte-identical to the pre-task code — confirmed by `git diff`, which shows only the cast expression replaced by the predicate call on each touched line, plus three condensed rationale comments (the old "Cast: …" notes now point at `isDisposed()`'s own doc instead of repeating the CFA explanation three times).

**The `[CONC]` fresh-read invariant — proved by mutation, not asserted.** Per the brief's step 4, at the top of `startInternal` a temporary `const disposedAtStart = this.isDisposed();` was introduced and the two post-await checks (old `:395`/`:438`, now inside the `try`) were switched to read `disposedAtStart` instead of calling `this.isDisposed()` fresh — simulating the exact stale-cache mistake the predicate's own doc comment warns against. Re-running the `dispose() racing startInternal` suite (`ConnectionSupervisor.test.ts:819+`) with this mutation in place: **all 5 tests in that describe block FAILED** — `G2` (`dispose()` landing mid-connect-phase) no longer rejected `/disposed/i` at all (resolved instead), the `markDisposed()`-alone variant of `G2` likewise resolved, `G1` (dispose racing the `resolveHermes` await) also stopped rejecting, and the two respawn-caller/health-safety tests both lost their guard (a spurious `{state:'ok'}` health event fired, and `reconnect()` returned `{ok:true}` instead of refusing). This proves the fresh per-call read at those two check points is load-bearing, not incidental — a cached pre-await snapshot genuinely defeats the re-entrant-dispose detection the whole predicate exists for. Reverted to the fresh `this.isDisposed()` calls; re-ran the same suite: all 5 tests passed again, confirming the restore was byte-identical to the intended fix.

**Second tooth — the predicate is actually wired.** `isDisposed()`'s comparison was inverted (`!==` for `===`) and the full `ConnectionSupervisor.test.ts` file re-run: **28 of 32 tests failed** (4 passed — tests unrelated to any disposed-state branch), including an unhandled-rejection surfaced by `startInternal`'s own entry guard now firing on every non-disposed call. Restored `return this.acpState === 'disposed';` byte-identical; re-ran the full file: back to the pre-mutation baseline.

## ADR-025-AQ — `emitReplayFailure` + `unwindFailedLoadIdentity` single-source the two failure-unwind choreographies (task F7-3, WS-F7 — LAST task of WS-F7, FI-03, `[CONC]`, CHARACTERIZATION-FIRST, ADR-FSU-09)

**Outcome (task F7-3, WS-F7, FI-03, `[CONC]`, 2 commits):** re-grounded by symbol on `0f0441c` (F7-2-closed HEAD, the live tip at kickoff) — every branch named in the brief's `1eae3f4` estimates matched (F7-1/F7-2 never touched `AcpBackend.ts`/`SessionController.ts`, so no drift). Two DISTINCT, independently-committed choreographies, both the least-exercised/most safety-critical paths in the codebase (session load/replay failure unwind):

**Part A — `SessionController.loadReplayOutcome`'s replay-fail unwind (F7-3a, `f7e8059`).** Three exits shared a `setReplaying(false)` + `replay=undefined` + `error`/`turn.end` emit + `markSubagentsInterrupted()` cleanup pattern; branches 1 (catch → `load-failed`) and 2 (`!result.found` → `not-found`) differed only in the error message and return `kind`; branch 3 (success) shared only the `setReplaying(false); this.replay = undefined;` prefix (no error emit). New private `emitReplayFailure(replay, sessionId, message)` single-sources branches 1 & 2's shared tail; each call site keeps its own INLINE `if (this.replay !== replay) return { kind: 'superseded' };` supersede-recheck (UNCHANGED — the `[CONC]` guard reading `this.replay` fresh after the `client.loadSession` await, and the thing that determines the differing return) followed by `this.emitReplayFailure(replay, sessionId, <message>); return { kind: ... };`. Branch 1's `errorMessage(err)` call count is preserved at two (once into the helper, once into the return) — pure function, behavior-identical either way, kept to minimize the diff. Branch 3 (success) is completely UNCHANGED — it never calls `emitReplayFailure` and keeps its own inline two-line prefix, exactly per the brief's "do NOT force it through the helper."

**Part B — `AcpBackend.loadSessionIntoTabInternal`'s identity-unwind (F7-3b, next commit).** Three failure exits unwind the identity a failed load adopted pre-load (`priorCwd` + `activeSessionId`): the TI-5 no-client short-circuit and the settleRace wall-clock timeout (both sit BEFORE the switch, session still registered → `close` then a nested `activeSessionId` reset), and the switch's fail-kinds arm (`no-client`/`load-failed`/`not-found` — `loadReplayOutcome` already emitted its own signal → NO close, flat guard). New private `unwindFailedLoadIdentity(sessionId, controller, priorCwd, close)`:
```ts
private unwindFailedLoadIdentity(
  sessionId: string,
  controller: SessionController,
  priorCwd: string | undefined,
  close: boolean,
): void {
  if (this.sessions.get(sessionId) !== controller) return;
  if (close) this.sessions.close(sessionId);
  if (this.activeSessionId === sessionId) {
    this.activeSessionId = undefined;
    this.cwd = priorCwd;
  }
}
```
`priorCwd`'s ground-truthed type is `string | undefined` (the brief's sketch said `string`; `AcpBackend.cwd` is declared `string | undefined`, and `priorCwd = this.cwd` inherits that — widened to match, no cast). For `close:true` this is exactly branches 1&2's old shape (registry-leg evaluated BEFORE `close()` deletes the map entry, `activeSessionId` leg nested inside it — the ORDER the brief demanded be preserved). For `close:false` it collapses to `if (match && activeSessionId===sessionId) reset` — branch 3's old flat guard, confirmed algebraically identical (an outer `if(match){ if(cond){X} }` with no other statement in the outer block *is* `if(match && cond){X}`). All 3 call sites now read `this.unwindFailedLoadIdentity(sessionId, controller, priorCwd, true|false)` followed by each exit's own inline `tab.error` emit (branches 1&2) or bare `return undefined` (branch 3, no emit — unchanged). The recurring "`activeSessionId` is never restored to the prior occupant (T24 Case A/B)" rationale, identical at all 3 old call sites, was condensed onto the helper's own doc comment instead of repeated three times.

**Characterization-first — the gaps the existing suites left open, closed BEFORE either extraction (both PASS on the pre-extraction `0f0441c` code, confirmed by isolated `vitest run -t` before touching production code).** The existing WS-R4 six-arm suite (`SessionController.test.ts`) and the F3-7/F3-7-S suites (`AcpBackend.test.ts`) already pinned close-or-not/reset-or-not/emit-or-not/return-kind for every branch via ACTIVE-tab vs NON-active-tab pairs, but left two classes of behavior unpinned:
- **Part A:** the supersede-recheck's superseding short-circuit was pinned for the `!result.found` branch (`resolveLoad(0, {found:false})` after a same-instance supersede) but NEVER for the catch/`load-failed` branch's OWN identical inline recheck. Added `ARM superseded-mid-await via REJECT (catch branch)` (drives the SAME supersede scenario through `client.rejectLoad` instead of `resolveLoad`). Also added two `endForRestart()`-probe tests (`load-failed clears this.replay` / `not-found clears this.replay`) — `emitReplayFailure`'s `this.replay = undefined` postcondition is invisible in the returned union or the failing call's own emissions, so each probes it by calling `endForRestart()` on the SAME controller afterward and asserting no SECOND, stale `turn.end` fires (which `endTurnBracket`'s `else if (this.replay !== undefined)` arm would emit if the reset were dropped).
- **Part B:** no test constructed the "registry-match is FALSE" (superseding-controller) case for `loadSessionIntoTabInternal`'s own exits, nor asserted that the switch's fail-kinds arm has NO `sessions.close` call at all (as opposed to merely "the activeSessionId guard misses"). Added `switch-fail path (branch 3, not-found) never closes the controller` (asserts `hasController` stays `true` after a fast `found:false` reaching the switch) and `a superseding controller for the SAME sessionId is NEVER closed by a stale settleRace-timeout unwind` — the latter hangs `client.loadSession`, lets `loadSessionIntoTabInternal` mint its controller and start the settleRace deadline, then directly calls `backend.sessions.open('history-session', ...)` (the same test-only registry-reflection idiom already established at `mintOnCwdForTab`, `hasController`, etc.) to mint a FRESH second controller under the SAME sessionId — mirroring exactly what `SessionRegistry.open`'s own W6-FB collision guard does for a real racing caller — before advancing the fake-timer deadline. This makes the STALE load's local `controller` reference genuinely dangle against the registry, the one scenario that actually exercises the registry-match guard's reason for existing.

**5 characterization tests added** (3 in `SessionController.test.ts`, 2 in `AcpBackend.test.ts`) — all 0-EDIT through both extractions (`git diff` on both test files after the production rewrites shows ONLY the pre-extraction additions, nothing touched again).

**The `[CONC]`/ORDER invariants — proved by mutation, not asserted (review focus (a)/(c)).**
- *Part A, `status` flip* (`'error'` → `'complete'` inside `emitReplayFailure`): re-ran `SessionController.test.ts -t "ARM load-failed|ARM not-found"` — **both tests FAILED** (the `turn.end{status:'error'}` `objectContaining` no longer matched; received `status:'complete'`). Restored; re-ran: both passed.
- *Part A, dropped `this.replay = undefined`*: re-ran the two new `endForRestart()`-probe tests — **both FAILED** (`turn.end` count went from 1 to 2 — the stale replay bracket fired a second, spurious `turn.end`). Restored; re-ran: both passed.
- *Part B, ORDER tooth* (moved `if (close) this.sessions.close(sessionId);` BEFORE the registry-match guard, i.e. close unconditionally): re-ran the superseding-controller test — **FAILED** (`registry.sessions.get('history-session')` returned `undefined` instead of the fresh controller — the unconditional close disposed the CORRECT, currently-registered controller by sessionId, exactly the bug class the guard exists to prevent). Restored; re-ran: passed.
- *Part B, close-flag tooth* (flipped branch 3's call site to `close:true`): re-ran the switch-fail-never-closes test — **FAILED** (`hasController('session-ghost')` came back `false` instead of `true`). Restored; re-ran: passed.

All 4 mutations were introduced, confirmed to flip a characterization test, then reverted byte-identical (confirmed by re-running the full affected suite green afterward).

**Deliberate per-branch differences survive (review focus (b)).** Part A branch 3 (success) never calls `emitReplayFailure` — no error emitted on success, confirmed unchanged by `git diff` (zero lines touched in that arm). Part B branch 3 (switch) calls the helper with `close:false` and, per its own code, never fires a `tab.error` — `loadReplayOutcome`'s own arms already emitted the session-scoped signal; branches 1&2 keep their own distinct `reason`/`message` (`'disconnected'`/`'timeout'`) emitted inline, AFTER the helper call, unchanged.

**Both helpers are synchronous (review focus (c)) — no `await` introduced.** `emitReplayFailure`'s body is 5 synchronous statements; `unwindFailedLoadIdentity`'s body is a guard + 2 synchronous statements. Confirmed by inspection and by the mutation-recovery runs above never needing a re-added `await`.

**Emit shapes byte-identical (review focus (e)).** `error`: `{type, sessionId, message, turnId: replay.currentTurnId}`. `turn.end`: `{type, turnId: replay.currentTurnId, sessionId, status: 'error'}`. `tab.error` (Part B, inline at each call site, unchanged): `{type, tabId, kind: 'session-lost', reason, message}`. Field order and values confirmed identical to the pre-extraction source by `git diff` (each emit block deleted verbatim, nothing re-typed by hand except the two call-site one-liners).

**No new `as`-cast, no `any`, no new `!`, no spread (review focus (f)).** A line-by-line scan of every `+` line across both production diffs and both test diffs found zero `any`, zero new non-null-assertion `!`, zero object/array spread, and zero new `as`-cast in PRODUCTION code. The one `as unknown as {...}` in the new Part B registry-reflection test follows the SAME established test-only private-member-reflection idiom already used 68 times elsewhere in `AcpBackend.test.ts` (e.g. `mintOnCwdForTab`, `hasController`, `connectionSupervisorOf`) — not a new pattern, no unsafe narrowing of production logic.

**0-EDIT through both moves + suites green.** `git diff --stat` on both test files shows only the characterization ADDITIONS from the pre-extraction step — zero lines touched inside any pre-existing test after either production rewrite. `SessionController.test.ts`: 78/78 passed (was 75/75 pre-task; +3 characterization). `AcpBackend.test.ts`: 491/491 passed (was 489/489 pre-task; +2 characterization). Full `npx vitest run src/host/backend/`: 42 files, 1378 passed | 6 skipped (1384), tsc 0 — both before and after Part B's production edit.

**Count discipline.** Baseline (`0f0441c`, F7-2-closed HEAD) full `npm run gate`: **350 test files, 7657 passed | 15 skipped (7672), tsc 0 (host + webview)**. After the characterization-first step (5 new tests, still pre-extraction): **350 files, 7662 passed | 15 skipped (7677), tsc 0** — full gate run and confirmed GREEN before F7-3a's commit. After F7-3a (Part A production rewrite): unchanged, **7662|15 (7677), tsc 0** — full gate re-run and confirmed GREEN before F7-3b's commit. After F7-3b (Part B production rewrite, this entry's final state): **350 files, 7662 passed | 15 skipped (7677), tsc 0** — identical count to the characterization-first checkpoint, the expected signature of a pure, behavior-preserving extraction. Net delta vs the F7-2 baseline: **+5 tests, 0 files** (the 5 characterization additions; zero regressions, zero incidental test changes).
